

# SECTION: Cardiovascular System (Count: 140 Questions)


### Question 1
A nurse is reviewing a resting adult client's EKG tracing on the telemetry monitor. Which component of the EKG waveform should the nurse interpret as representing normal ventricular depolarization?

A) P wave  
B) QRS complex  
C) T wave  
D) PR interval  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** B) QRS complex

**Lens:** Data Interpretation
**Rationale:** In a normal EKG, the QRS complex represents ventricular depolarization, which triggers the contraction of the ventricles. The P wave represents atrial depolarization. The T wave represents ventricular repolarization (the resting phase). The PR interval represents the time it takes for the electrical impulse to travel from the SA node through the AV node to the ventricles.
</details>

---

### Question 2
A client newly diagnosed with primary hypertension is prescribed a daily oral antihypertensive medication. Which statement by the client indicates that the nurse's teaching regarding safety has been effective?

A) "I can stop the medication once my blood pressure reading reaches 120/80 mmHg."  
B) "I will double my morning dose if my blood pressure is high when I wake up."  
C) "I will rise slowly when standing up from a sitting or lying position."  
D) "I do not need to restrict my salt intake since the medication will control my blood pressure."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** C) "I will rise slowly when standing up from a sitting or lying position."

**Lens:** Client Teaching
**Rationale:** Antihypertensive medications can cause orthostatic hypotension, which increases the risk of dizziness and falls. Changing positions slowly is a crucial safety measure. Hypertension medications should not be stopped abruptly even if blood pressure normalizes, as this can cause rebound hypertension. Doubling doses without a provider's order is unsafe and can lead to severe hypotension. Dietary modifications, such as limiting sodium, are still necessary even when taking medication.
</details>

### Question 3
The nurse is assessing a client diagnosed with right-sided heart failure. Which clinical manifestation is an expected finding for this specific condition?

A) Pitting edema in the lower extremities  
B) Frequent episodes of orthopnea  
C) Crackles heard upon auscultation of the lungs  
D) A persistent, dry hacking cough  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** A) Pitting edema in the lower extremities

**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**Rationale:** Right-sided heart failure causes venous congestion in the systemic circulation, leading to expected expected signs such as peripheral pitting edema, jugular venous distention (JVD), and hepatomegaly. Orthopnea, lung crackles, and a persistent cough are classic manifestations of left-sided heart failure due to pulmonary congestion.
</details>

---

### Question 4
A nurse is caring for a stable client who was admitted with hypertension. Which task is most appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client for signs of orthostatic hypotension during ambulation  
B) Teaching the client about the importance of a low-sodium diet  
C) Evaluating the effectiveness of the client's morning antihypertensive medication  
D) Obtaining and recording the client's routine morning blood pressure  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** D) Obtaining and recording the client's routine morning blood pressure

**Lens:** Delegation
**Rationale:** Obtaining routine vital signs on a stable client is within the scope of practice for the UAP. Assessing a client, providing dietary education, and evaluating the effectiveness of a medication all require nursing judgment and fall exclusively within the scope of practice of the Registered Nurse (RN). 
</details>

### Question 5
A nurse is preparing to perform a standard 12-lead EKG on a client. Which procedural action should the nurse take to ensure a clear and accurate reading?

A) Instruct the client to take deep breaths during the recording.  
B) Ensure the client's chest skin is clean, dry, and free of excess hair.  
C) Place the V1 electrode in the second intercostal space on the right side.  
D) Apply moisturizing lotion to the chest to help the electrodes adhere securely.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** B) Ensure the client's chest skin is clean, dry, and free of excess hair.

**Lens:** Procedural Step
**Rationale:** To obtain a clear EKG tracing without artifact, the skin must be clean and dry, and excessive chest hair should be clipped so the electrodes adhere properly to the skin. Deep breathing or talking can cause movement artifact; the client should breathe normally and remain still. The V1 electrode is placed in the fourth (not second) intercostal space at the right sternal border. Lotion creates a barrier that prevents proper adhesion and electrical conduction, resulting in a poor EKG signal.
</details>
### Question 6
*Lens: Data Interpretation*

A nurse is analyzing a normal sinus rhythm on an electrocardiogram (EKG) for a healthy adult client during a routine physical. Which statement correctly interprets a component of this cardiac tracing?

A) The QRS complex indicates ventricular repolarization.  
B) The T wave signifies atrial contraction.  
C) The P wave represents atrial depolarization.  
D) The PR interval measures the client's overall heart rate.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
On a normal EKG tracing, the P wave represents atrial depolarization, which leads to atrial contraction. Option A is incorrect because the QRS complex represents ventricular depolarization (contraction), while the T wave represents ventricular repolarization (relaxation). Option B is incorrect because the T wave signifies ventricular repolarization. Option D is incorrect because the PR interval measures the time it takes for the electrical impulse to travel from the SA node to the AV node, not the overall heart rate.
</details>

---

### Question 7
*Lens: Expected vs Unexpected*

A nurse is collecting data on a client with a history of left-sided heart failure. Which assessment finding is an expected clinical manifestation specific to this condition?

A) Ascites in the abdomen  
B) Severe pitting edema in the lower extremities  
C) Distention of the jugular veins  
D) Shortness of breath when lying flat  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Left-sided heart failure primarily affects the pulmonary system because the left ventricle cannot efficiently pump blood out to the body, causing fluid to back up into the lungs. This leads to expected pulmonary symptoms such as orthopnea (shortness of breath when lying flat), dyspnea, and crackles. Options A, B, and C are classic clinical manifestations of right-sided heart failure, where fluid backs up into the systemic venous circulation.
</details>

---

### Question 8
*Lens: Client Teaching*

A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client who is newly prescribed an oral antihypertensive medication. Which instruction should the nurse include to promote client safety?

A) "Stop taking the medication immediately if you experience a mild headache."  
B) "Change positions slowly when moving from a sitting or lying position to a standing position."  
C) "Double your next dose if you miss a pill to ensure your blood pressure stays low."  
D) "Limit your daily fluid intake to 1 liter to prevent the medication from causing swelling."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Blood pressure medications can cause orthostatic hypotension, which is a sudden drop in blood pressure when changing positions. Teaching the client to rise slowly prevents dizziness and minimizes the risk of falls. Option A is incorrect because abruptly stopping antihypertensives can cause dangerous rebound hypertension. Option C is incorrect as clients should never double a dose of medication. Option D is incorrect because fluid restrictions are not universally required for all antihypertensives and require specific provider orders.
</details>

---

### Question 9
*Lens: Delegation*

A nurse is managing the care of a client admitted with an exacerbation of heart failure. Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A) Auscultating the client's lung sounds for new crackles.  
B) Instructing the client on the importance of a low-sodium diet.  
C) Emptying the urinal and recording the client's strict fluid output.  
D) Assessing the depth of pitting edema in the client's ankles.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Delegating the task of emptying a urinal and documenting intake and output (I&O) is entirely within the scope of practice for a UAP. Options A and D require nursing assessment and clinical judgment, which cannot be delegated to a UAP. Option B involves primary client education, which is also strictly the responsibility of the registered nurse. 
</details>

### Question 10
*Lens: Therapeutic Communication*

A client diagnosed with hypertension tells the clinic nurse, "I stopped taking my blood pressure medicine because it makes me feel dizzy and tired all the time." Which response by the nurse is the most appropriate?

A) "Tell me more about the dizziness and tiredness you've been experiencing."  
B) "You must keep taking your medication, or you could have a stroke."  
C) "Why would you stop taking your prescribed medication without asking the doctor?"  
D) "I will leave a note for the provider to change your prescription right away."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Therapeutic communication encourages the client to openly share their feelings and symptoms. By asking an open-ended question ("Tell me more..."), the nurse explores the client's experience without judgment, which helps determine the exact cause of non-adherence. Option B is non-therapeutic because it uses fear and is authoritative. Option C uses the word "Why," which sounds accusatory and can make the client defensive. Option D is premature; the nurse needs to assess the client's symptoms before assuming a medication change is the only solution.
</details>
### Question 11
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
A nurse is reviewing a normal electrocardiogram (EKG) tracing for a healthy adult client during a routine physical exam. The nurse identifies the first upward waveform on the tracing, which represents atrial depolarization. 

Which component of the EKG tracing is the nurse observing?
A) T wave
B) QRS complex
C) P wave
D) U wave

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
On a normal EKG tracing, the P wave is the first waveform and represents atrial depolarization (contraction of the atria). The QRS complex represents ventricular depolarization, the T wave represents ventricular repolarization, and the U wave (if present) represents the repolarization of the Purkinje fibers. 
</details>

### Question 12
**Lens:** Client Teaching
A client with primary hypertension is prescribed a daily oral antihypertensive medication. The nurse is providing discharge instructions to promote safety at home. 

Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?
A) "I should sit on the edge of the bed for a few minutes before standing up."
B) "I will take my blood pressure only when I feel dizzy or lightheaded."
C) "I should stop taking the medication once my blood pressure is normal."
D) "I will double my medication dose if I forget to take it the day before."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Antihypertensive medications often cause orthostatic hypotension, which increases the risk of falls. Sitting on the edge of the bed before standing allows the cardiovascular system to adjust to positional changes, promoting client safety. Medication should not be stopped abruptly, doses should never be doubled without a provider's order, and blood pressure should be checked regularly, not just when symptoms occur.
</details>

---

### Question 13
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
A nurse is assessing a client who has an established diagnosis of right-sided heart failure. The nurse conducts a focused physical assessment. 

Which finding should the nurse expect to observe in this client?
A) Blood-tinged sputum
B) Peripheral edema
C) Crackles in the lungs
D) Dry, hacking cough

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Right-sided heart failure causes blood to back up into the systemic venous system, leading to expected findings such as peripheral edema (swelling of the legs and ankles), jugular vein distention (JVD), and hepatomegaly. Crackles, a dry cough, and blood-tinged sputum are signs of pulmonary congestion, which are expected findings in *left-sided* heart failure.
</details>

### Question 14
**Lens:** Delegation
A nurse is caring for a stable client admitted with primary hypertension. The client is scheduled to have their vital signs checked before breakfast. 

Which task is most appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?
A) Provide education on a low-sodium dietary plan.
B) Assess the client's adherence to their home medication regimen.
C) Evaluate the effectiveness of the morning blood pressure pill.
D) Obtain and record the client's morning blood pressure.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Obtaining and recording routine vital signs (including blood pressure) on a stable client is a standard, repetitive task that falls within the scope of practice for a UAP. Assessing a client, evaluating the effectiveness of a medication, and providing education are all functions of the nursing process that require a nursing license and cannot be delegated.
</details>

### Question 15
**Lens:** Evaluation
A client with mild left-sided heart failure has been receiving a daily oral loop diuretic to manage fluid retention. The nurse is reviewing the client's chart to determine the effectiveness of the medication. 

Which finding best indicates to the nurse that the diuretic treatment is effective?
A) The client's daily weight decreases by 2 pounds (0.9 kg).
B) The client's resting heart rate increases from 80 to 95 beats per minute.
C) The client reports an increase in nighttime urination.
D) The client's blood pressure reading is 142/90 mmHg.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Diuretics are prescribed in heart failure to remove excess fluid from the body. A decrease in daily weight is the most objective and reliable indicator that fluid is being successfully excreted, demonstrating that the medication is effective. Increased heart rate or elevated blood pressure do not indicate effective diuretic therapy. While increased urination is a mechanism of the drug, weight loss confirms the actual reduction of fluid volume overload.
</details>
### Question 16
A nurse is reviewing an electrocardiogram (EKG) strip of a healthy young adult client during a routine physical. The nurse observes a normal sinus rhythm. Which specific EKG finding represents ventricular depolarization?

A) The P wave
B) The T wave
C) The PR interval
D) The QRS complex

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Data Interpretation<br>
In a normal EKG, the QRS complex represents ventricular depolarization, which triggers the contraction of the ventricles. The P wave represents atrial depolarization, the T wave represents ventricular repolarization, and the PR interval measures the time it takes for the electrical impulse to travel from the atria to the ventricles.
</details>

### Question 17
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly diagnosed with primary hypertension. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of safe lifestyle modifications?

A) "I will season my food with salt substitutes that contain potassium."
B) "I should aim to walk for at least 30 minutes most days of the week."
C) "I will limit my daily sodium intake to a maximum of 4,000 milligrams."
D) "I can stop taking my blood pressure pills as soon as my readings are normal."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
Regular aerobic exercise, such as walking for 30 minutes most days, is a safe and effective lifestyle modification for managing hypertension. Option A is unsafe because salt substitutes often contain high potassium, which can cause dangerous hyperkalemia if the client is taking certain blood pressure medications like ACE inhibitors. Option C is incorrect; sodium should generally be limited to 1,500-2,300 mg daily. Option D is incorrect; hypertension is a chronic condition, and medication should never be stopped abruptly without consulting the healthcare provider.
</details>

### Question 18
A nurse is assessing a client with a known history of right-sided heart failure. Which of the following findings should the nurse expect to observe during the assessment?

A) Peripheral edema in the lower extremities
B) Pink, frothy sputum
C) Crackles heard upon lung auscultation
D) Shortness of breath when lying flat

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected<br>
Right-sided heart failure primarily causes systemic congestion as the right ventricle fails to pump blood effectively into the lungs, causing fluid to back up into the body. This leads to expected findings such as peripheral edema (swelling in the legs and feet), jugular venous distention (JVD), and an enlarged liver. Options B, C, and D are classic expected signs of left-sided heart failure, which causes pulmonary (lung) congestion.
</details>

### Question 19
A nurse is preparing to perform a standard 12-lead electrocardiogram (EKG) on a resting client. Which action represents a correct procedural step when placing the chest electrodes?

A) Ask the client to hold their breath for 30 seconds while placing the leads.
B) Apply a thick layer of ultrasound gel to each electrode site before attachment.
C) Place the V1 electrode at the fourth intercostal space, right sternal border.
D) Shave the client's entire chest completely before applying any leads.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
The correct procedural step for placing the V1 electrode is at the fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border. Option A is incorrect; the client should breathe normally and remain still. Option B is incorrect; modern EKG electrodes are pre-gelled or have an adhesive conductive gel and do not require thick ultrasound gel. Option D is incorrect; hair is only clipped in specific, small areas if necessary for electrode adhesion, not routinely shaved over the entire chest.
</details>

### Question 20
The registered nurse (RN) is caring for a stable client who has a history of essential hypertension. Which task is most appropriate for the RN to safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Educating the client on the importance of adhering to a low-sodium diet.
B) Administering the client's scheduled daily oral antihypertensive medication.
C) Assessing the client's intravenous (IV) site for signs of infiltration.
D) Measuring the client's vital signs, including routine blood pressure.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation<br>
The UAP can safely perform routine tasks with predictable outcomes on stable clients, such as measuring vital signs and obtaining a routine blood pressure reading. Administering medication (Option B), assessing the IV site (Option C), and providing client education (Option A) require the nursing judgment, critical thinking, and specific scope of practice of a licensed nurse and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>
### Question 21
The nurse is providing discharge instructions to a client newly diagnosed with basic heart failure. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of daily weight monitoring?

A) "I should weigh myself right before I go to bed every night."
B) "I will only weigh myself if I feel like my legs are swelling."
C) "I will weigh myself every morning after urinating and before eating."
D) "I should wear heavy winter clothes to make sure my scale is accurate."

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer: C**

**Lens: Client Teaching**
**Rationale:** Daily weights are the most reliable basic indicator of fluid status for a client with heart failure. To ensure accurate and comparable results, the client should be taught to weigh themselves at the exact same time every day—ideally first thing in the morning after emptying their bladder, before eating breakfast, and while wearing similar, lightweight clothing. 
</details>

### Question 22
The nurse is reviewing a normal electrocardiogram (EKG) tracing for a healthy adult client during a routine physical. When interpreting the basic rhythm strip, what physiological event does the nurse know the P wave represents?

A) Ventricular repolarization
B) Atrial depolarization
C) Ventricular depolarization
D) Atrial repolarization

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer: B**

**Lens: Data Interpretation**
**Rationale:** On a normal EKG, the P wave represents atrial depolarization, which initiates the contraction of the atria. The QRS complex represents ventricular depolarization, and the T wave represents ventricular repolarization. Understanding these basic waveforms is essential for normal EKG interpretation.
</details>

### Question 23
A registered nurse (RN) is caring for a stable client admitted with essential hypertension. Which cardiovascular safety task is most appropriate for the RN to assign to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Obtaining the morning blood pressure reading using a manual cuff.
B) Providing education on a low-sodium diet for blood pressure control.
C) Administering the scheduled oral dose of a daily antihypertensive medication.
D) Assessing the client for signs of orthostatic dizziness upon standing.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer: A**

**Lens: Delegation**
**Rationale:** The UAP can safely be delegated the task of obtaining routine vital signs, including manual blood pressure measurements, on a stable client. Education (B), medication administration (C), and clinical assessment (D) strictly require nursing judgment and are solely within the scope of practice of licensed nurses.
</details>

### Question 24
The nurse is performing a cardiovascular assessment on a client with a known history of left-sided heart failure. Which clinical finding is an expected characteristic of this specific condition?

A) Pronounced jugular vein distention (JVD)
B) Dependent pitting edema in the lower extremities
C) Palpable hepatomegaly and abdominal ascites
D) Bilateral crackles auscultated at the lung bases

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer: D**

**Lens: Expected vs Unexpected**
**Rationale:** Left-sided heart failure primarily affects the pulmonary system as fluid backs up into the lungs. Expected findings for left-sided failure include bilateral lung crackles, dyspnea, and a hacking cough. Jugular vein distention (JVD), peripheral pitting edema, and hepatomegaly are expected findings associated with right-sided heart failure due to systemic venous congestion.
</details>

### Question 25
A client with newly diagnosed hypertension is started on an oral antihypertensive medication. To maintain client safety, the nurse identifies that this client is at a high risk for which of the following medication-related occurrences?

A) Sudden development of an irregular heartbeat
B) Orthostatic hypotension leading to a potential fall
C) Extreme spikes in blood pressure immediately after eating
D) Acute respiratory failure from sudden bronchodilation

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer: B**

**Lens: Risk Recognition**
**Rationale:** A common and significant safety risk when initiating antihypertensive medications is orthostatic (postural) hypotension. As the blood pressure lowers, clients are at a high risk for dizziness and falls when changing positions. The nurse must recognize this risk and instruct the client to change positions slowly, especially when moving from lying down to sitting or standing.
</details>
### Question 26
A nurse is educating a client newly diagnosed with primary hypertension about dietary modifications to safely manage their condition. Which statement by the client indicates a successful understanding of the teaching?

A. "I should add a pinch of salt to my meals to maintain my electrolytes."
B. "I will read nutrition labels to help me limit my daily sodium intake."
C. "I can eat as much canned soup as I want if I dilute it with water."
D. "I need to avoid eating any fresh fruits and vegetables."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

* **Correct Answer: B** (Lens: Client Teaching)
* **Rationale:** A key component of managing primary hypertension is reducing dietary sodium. Reading nutrition labels helps clients identify and avoid hidden sodium in processed foods. 
* **Incorrect Options:**
  * **A:** Adding salt to meals increases sodium intake, which exacerbates hypertension and is unsafe.
  * **C:** Canned soups are notoriously high in sodium. Diluting them with water does not reduce the total amount of sodium consumed.
  * **D:** Fresh fruits and vegetables are generally low in sodium and high in potassium, making them highly recommended for a heart-healthy diet.
</details>

### Question 27
The nurse is reviewing the electrocardiogram (EKG) rhythm strip of a resting adult client. The nurse observes a P wave preceding every QRS complex, a regular rhythm, and a heart rate of 75 beats per minute. How should the nurse interpret this data?

A. Sinus bradycardia
B. Atrial flutter
C. Ventricular fibrillation
D. Normal sinus rhythm

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

* **Correct Answer: D** (Lens: Data Interpretation)
* **Rationale:** A normal sinus rhythm is characterized by a heart rate between 60 and 100 beats per minute, a regular rhythm, and a P wave consistently preceding every QRS complex. The data provided perfectly matches these criteria.
* **Incorrect Options:**
  * **A:** Sinus bradycardia has the same characteristics as a normal sinus rhythm, but the heart rate is less than 60 beats per minute.
  * **B:** Atrial flutter is characterized by "sawtooth" shaped flutter waves rather than normal P waves.
  * **C:** Ventricular fibrillation is a chaotic, irregular rhythm with no identifiable P waves or QRS complexes, representing a life-threatening emergency.
</details>

### Question 28
A nurse is assessing a client with a history of right-sided heart failure. Which of the following findings should the nurse identify as an expected manifestation of this specific condition?

A. Peripheral pitting edema
B. Pink, frothy sputum
C. Crackles auscultated in the lungs
D. Severe shortness of breath at rest

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

* **Correct Answer: A** (Lens: Expected vs Unexpected)
* **Rationale:** Right-sided heart failure causes blood to back up into the systemic circulation. This results in expected systemic manifestations such as peripheral pitting edema, jugular venous distention (JVD), and ascites.
* **Incorrect Options:**
  * **B:** Pink, frothy sputum is an unexpected finding for isolated right-sided heart failure; it is a classic sign of pulmonary edema, which is associated with left-sided heart failure.
  * **C:** Crackles in the lungs are caused by fluid backing up into the pulmonary system, which is a sign of left-sided heart failure.
  * **D:** Severe shortness of breath at rest is heavily associated with pulmonary congestion caused by left-sided heart failure.
</details>

---

### Question 29
A client with a history of severe hypertension asks the nurse for an over-the-counter (OTC) medication to treat a stuffy nose. Which class of OTC medications is strictly contraindicated for this client due to the risk of a dangerous elevation in blood pressure?

A. Expectorants
B. Antihistamines
C. Oral decongestants
D. Cough suppressants

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

* **Correct Answer: C** (Lens: Contraindication)
* **Rationale:** Oral decongestants (such as pseudoephedrine or phenylephrine) cause systemic vasoconstriction to relieve nasal congestion. In a client with severe hypertension, this vasoconstriction can dangerously elevate blood pressure and is contraindicated.
* **Incorrect Options:**
  * **A:** Expectorants (like guaifenesin) help thin mucus and do not typically cause vasoconstriction or raise blood pressure.
  * **B:** Antihistamines block histamine receptors to relieve allergy symptoms and generally do not have a direct, significant hypertensive effect.
  * **D:** Cough suppressants (like dextromethorphan) work on the cough center in the brain and do not typically raise blood pressure.
</details>

### Question 30
The registered nurse (RN) is caring for a client with an exacerbation of heart failure. Which task can the RN safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Evaluating the effectiveness of the client's morning diuretic medication.
B. Assessing the client's lung sounds for new fluid buildup.
C. Obtaining the client's daily morning weight using the bed scale.
D. Teaching the client about the importance of restricting daily fluids.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

* **Correct Answer: C** (Lens: Delegation)
* **Rationale:** Obtaining a daily morning weight is a routine, standard, and non-invasive procedural task that falls safely within the scope of practice for a UAP. Daily weights are crucial for monitoring fluid retention in heart failure.
* **Incorrect Options:**
  * **A:** Evaluating the effectiveness of a medication requires nursing judgment and falls exclusively under the RN's scope of practice.
  * **B:** Assessing lung sounds is a clinical assessment skill that must be performed by the RN or LPN/PN, not a UAP.
  * **D:** Initial or primary client teaching regarding fluid restrictions is an RN responsibility and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>
### Question 31
The nurse is reviewing the normal electrocardiogram (EKG) of a client admitted for routine observation. The nurse identifies the P wave on the tracing. Based on data interpretation, what physiological event does the P wave represent?

A. Ventricular repolarization  
B. Ventricular depolarization  
C. Atrial depolarization  
D. Atrial repolarization  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
In a normal EKG tracing, the P wave represents atrial depolarization, which is the electrical signal that causes the atria to contract. The QRS complex represents ventricular depolarization, and the T wave represents ventricular repolarization. Atrial repolarization is typically hidden within the QRS complex.
</details>

### Question 32
A client with newly diagnosed hypertension is prescribed a daily oral antihypertensive medication. Which instruction is most important for the nurse to include in the client teaching to promote safety?

A. "Increase your daily intake of potassium-rich foods."  
B. "Change positions slowly from lying to sitting."  
C. "Take the medication with a full glass of grapefruit juice."  
D. "Stop taking the medication once your blood pressure is normal."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Antihypertensive medications can cause orthostatic (postural) hypotension, which significantly increases the risk of dizziness and falls. Teaching the client to change positions slowly is a crucial safety measure. Grapefruit juice can interact dangerously with some cardiac medications. Medications should never be stopped abruptly without a provider's order, and increasing potassium is specific only to certain types of diuretics, not all antihypertensives.
</details>

---

### Question 33
The nurse is assessing a client with a known history of left-sided heart failure. When distinguishing between expected versus unexpected findings, which clinical sign should the nurse expect to observe in this specific client?

A. Bilateral crackles in the lung bases  
B. Jugular venous distention (JVD)  
C. Marked dependent edema in the ankles  
D. Enlarged liver (hepatomegaly)  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Left-sided heart failure results in fluid backing up into the pulmonary circulation, causing pulmonary congestion. Expected findings include crackles in the lungs, shortness of breath, and a cough. Jugular venous distention, dependent edema, and hepatomegaly are classic expected findings of right-sided heart failure, where fluid backs up into the systemic circulation.
</details>

---

### Question 34
A registered nurse (RN) is caring for a client with heart failure who requires close monitoring of fluid status. Which task can the RN safely delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A. Assessing the client's lung sounds for crackles  
B. Educating the client on a low-sodium diet  
C. Evaluating the effectiveness of the morning diuretic  
D. Weighing the client before breakfast using the same scale  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Weighing a client is a standard, routine procedural task that does not require nursing judgment and falls completely within the scope of practice for a UAP. Assessing lung sounds, providing diet education, and evaluating the effectiveness of a medication all require clinical nursing judgment and must be performed by the RN.
</details>

---

### Question 35
The nurse is preparing to place continuous electrocardiogram (EKG) telemetry electrodes on a client's chest. What is the correct initial procedural step the nurse should take to ensure a clear signal?

A. Clean and dry the skin where the electrodes will be placed.  
B. Apply a thick layer of ultrasound gel to the client's skin.  
C. Place the electrodes directly over the client's hospital gown.  
D. Instruct the client to hold their breath while the leads are attached.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
To ensure a clear EKG tracing and proper adhesion, the skin must be clean, dry, and free of excess oils, moisture, or heavy hair before applying the adhesive electrodes. Telemetry electrodes are pre-gelled, so adding ultrasound gel is incorrect. Leads must be placed directly on the skin, not clothing, and the client should breathe normally during placement.
</details>
### Question 36
**Lens: Expected vs Unexpected**
A nurse is reviewing a normal sinus rhythm electrocardiogram (EKG) strip for a healthy adult client during a routine physical. Which measurement should the nurse recognize as an expected, normal finding?

A. A PR interval measuring 0.16 seconds.  
B. An inverted T wave in lead II.  
C. A QRS complex measuring 0.18 seconds.  
D. Absent P waves before the QRS complexes.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** A normal PR interval measures between 0.12 and 0.20 seconds, representing the time it takes for the electrical impulse to travel from the SA node to the AV node. An inverted T wave, an abnormally wide QRS complex (normal is less than 0.12 seconds), and absent P waves are all unexpected, abnormal findings that indicate potential cardiac issues.
</details>

---

### Question 37
**Lens: Client Teaching**
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly prescribed an oral antihypertensive medication. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of safety precautions?

A. "I will double my medication dose if I wake up with a headache."  
B. "I will change positions slowly when moving from sitting to standing."  
C. "I can stop taking the pills once my blood pressure readings reach normal levels."  
D. "I should drink a glass of grapefruit juice to help absorb my morning dose."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Antihypertensive medications often cause orthostatic hypotension, which is a sudden drop in blood pressure upon standing. Changing positions slowly is a critical safety measure to prevent dizziness and falls. Clients should never double a dose without medical instruction, should not stop medications abruptly even if blood pressure normalizes, and should avoid grapefruit juice with many cardiac medications due to interaction risks.
</details>

---

### Question 38
**Lens: Delegation**
A registered nurse (RN) is coordinating care for a stable client admitted with chronic heart failure. Which task is most appropriate for the RN to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Assessing the client's lung bases for the presence of crackles.  
B. Explaining the importance of a daily sodium restriction to the client.  
C. Weighing the client on the standing scale before breakfast.  
D. Evaluating the effectiveness of the client's morning diuretic dose.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Obtaining a daily weight is a standard, routine procedure that is well within the scope of practice for a UAP and provides vital data for managing heart failure. Assessing lung sounds, teaching dietary restrictions, and evaluating medication effectiveness are all tasks that require nursing judgment and cannot be delegated.
</details>

---

### Question 39
**Lens: Procedural Step**
A nurse is preparing a client for a resting 12-lead electrocardiogram (EKG). Which procedural step must the nurse take to ensure a clear and accurate waveform tracing?

A. Instruct the client to take deep, rapid breaths during the recording.  
B. Place the precordial chest leads directly over the client's hospital gown.  
C. Ask the client to grip the bed rails tightly to avoid sudden movement.  
D. Ensure the client's chest skin is clean, dry, and free of excessive hair.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** To ensure excellent electrode adhesion and a clear electrical signal without artifact, the skin must be clean, dry, and sometimes clipped of excessive hair. The client should breathe normally (not rapidly), electrodes must be placed directly on the skin (not clothing), and the client should remain relaxed with arms resting at their sides, as gripping rails will cause skeletal muscle artifact on the EKG tracing.
</details>

---

### Question 40
**Lens: Evaluation**
A client with a history of right-sided heart failure has been receiving diuretic therapy and fluid restrictions for the past three days. Which clinical finding best indicates to the nurse that the treatment plan is effective?

A. The client currently has 3+ pitting edema in both lower extremities.  
B. The client's jugular vein distension (JVD) is no longer visible.  
C. The client reports waking up gasping for air in the middle of the night.  
D. The client's daily weight shows an increase of 2 pounds (0.9 kg) since admission.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Right-sided heart failure leads to systemic venous congestion, which presents as jugular vein distension (JVD) and peripheral edema. The resolution of JVD indicates a successful decrease in fluid volume overload, showing that the diuretic therapy is effective. Pitting edema, paroxysmal nocturnal dyspnea (gasping for air), and weight gain all indicate worsening or ongoing fluid retention.
</details>
### Question 41
A nurse is reviewing the electrocardiogram (EKG) of a healthy adult client during a routine physical exam. The nurse observes a positive, upright wave just before every QRS complex. The nurse recognizes that this wave represents which of the following cardiac events?

A) Ventricular repolarization  
B) Ventricular depolarization  
C) Atrial depolarization  
D) Atrial repolarization  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
<b>Lens: Data Interpretation</b><br>
In a normal sinus rhythm, the positive, upright deflection preceding the QRS complex is the P wave. The P wave represents atrial depolarization, which is the electrical impulse spreading through the atria causing them to contract. The QRS complex represents ventricular depolarization, and the T wave represents ventricular repolarization. Atrial repolarization is hidden within the QRS complex.
</details>

---

### Question 42
A client is newly prescribed an oral antihypertensive medication. Which instruction should the nurse include in the teaching plan to promote client safety?

A) "Stop taking the medication immediately if you feel dizzy."  
B) "Change positions slowly when moving from lying down to standing."  
C) "Double your next dose if you forget to take your morning pill."  
D) "Take hot baths in the evening to help relax your blood vessels."  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
<b>Lens: Client Teaching</b><br>
Antihypertensive medications often cause orthostatic (postural) hypotension, which can lead to dizziness and falls. Teaching the client to change positions slowly promotes safety. Clients should never abruptly stop blood pressure medication without consulting their provider (A), should never double doses (C), and should avoid extreme heat like hot baths (D) as it can cause excessive vasodilation and sudden drops in blood pressure.
</details>

---

### Question 43
The nurse is assessing a client with a known history of right-sided heart failure. Which clinical manifestation is an expected finding specifically associated with this condition?

A) Bilateral pitting edema in the lower extremities  
B) Crackles heard in the bases of the lungs  
C) Frothy, pink-tinged sputum  
D) Shortness of breath when lying flat  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
<b>Lens: Expected vs Unexpected</b><br>
Right-sided heart failure causes an inability of the right ventricle to pump blood forward into the lungs, leading to a backup of fluid in the systemic venous system. An expected finding is peripheral edema, such as bilateral pitting edema in the lower extremities. Crackles, frothy pink sputum, and orthopnea (shortness of breath when lying flat) are expected manifestations of left-sided heart failure, which causes fluid backup into the lungs.
</details>

---

### Question 44
A registered nurse (RN) is caring for a client admitted with essential hypertension. Which task is most appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Educate the client about choosing a low-sodium diet.  
B) Assess the client for the presence of a morning headache.  
C) Administer the client's scheduled oral blood pressure medication.  
D) Obtain the client's morning blood pressure using an electronic cuff.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
<b>Lens: Delegation</b><br>
Obtaining routine vital signs, including blood pressure, is within the scope of practice for the UAP. Education (A), assessment (B), and medication administration (C) require the nursing judgment, skill, and licensure of an RN or LPN/VN, and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 45
A client with heart failure has been receiving a daily dose of oral furosemide, a loop diuretic. Which finding best indicates to the nurse that the treatment plan has been effective?

A) The client reports feeling more thirsty than usual.  
B) The client's heart rate increases from 70 to 88 beats per minute.  
C) The client's daily morning weight has decreased by 2 pounds.  
D) The client's urine output has been 30 mL over the last 2 hours.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
<b>Lens: Evaluation</b><br>
Furosemide is a diuretic used in heart failure to remove excess fluid from the body. The most reliable indicator of fluid loss and effective diuretic therapy is a decrease in the client's daily weight. Increased thirst is a side effect but does not evaluate therapeutic success. An increased heart rate may indicate hypovolemia. A urine output of 30 mL over 2 hours (15 mL/hr) is dangerously low (oliguria) and indicates an unexpected or adverse outcome, not an effective one.
</details>
### Question 46
A nurse is reviewing a normal electrocardiogram (EKG) tracing for a healthy adult client. When interpreting the tracing, what does the P wave represent?

A) Ventricular repolarization  
B) Atrial depolarization  
C) Ventricular depolarization  
D) Atrial repolarization  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Lens:** Data Interpretation  
**Rationale:** On a normal EKG, the P wave represents atrial depolarization, which is the electrical signal that causes the atria to contract. Ventricular depolarization is represented by the QRS complex, and ventricular repolarization is represented by the T wave. Atrial repolarization occurs simultaneously with ventricular depolarization and is hidden by the QRS complex.
</details>

---

### Question 47
The nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client recently diagnosed with basic right-sided heart failure. Which statement by the client indicates an accurate understanding of the dietary instructions?

A) "I should drink at least 3 liters of water every day to flush out my kidneys."  
B) "I will avoid eating fresh apples, bananas, and pears."  
C) "I need to read food labels closely and limit my daily sodium intake."  
D) "I can use plenty of soy sauce as long as I don't add regular table salt."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Lens:** Client Teaching  
**Rationale:** Clients with heart failure must limit sodium intake to prevent fluid retention, which exacerbates heart failure symptoms. Reading food labels is a critical step in managing sodium intake. Drinking 3 liters of water is contraindicated as fluid restrictions are often needed. Fresh fruits are generally low in sodium and encouraged. Soy sauce is extremely high in sodium and should be avoided. 
</details>

---

### Question 48
A client with primary hypertension is prescribed a daily oral anti-hypertensive medication. After starting the medication, the nurse assesses the client. Which finding is an expected, minor side effect requiring basic safety precautions rather than an unexpected adverse reaction requiring the drug to be stopped?

A) Mild dizziness when standing up quickly  
B) Swelling of the lips, tongue, and throat  
C) A generalized, intensely itchy red rash over the chest  
D) Sudden and extreme shortness of breath while resting  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected  
**Rationale:** Mild dizziness when changing positions (orthostatic hypotension) is a common, expected side effect of many anti-hypertensive medications. The nurse should implement safety precautions, such as teaching the client to change positions slowly. Swelling of the lips/tongue (angioedema), generalized rashes, and extreme shortness of breath are unexpected, severe adverse reactions that require immediate medical intervention.
</details>

---

### Question 49
A registered nurse (RN) is caring for a stable client with chronic heart failure. Which task is most appropriate for the RN to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Evaluating the client's lung sounds for the presence of crackles  
B) Providing education on how to recognize worsening leg edema  
C) Administering the morning dose of oral furosemide  
D) Assisting the client to the bathroom and recording the urine output  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Lens:** Delegation  
**Rationale:** Assisting a stable client to the bathroom and recording daily output are routine tasks within the UAP's scope of practice. The RN cannot delegate tasks requiring nursing judgment, such as evaluating or assessing lung sounds (A), providing client education (B), or administering medications (C).
</details>

---

### Question 50
The nurse is preparing a client for a resting 12-lead electrocardiogram (EKG). Which procedural step should the nurse perform to ensure an accurate reading?

A) Instruct the client to lie down, remain still, and avoid talking during the tracing.  
B) Place the chest electrodes directly over the client's shirt and undergarments.  
C) Ask the client to hold their breath for a full 30 seconds during the test.  
D) Encourage the client to drink a cup of ice water right before applying the leads.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Lens:** Procedural Step  
**Rationale:** To obtain a clear and accurate EKG tracing without artifact, the client must lie still and avoid talking, as muscle movement can interfere with the electrical signals. Electrodes must be placed directly on bare skin, not clothing. The client should breathe normally, not hold their breath, and drinking ice water is unnecessary and could induce shivering, which creates artifact on the tracing.
</details>
### Question 51
A client with newly diagnosed hypertension is prescribed a daily oral antihypertensive medication. What instruction is most important for the nurse to include in the discharge teaching to promote client safety?

A. "Stop taking the medication immediately if you feel dizzy."
B. "Change positions slowly from lying down to standing up."
C. "Take your blood pressure every hour while awake."
D. "Restrict your daily fluid intake to 1000 mL."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<em>Lens: Client Teaching</em><br>
Antihypertensive medications commonly cause orthostatic hypotension, which increases the risk of falls and injury, especially when initiating therapy. Teaching the client to change positions slowly promotes safety by allowing the cardiovascular system time to adjust. Stopping the medication abruptly (A) can cause rebound hypertension. Hourly blood pressure checks (C) are unnecessary and anxiety-provoking for uncomplicated hypertension. Fluid restriction (D) is not a standard intervention for simple hypertension unless concurrent heart failure is present.
</details>

---

### Question 52
A nurse is assessing a client with a history of right-sided heart failure. Which clinical finding should the nurse expect to observe during the physical assessment?

A. Pink, frothy sputum
B. Crackles in the lung bases
C. Peripheral pitting edema
D. Frequent night sweats

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<em>Lens: Expected vs Unexpected</em><br>
Right-sided heart failure causes systemic venous congestion, leading to expected findings such as peripheral pitting edema, jugular venous distention (JVD), and ascites. Pink, frothy sputum (A) and crackles in the lungs (B) are expected findings of left-sided heart failure due to pulmonary congestion. Night sweats (D) are not associated with heart failure; they are typically seen with infections like tuberculosis or menopause. 
</details>

---

### Question 53
The nurse is preparing to perform a routine 12-lead electrocardiogram (EKG) on a resting client. Which action should the nurse take to ensure an accurate tracing?

A. Ask the client to lie completely still and breathe normally.
B. Place the chest electrodes directly over the client's clothing.
C. Have the client sit upright in a chair with their legs crossed.
D. Instruct the client to hold their breath for 10 seconds during the reading.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<em>Lens: Procedural Step</em><br>
When obtaining a 12-lead EKG, the client should lie flat (supine) or in semi-Fowler's position, remain completely still, and breathe normally to prevent muscle movement or respiratory artifacts from distorting the EKG tracing. Electrodes must be placed directly on bare skin (B). Crossing legs (C) can cause muscle tension and artifacts. Holding the breath (D) is unnecessary and may cause the client distress or alter the heart rate.
</details>

---

### Question 54
A client is admitted for observation of uncomplicated primary hypertension. Which task is appropriate for the registered nurse (RN) to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Evaluate the effectiveness of the client's new blood pressure medication.
B. Teach the client about making low-sodium food choices.
C. Assess the client for headaches and visual changes.
D. Measure and record the client's morning blood pressure.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<em>Lens: Delegation</em><br>
The RN can delegate routine, non-invasive tasks such as measuring and recording vital signs to the UAP. Evaluating medication effectiveness (A), providing client teaching (B), and performing physical assessments (C) require the advanced knowledge, skill, and judgment of a licensed nurse and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 55
A client recently diagnosed with heart failure tells the nurse, "I am so overwhelmed by all these new diet rules and having to weigh myself every single day. It's just too much." Which response by the nurse is most therapeutic?

A. "At least you caught the heart failure early before it got worse."
B. "It sounds like you are feeling frustrated with these new lifestyle changes."
C. "Weighing yourself daily is the only way to keep you out of the hospital."
D. "Don't worry, you will get used to the low-sodium diet very quickly."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<em>Lens: Therapeutic Communication</em><br>
Stating, "It sounds like you are feeling frustrated..." utilizes the therapeutic communication technique of reflection and validation. It acknowledges the client's feelings and encourages further exploration of their concerns. Option A minimizes the client's feelings. Option C is threatening and dismissive of the client's emotional state. Option D provides false reassurance, which halts therapeutic dialogue.
</details>
### Question 56
A nurse is assessing a client with a history of right-sided heart failure. Using the lens of **EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED** findings, which of the following clinical manifestations should the nurse anticipate?

A) Jugular venous distention
B) Crackles in the lung bases
C) Dry, hacking cough
D) Orthopnea

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Jugular venous distention

**Rationale:** Right-sided heart failure impairs the heart's ability to pump blood effectively to the lungs, causing blood to back up into the systemic venous circulation. This systemic congestion typically presents as jugular venous distention (JVD), peripheral edema, and hepatomegaly. Crackles, a dry cough, and orthopnea are expected findings of *left-sided* heart failure due to pulmonary congestion.
</details>

---

### Question 57
A nurse is performing **DATA INTERPRETATION** on a normal electrocardiogram (ECG) rhythm strip for a healthy adult client. Which of the following waveforms correctly represents ventricular depolarization?

A) P wave
B) U wave
C) QRS complex
D) T wave

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) QRS complex

**Rationale:** On a normal ECG, the QRS complex represents ventricular depolarization (the electrical impulse spreading through the ventricles), which triggers ventricular contraction. The P wave represents atrial depolarization, the T wave represents ventricular repolarization, and the U wave (if present) is thought to represent repolarization of the Purkinje fibers.
</details>

---

### Question 58
A nurse is providing **CLIENT TEACHING** to a patient who is newly prescribed a daily antihypertensive medication. Which instruction must the nurse include to promote safety?

A) "Take the medication with a full glass of milk to prevent upset stomach."
B) "Change positions slowly from lying to sitting to standing."
C) "Stop taking the pill immediately if you develop a mild headache."
D) "Check your temperature every morning before taking the medication."

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "Change positions slowly from lying to sitting to standing."

**Rationale:** A common and significant safety risk associated with antihypertensive medications is orthostatic (postural) hypotension, which can lead to dizziness and falls. Teaching the client to change positions slowly allows their cardiovascular system time to adjust. Mild headaches do not warrant abrupt discontinuation (which could cause rebound hypertension), and temperature monitoring is not a standard safety protocol for basic antihypertensives.
</details>

---

### Question 59
The nurse is preparing to place electrodes on a client's chest for a standard 12-lead ECG. As a strict **PROCEDURAL STEP**, where should the nurse properly place the V1 chest lead?

A) Fourth intercostal space at the left sternal border
B) Fifth intercostal space at the midclavicular line
C) Fifth intercostal space at the anterior axillary line
D) Fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border

**Rationale:** Proper electrode placement is crucial for an accurate ECG reading. The V1 lead is placed at the fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border. The V2 lead is placed at the fourth intercostal space at the left sternal border, and V4 is placed at the fifth intercostal space at the midclavicular line.
</details>

---

### Question 60
A client with fluid volume overload due to basic heart failure has been receiving diuretic therapy for the past two days. During the **EVALUATION** of the client's response to treatment, which finding indicates to the nurse that the therapy is effective?

A) The client exhibits 2+ pitting edema in both ankles.
B) The client reports feeling more thirsty than usual.
C) The client's daily weight has decreased by 2 kg (4.4 lbs).
D) The client's blood pressure increased from 110/70 to 130/80 mmHg.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) The client's daily weight has decreased by 2 kg (4.4 lbs).

**Rationale:** Daily weights are the most accurate indicator of fluid volume status. A decrease in weight (2 kg = roughly 2 liters of fluid) demonstrates that the diuretic is effectively pulling excess fluid from the body. Pitting edema indicates ongoing fluid overload, an increase in blood pressure does not indicate fluid offloading, and thirst is a side effect but not a direct measure of therapeutic effectiveness.
</details>
### Question 61
**Lens: CLIENT TEACHING**
A nurse is teaching a client newly diagnosed with hypertension about safety measures related to their new blood pressure medications. What statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should stop taking my medication immediately if I start to feel dizzy."
B) "I will change positions slowly, especially when standing up from a bed or chair."
C) "I should restrict my water intake so my blood pressure will go down faster."
D) "I will take hot baths every evening to help relax my blood vessels."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Antihypertensive medications can cause orthostatic hypotension, leading to dizziness and an increased risk of falls. Changing positions slowly allows the cardiovascular system time to adjust, keeping the client safe. Stopping medications abruptly without consulting a provider can cause rebound hypertension. Restricting fluids unnecessarily can lead to dehydration and worsening hypotension. Hot baths cause vasodilation, which can dangerously lower blood pressure and increase dizziness.
</details>

---

### Question 62
**Lens: DATA INTERPRETATION**
A nurse is reviewing the electrocardiogram (EKG) of a healthy adult client during a routine physical. Which of the following EKG waveforms should the nurse interpret as a normal finding representing ventricular depolarization?

A) P wave
B) T wave
C) QRS complex
D) U wave

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
The QRS complex represents ventricular depolarization, which triggers the contraction of the ventricles. This is a normal and expected finding on an EKG. The P wave represents atrial depolarization. The T wave represents ventricular repolarization. A U wave is an uncommon finding that may occasionally be seen in normal individuals but is often associated with hypokalemia.
</details>

---

### Question 63
**Lens: EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED**
The nurse is assessing a client with a known history of right-sided heart failure. Which of the following assessment findings is an expected manifestation of this specific condition?

A) Peripheral edema in the lower extremities
B) Pink, frothy sputum
C) Crackles heard in the lung bases
D) Severe shortness of breath when lying flat

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Right-sided heart failure involves the right ventricle's inability to pump effectively, causing blood to back up into the systemic venous system. This results in expected findings such as peripheral edema (swelling in the legs and ankles), jugular venous distention (JVD), and hepatomegaly. Pink frothy sputum, crackles in the lungs, and orthopnea (shortness of breath when lying flat) are expected manifestations of left-sided heart failure due to fluid backing up into the pulmonary system.
</details>

---

### Question 64
**Lens: DELEGATION**
A registered nurse (RN) is caring for a client admitted with stable heart failure and a history of hypertension. Which task is appropriate to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Evaluating the client's understanding of a newly prescribed low-sodium diet
B) Assessing the client's lung sounds to check for signs of fluid overload
C) Administering the client's scheduled morning dose of an oral antihypertensive
D) Recording the client's daily morning weight before breakfast

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Recording a daily weight is a standard, routine task with predictable outcomes that falls safely within the UAP's scope of practice. Daily weights are crucial for monitoring fluid status in heart failure. Evaluating teaching (A), performing physiological assessments (B), and administering medications (C) require the clinical judgment and specialized training of a licensed nurse and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 65
**Lens: PROCEDURAL STEP**
A nurse is preparing to perform a standard 12-lead electrocardiogram (EKG) on a client admitted for monitoring. Which action is the correct procedural step when placing the chest (precordial) leads?

A) Place lead V1 at the fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border.
B) Place lead V2 at the fifth intercostal space at the midclavicular line.
C) Place lead V4 at the fourth intercostal space at the left sternal border.
D) Place all chest leads securely over the client's hospital gown to ensure privacy.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The correct anatomical placement for lead V1 is the fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border. Lead V2 belongs at the fourth intercostal space at the left sternal border. Lead V4 is placed at the fifth intercostal space at the midclavicular line. All EKG electrodes must be placed directly on the client's bare skin, not over clothing or gowns, to obtain an accurate electrical tracing.
</details>
### Question 66
A nurse is assessing a client who has a history of right-sided heart failure. Which finding is considered an expected manifestation specifically related to this condition?

A) Shortness of breath while lying flat in bed
B) Crackles heard upon auscultation of the lung bases
C) Dependent edema in the lower extremities 
D) A productive cough with pink, frothy sputum

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected  
Right-sided heart failure causes blood to back up into the systemic circulation, leading to systemic venous congestion. This presents clinically as dependent peripheral edema (such as swelling in the legs and ankles), ascites, and jugular venous distention. Options A, B, and D are classic expected findings of left-sided heart failure, which causes blood to back up into the pulmonary system.
</details>

---

### Question 67
The nurse is providing education to a client newly diagnosed with primary hypertension. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of safety and lifestyle management?

A) "I should limit my aerobic exercise to only once a week to avoid straining my heart."
B) "I will read food nutrition labels carefully to help reduce my daily sodium intake."
C) "I can stop taking my blood pressure medication as soon as my daily readings are normal."
D) "I will reduce my daily water intake so my blood pressure doesn't go up."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Lens:** Client Teaching  
Reducing dietary sodium is a foundational lifestyle modification for managing hypertension, and reading food labels helps clients identify hidden sodium. Aerobic exercise should be done regularly (typically most days of the week), not just once a week. Antihypertensive medications should never be stopped abruptly, even if readings are normal, as this can cause rebound hypertension. Fluid restriction is not a standard treatment for primary hypertension unless the client also has a condition like heart failure.
</details>

---

### Question 68
A nurse is reviewing a client's electrocardiogram (ECG) tracing and identifies a normal sinus rhythm. When interpreting the cardiac cycle on the ECG, what does the nurse understand the P wave to represent?

A) Ventricular repolarization
B) Ventricular depolarization
C) Atrial repolarization
D) Atrial depolarization

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Lens:** Data Interpretation  
In a normal ECG tracing, the P wave represents atrial depolarization, which is the electrical signal that causes the atria to contract. The QRS complex represents ventricular depolarization, and the T wave represents ventricular repolarization. Atrial repolarization is typically hidden within the QRS complex and is not seen as a distinct wave.
</details>

---

### Question 69
The registered nurse (RN) is caring for a client with a history of heart failure and hypertension. Which task is most appropriate for the RN to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Obtain and record the client's daily morning weight.
B) Assess the severity of the client's peripheral edema.
C) Educate the client about low-sodium dietary choices.
D) Evaluate the client's response to an administered oral antihypertensive.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Lens:** Delegation  
Obtaining a daily weight is a standard, routine measurement that does not require nursing judgment, making it safe and appropriate to delegate to a UAP. Assessing edema, providing client education, and evaluating therapeutic responses to medications all require nursing judgment and fall strictly within the scope of practice of the RN.
</details>

---

### Question 70
The nurse is preparing to perform a standard 12-lead electrocardiogram (ECG) on a client as part of a routine cardiac screening. To ensure an accurate reading, where should the nurse place the V4 precordial electrode?

A) The fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border
B) The fourth intercostal space at the left sternal border
C) The fifth intercostal space at the midclavicular line
D) The fifth intercostal space at the midaxillary line

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Lens:** Procedural Step  
Proper electrode placement is essential for an accurate ECG. The V4 electrode must be placed at the fifth intercostal space at the midclavicular line. The fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border is for V1. The fourth intercostal space at the left sternal border is for V2. The fifth intercostal space at the midaxillary line is for V6.
</details>
### Question 71
**Lens:** Procedural Step  
**Topic:** Normal EKG  

A nurse is preparing to perform a routine 12-lead electrocardiogram (EKG) on a conscious adult client. Which action is the most appropriate procedural step to ensure a clear and accurate tracing?

A) Place the client in a prone position before attaching the electrodes.  
B) Ensure the client's skin is clean, dry, and free of excess oils or lotions.  
C) Ask the client to hold their breath for 20 seconds while the machine records.  
D) Apply the precordial (chest) leads directly over the client's clothing.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Ensure the client's skin is clean, dry, and free of excess oils or lotions.  

**Rationale:** For an EKG machine to accurately capture the electrical activity of the heart, the electrodes must have direct, unimpeded contact with the skin. Cleaning and drying the skin removes oils, lotions, or moisture that could cause artifact or prevent the electrodes from sticking. The client should ideally be in a supine or semi-Fowler's position (not prone), should breathe normally (holding breath is unnecessary), and leads must be placed directly on bare skin, not over clothing.
</details>

---

### Question 72
**Lens:** Client Teaching  
**Topic:** Basic Heart Failure  

A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly diagnosed with basic heart failure. Which instruction regarding daily weight monitoring should the nurse include?

A) "Weigh yourself at the same time every morning after emptying your bladder."  
B) "Weigh yourself right before going to bed each night."  
C) "Weigh yourself once a week on the same day."  
D) "Weigh yourself only when you notice swelling in your feet or ankles."  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "Weigh yourself at the same time every morning after emptying your bladder."  

**Rationale:** Daily weights are the most reliable indicator of fluid status in clients with heart failure. To ensure consistency and accuracy, clients should be taught to weigh themselves every morning, after voiding, before eating or drinking, and while wearing similar clothing. Weighing at night, weekly, or only when symptomatic delays the recognition of fluid retention.
</details>

---

### Question 73
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected  
**Topic:** Hypertension Safety  

A client who recently started taking a new antihypertensive medication asks the nurse what side effects to look out for. Which of the following is an expected side effect that the nurse should teach the client to manage safely?

A) Ringing in the ears when lying flat.  
B) A sudden, severe spike in blood pressure.  
C) A resting heart rate drop to 30 beats per minute.  
D) Dizziness when standing up too quickly from a seated position.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Dizziness when standing up too quickly from a seated position.  

**Rationale:** Orthostatic (postural) hypotension is a common and expected side effect of many antihypertensive medications. Clients should be taught safety measures, such as changing positions slowly (from lying to sitting, and sitting to standing), to prevent dizziness and potential falls. Severe bradycardia, ringing in the ears (tinnitus), and sudden blood pressure spikes are unexpected and warrant immediate medical evaluation.
</details>

---

### Question 74
**Lens:** Delegation  
**Topic:** Basic Heart Failure  

The registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a stable client with heart failure. Which task is most appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Teach the client about low-sodium food choices for lunch.  
B) Assess the client's lung sounds for the presence of crackles.  
C) Assist the client to the standing scale to obtain a morning weight.  
D) Evaluate the client's response to the morning dose of a diuretic.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Assist the client to the standing scale to obtain a morning weight.  

**Rationale:** Assisting a stable client to the scale and recording their weight is a routine, standard task that falls safely within the UAP's scope of practice. The RN cannot delegate teaching (Option A), assessment (Option B), or evaluation (Option D), as these require the specialized knowledge and clinical judgment of a licensed nurse.
</details>

### Question 75
**Lens:** Data Interpretation  
**Topic:** Normal EKG  

A nurse is reviewing a normal EKG tracing for a healthy adult client. The nurse observes a normal, upright P wave on the telemetry strip. The nurse correctly interprets that the P wave represents which physiological event in the heart?

A) Ventricular repolarization.  
B) Atrial depolarization.  
C) Ventricular depolarization.  
D) Purkinje fiber activation.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Atrial depolarization.  

**Rationale:** In a normal EKG tracing, the P wave represents atrial depolarization, which is the electrical signal that causes the atria to contract. Ventricular depolarization is represented by the QRS complex, and ventricular repolarization is represented by the T wave. 
</details>
### Question 76
The nurse is reviewing the electrocardiogram (EKG) of a healthy adult client. Which finding indicates normal atrial depolarization?

A. A narrow QRS complex
B. The presence of a P wave before every QRS complex
C. A T wave that is inverted
D. An elevated ST segment

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Data Interpretation</strong><br>
In a normal sinus rhythm EKG, the P wave represents atrial depolarization and should appear upright and precede every QRS complex. A narrow QRS represents normal ventricular depolarization. Inverted T waves or elevated ST segments are abnormal findings that indicate cardiac ischemia or injury, not normal EKG activity.
</details>

---

### Question 77
A client is newly prescribed an antihypertensive medication. Which instruction should the nurse include in the teaching plan to prevent injury?

A. "Stop taking the medication if you experience a headache."
B. "Take the medication on an empty stomach to increase absorption."
C. "Change positions slowly from lying to standing."
D. "Limit your fluid intake to 1 liter per day."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Client Teaching</strong><br>
Antihypertensive medications can cause orthostatic (postural) hypotension, which places the client at a high risk for falls. Changing positions slowly allows the cardiovascular system time to adjust, preventing dizziness and injury. Clients should never stop antihypertensives abruptly due to the risk of rebound hypertension. Fluid limitation and food requirements vary widely depending on the specific drug and overall health status, but changing positions slowly is a universal safety measure.
</details>

---

### Question 78
The registered nurse (RN) is caring for a client with an exacerbation of heart failure. Which task is most appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Evaluating the client's respiratory response to an administered diuretic.
B. Listening to the client's lung sounds for crackles.
C. Teaching the client about a low-sodium diet.
D. Assisting the client onto the scale to obtain a daily morning weight.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Delegation</strong><br>
Obtaining a daily weight is a standard, routine procedure that is well within the scope of practice for a UAP. Daily weights are crucial for monitoring fluid retention in heart failure. Evaluating medication responses, assessing lung sounds, and providing dietary teaching all require the clinical judgment and assessment skills of the RN and cannot be delegated to UAP.
</details>
