### Question 79
The nurse is assessing a client with a history of left-sided heart failure. Which clinical finding should the nurse expect to observe?

A. Crackles heard during lung auscultation
B. Severe jugular venous distention
C. Dependent pitting edema in the ankles
D. Enlargement of the liver (hepatomegaly)

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Expected vs Unexpected</strong><br>
Left-sided heart failure primarily affects the pulmonary system because the left ventricle cannot pump blood effectively to the body, causing fluid to back up into the lungs. This presents expectedly as crackles, shortness of breath, and a moist cough. Jugular venous distention, peripheral ankle edema, and hepatomegaly are expected findings of right-sided heart failure, where fluid backs up into the systemic venous circulation.
</details>

---

### Question 80
The nurse is preparing to measure a client's blood pressure to screen for hypertension. Which procedural step is essential for obtaining an accurate reading?

A. Having the client sit with their legs crossed at the knees
B. Positioning the client's arm completely relaxed and supported at heart level
C. Deflating the blood pressure cuff rapidly at 10 mm Hg per second
D. Using a blood pressure cuff that is slightly too small for the arm

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Procedural Step</strong><br>
To obtain an accurate blood pressure reading, the client's arm should be relaxed and supported at heart level. An unsupported arm or an arm positioned below heart level can cause a falsely high reading. The client's legs should be uncrossed with feet flat on the floor, the deflation rate should be a slow and steady 2 to 3 mm Hg per second, and a cuff that is too small will result in a falsely elevated reading.
</details>
### Question 81
A nurse is reviewing a normal electrocardiogram (ECG) for a healthy adult client during a routine physical. Which of the following electrical activities does the P wave represent?

A) Ventricular repolarization
B) Ventricular depolarization
C) Atrial depolarization
D) Atrial repolarization

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C) Atrial depolarization</strong><br><br>
<strong>Data Interpretation:</strong> In a normal ECG rhythm, the P wave represents atrial depolarization, which is the electrical signal that causes the atria to contract. Ventricular depolarization is represented by the QRS complex (Option B), and ventricular repolarization is represented by the T wave (Option A). Atrial repolarization (Option D) occurs at the same time as ventricular depolarization and is typically hidden within the QRS complex. 
</details>

---

### Question 82
A nurse is performing a physical assessment on a client with a history of right-sided heart failure. Which of the following findings should the nurse expect to observe?

A) Peripheral edema
B) Crackles in the lungs
C) Shortness of breath at rest
D) Pink, frothy sputum

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A) Peripheral edema</strong><br><br>
<strong>Expected vs Unexpected:</strong> Right-sided heart failure causes blood to back up into the systemic venous system. Therefore, expected findings include peripheral edema (swelling in the legs and ankles), jugular venous distention, and an enlarged liver. Crackles (Option B), shortness of breath at rest (Option C), and pink, frothy sputum (Option D) are signs of pulmonary congestion, which are expected in left-sided heart failure, not right-sided.
</details>

---

### Question 83
A nurse is reinforcing teaching with a client who has newly diagnosed essential hypertension and was prescribed a daily antihypertensive medication. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of safety measures regarding the medication?

A) "I can stop taking my medication once my blood pressure is normal."
B) "I should change positions slowly when standing up."
C) "I will double my next dose if I forget to take my pill."
D) "I should avoid drinking water when taking this medication."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B) "I should change positions slowly when standing up."</strong><br><br>
<strong>Client Teaching:</strong> Changing positions slowly helps prevent orthostatic hypotension, a common side effect of antihypertensive medications that can lead to dizziness and falls. Clients should be taught never to stop taking blood pressure medication abruptly without consulting their provider (Option A), as this can cause rebound hypertension. They should never double a dose (Option C) and should take the medication with a normal amount of water (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 84
A nurse is assigning morning care for a group of clients on a medical-surgical unit. Which of the following tasks related to a client with hypertension is appropriate to delegate to an Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A) Evaluating the effectiveness of the client's morning blood pressure medication.
B) Teaching the client about a low-sodium diet.
C) Assisting the client with ambulation to the bathroom.
D) Recommending over-the-counter pain relievers that are safe for high blood pressure.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C) Assisting the client with ambulation to the bathroom.</strong><br><br>
<strong>Delegation:</strong> Assisting a stable client with ambulation is a standard, routine task that falls safely within the UAP's scope of practice. Evaluating medication effectiveness (Option A), teaching dietary modifications (Option B), and recommending medications (Option D) all require nursing judgment, assessment, and knowledge, which must be performed by the registered nurse.
</details>

### Question 85
A client with a recent diagnosis of heart failure tells the nurse, "I feel so useless now that I can't do all the yard work like I used to because I get tired so quickly." Which of the following responses by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A) "You should be thankful you are still able to walk around the house."
B) "Why do you feel useless when you have a family that loves you?"
C) "I will ask the doctor to increase your energy medication."
D) "It sounds like you are feeling frustrated by these new physical limitations."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D) "It sounds like you are feeling frustrated by these new physical limitations."</strong><br><br>
<strong>Therapeutic Communication:</strong> This response uses the therapeutic communication techniques of reflection and validation. It acknowledges the client's feelings of frustration regarding their fatigue and limitations without minimizing their feelings. Option A minimizes the client's feelings. Option B uses a "why" question, which can make the client feel defensive. Option C ignores the client's emotional concern and gives false reassurance about an "energy medication."
</details>
### Question 86
A nurse is teaching a client newly diagnosed with primary hypertension who is prescribed a daily antihypertensive medication. Which instruction should the nurse include to promote safety?

A) "Stop taking the medication immediately if you experience a mild headache."
B) "Change positions slowly from lying down to standing up."
C) "Take the medication with a large glass of grapefruit juice."
D) "Limit your daily water intake to 1 liter to prevent swelling."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Changing positions slowly prevents orthostatic hypotension, a common and potentially dangerous side effect of many antihypertensive medications that can lead to dizziness and falls. Mild headaches may occur but stopping the medication abruptly can cause dangerous rebound hypertension. Grapefruit juice interacts with many cardiac medications, and general fluid restriction is not indicated for uncomplicated hypertension unless prescribed for a concurrent condition like heart failure. 
<em>Lens: Client Teaching</em>
</details>

---

### Question 87
The nurse is reviewing a normal electrocardiogram (EKG) tracing for a healthy adult client. The nurse understands that the P wave represents which physiological event in the heart?

A) Ventricular repolarization
B) Ventricular depolarization
C) Atrial depolarization
D) Atrial repolarization

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
On a normal EKG tracing, the P wave represents atrial depolarization, which is the electrical impulse spreading through the atria causing them to contract. Ventricular depolarization is represented by the QRS complex, and ventricular repolarization is represented by the T wave. Atrial repolarization is typically hidden within the QRS complex.
<em>Lens: Data Interpretation</em>
</details>

---

### Question 88
A nurse is caring for a client admitted with an exacerbation of left-sided heart failure. Which of the following tasks can the nurse safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assisting the client onto the standing scale to obtain a daily morning weight.
B) Assessing the client's lung sounds for the presence of crackles.
C) Teaching the client about the importance of a low-sodium diet.
D) Evaluating the client's breathing response to an administered diuretic.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Obtaining a daily morning weight is a standard, routine procedure that falls within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing lung sounds, teaching dietary guidelines, and evaluating medication effectiveness are all core nursing responsibilities that require clinical judgment and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
<em>Lens: Delegation</em>
</details>

### Question 89
The nurse is preparing to perform a routine 12-lead electrocardiogram (EKG) on a client complaining of mild chest discomfort. Where should the nurse correctly place the V1 chest electrode?

A) Fifth intercostal space at the midclavicular line
B) Fourth intercostal space at the left sternal border
C) Fifth intercostal space at the anterior axillary line
D) Fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
For a standard 12-lead EKG, the V1 electrode is placed at the fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border. V2 is placed at the fourth intercostal space at the left sternal border. V4 is placed at the fifth intercostal space at the midclavicular line. Proper lead placement is essential for obtaining an accurate EKG reading.
<em>Lens: Procedural Step</em>
</details>

### Question 90
A client with a known history of chronic right-sided heart failure comes to the clinic for a routine follow-up. Which clinical finding should the nurse identify as an expected manifestation of this condition?

A) Pink, frothy sputum
B) Shortness of breath when lying flat
C) Bilateral pitting edema in the lower extremities
D) Dry, hacking cough

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Bilateral lower extremity edema is a classic, expected sign of right-sided heart failure. When the right ventricle fails, it cannot effectively pump blood into the lungs, causing fluid to back up into the systemic venous circulation, leading to peripheral edema, ascites, and jugular venous distention. Pink frothy sputum, orthopnea (shortness of breath lying flat), and a cough are typically manifestations of left-sided heart failure due to pulmonary congestion.
<em>Lens: Expected vs Unexpected</em>
</details>
### Question 91
A nurse is reviewing the electrocardiogram (EKG) of a healthy adult client during a routine physical. Based on the expected findings of a normal sinus rhythm, which of the following is an expected characteristic?

A) Absent P waves before the QRS complexes.
B) Inverted T waves in the majority of the leads.
C) A PR interval measuring between 0.12 and 0.20 seconds.
D) A QRS complex duration of 0.24 seconds.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> In a normal sinus rhythm, the PR interval is expected to measure between 0.12 and 0.20 seconds. This represents the normal time it takes for an electrical impulse to travel from the SA node to the AV node. Absent P waves, inverted T waves, and a prolonged QRS complex (normal is less than 0.12 seconds) are unexpected findings that indicate potential cardiac issues.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Cardiovascular System (Normal EKG)
</details>

---

### Question 92
A client is prescribed a new oral antihypertensive medication to manage essential hypertension. Which safety instruction should the nurse include during client teaching?

A) "Take the medication only when you are experiencing a severe headache."
B) "Change positions slowly when moving from lying down to standing up."
C) "Restrict your daily oral fluid intake to less than 1 liter per day."
D) "Stop taking the medication immediately if you feel mildly dizzy."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Antihypertensive medications commonly cause orthostatic (postural) hypotension, which can lead to dizziness and falls. Teaching the client to change positions slowly promotes safety and prevents injury. Antihypertensives should be taken as prescribed, not just when symptomatic. Abruptly stopping the medication can cause rebound hypertension. Fluid restriction is not a standard intervention for simple hypertension unless concurrent conditions like heart failure exist.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Cardiovascular System (Hypertension Safety)
</details>

---

### Question 93
The nurse is assessing a client with a known history of right-sided heart failure. When reviewing the client's physical assessment data, which finding is most consistent with this condition?

A) Frothy, pink-tinged sputum.
B) Crackles auscultated in the lung bases.
C) A dry, hacking, persistent cough.
D) Bilateral pitting edema in the lower extremities.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Right-sided heart failure causes systemic venous congestion because the right ventricle cannot effectively pump blood forward into the lungs. This leads to peripheral manifestations such as bilateral pitting edema in the lower extremities, jugular venous distention, and hepatomegaly. Frothy pink sputum, lung crackles, and a persistent cough are classic signs of left-sided heart failure due to pulmonary congestion.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Data Interpretation<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Cardiovascular System (Basic Heart Failure)
</details>

---

### Question 94
A nurse is preparing to perform a routine 12-lead electrocardiogram (EKG) on a client admitted for observation. Which action represents an appropriate procedural step?

A) Ensure the client's skin is clean, dry, and free of excessive hair before applying electrodes.
B) Instruct the client to take deep, rapid breaths while the machine records the tracing.
C) Place the precordial (chest) electrodes directly over the client's hospital gown.
D) Apply all four limb leads to the left side of the client's body.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Proper skin preparation is essential for a clear EKG tracing. The skin should be clean, dry, and free of excessive hair or oils to ensure the electrodes adhere properly and conduct electrical signals accurately. The client should breathe normally and remain still, not breathe rapidly. Electrodes must be placed directly on the skin, not over clothing. Limb leads must be placed on all four extremities (right arm, left arm, right leg, left leg), not just one side.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Cardiovascular System (Normal EKG)
</details>

### Question 95
A client newly diagnosed with heart failure and hypertension is placed on a strict low-sodium diet. The client sighs and says to the nurse, "I am never going to be able to eat any food I actually like again." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A) "You will get used to the taste of unsalted food eventually."
B) "Why do you think the doctor ordered this specific diet for you?"
C) "If you don't follow the diet, your blood pressure will continue to rise."
D) "It sounds like you are feeling frustrated about these new dietary changes."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Stating "It sounds like you are feeling frustrated..." utilizes the therapeutic communication technique of reflecting/acknowledging the client's feelings. This validates the client's emotional experience and encourages further exploration. Telling the client they will "get used to it" dismisses their feelings. Asking "why" can make the client feel defensive. Threatening the client with worsening conditions is non-therapeutic and damages the nurse-client relationship.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Therapeutic Communication<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Cardiovascular System (Basic Heart Failure & Hypertension)
</details>
### Question 96
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
A nurse is reviewing a normal electrocardiogram (EKG) strip for a healthy young adult during a routine physical. What does the P wave on the EKG tracing represent?

A) Ventricular repolarization
B) Atrial depolarization
C) Ventricular depolarization
D) Atrial repolarization

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Atrial depolarization

**Rationale:** In a normal EKG tracing, the P wave represents atrial depolarization, which is the electrical signal that causes the atria to contract. Ventricular depolarization is represented by the QRS complex. Ventricular repolarization is represented by the T wave. Atrial repolarization occurs simultaneously with ventricular depolarization and is hidden within the QRS complex.
</details>

---

### Question 97
**Lens:** Client Teaching
A nurse is teaching a client who was recently diagnosed with primary hypertension about home safety and lifestyle management. Which instruction should the nurse include in the teaching plan?

A) Engage in moderate aerobic exercise for at least 30 minutes most days of the week.
B) Increase sodium intake to help maintain blood vessel elasticity.
C) Avoid all dietary potassium to prevent interactions with blood pressure medications.
D) Limit daily physical activity to preserve cardiac muscle strength.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Engage in moderate aerobic exercise for at least 30 minutes most days of the week.

**Rationale:** Moderate aerobic exercise (like brisk walking) for 30 minutes most days of the week is a standard and highly effective lifestyle recommendation for managing primary hypertension and promoting cardiovascular safety. Clients with hypertension should restrict, not increase, their sodium intake. Adequate dietary potassium is generally encouraged to help lower blood pressure, unless specifically contraindicated by certain medications or underlying kidney issues. Physical activity should be encouraged, not limited.
</details>

---

### Question 98
**Lens:** Delegation
A nurse is caring for a client with stable heart failure and a history of hypertension. Which task is most appropriate for the nurse to safely delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Teaching the client how to read food labels for hidden sodium content.
B) Evaluating the effectiveness of the client's morning dose of a diuretic.
C) Assessing the client's lung sounds for the presence of fluid.
D) Assisting the client with daily weighing on the same scale every morning.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Assisting the client with daily weighing on the same scale every morning.

**Rationale:** Daily weights are a crucial, routine task for monitoring fluid retention in heart failure clients. This is a standard, non-invasive procedure that can safely be delegated to a UAP. Teaching, evaluating interventions, and assessing physiological conditions (like listening to lung sounds) fall strictly within the registered nurse's scope of practice and cannot be delegated to UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 99
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
A nurse is assessing a client with a known history of right-sided heart failure. Which of the following findings is an expected manifestation of this specific condition?

A) Pink, frothy sputum
B) Shortness of breath when lying flat
C) Peripheral edema in the lower extremities
D) Crackles auscultated in the bilateral lung bases

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Peripheral edema in the lower extremities

**Rationale:** Right-sided heart failure causes systemic venous congestion because the right ventricle cannot effectively pump blood forward into the lungs. This leads to an expected backup of fluid into the body, causing peripheral edema (swelling in the legs and ankles), jugular venous distention, and hepatomegaly. Pink, frothy sputum, orthopnea (shortness of breath when lying flat), and lung crackles are classic signs of left-sided heart failure due to fluid backing up into the pulmonary system.
</details>

---

### Question 100
**Lens:** Procedural Step
A nurse is preparing to perform a standard 12-lead electrocardiogram (EKG) on a client experiencing mild chest discomfort. Which action represents the correct procedural step for placing the V1 chest lead?

A) Place it at the fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border.
B) Place it at the fifth intercostal space at the midclavicular line.
C) Place it at the fourth intercostal space at the left sternal border.
D) Place it at the fifth intercostal space at the anterior axillary line.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Place it at the fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border.

**Rationale:** The correct placement for the V1 lead in a standard 12-lead EKG is the fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border. Proper lead placement is essential for an accurate EKG reading. V2 is placed at the fourth intercostal space at the left sternal border. V4 is placed at the fifth intercostal space at the midclavicular line. V5 is placed at the fifth intercostal space at the anterior axillary line.
</details>
### Question 101
A nurse is reviewing a normal electrocardiogram (EKG) rhythm strip for a healthy adult client during a routine physical. What should the nurse identify as the expected representation of atrial depolarization?

A) The QRS complex  
B) The T wave  
C) The P wave  
D) The ST segment  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The P wave represents atrial depolarization, which is the electrical signal causing the atria to contract. The QRS complex represents ventricular depolarization, and the T wave represents ventricular repolarization. Recognizing the normal components of an EKG is a basic and essential nursing skill.
</details>

### Question 102
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client recently diagnosed with heart failure. Which instruction is most important for the nurse to include regarding daily monitoring at home?

A) Weigh yourself every morning after voiding and before eating.  
B) Measure your pulse rate once a week before breakfast.  
C) Drink at least 3 liters of water every day to flush the kidneys.  
D) Check your blood pressure only when you feel dizzy or lightheaded.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Daily weights are the most reliable indicator of fluid retention and volume status in clients with heart failure. Clients should be taught to weigh themselves at the same time every day, usually in the morning after voiding and before eating, wearing the same amount of clothing. Encouraging high fluid intake (3 liters) is contraindicated in heart failure due to the risk of fluid overload.
</details>

---

### Question 103
A nurse is preparing to measure the blood pressure of a client diagnosed with hypertension. Which action is the correct procedural step to ensure an accurate and safe reading?

A) Have the client cross their legs while sitting in the chair.  
B) Position the client's arm so it is resting supported at heart level.  
C) Place the blood pressure cuff directly over the client's thick sweater.  
D) Deflate the blood pressure cuff as rapidly as possible to prevent discomfort.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> To obtain an accurate blood pressure reading, the client's arm should be supported and resting at heart level. If the arm is below heart level, the reading may be falsely high. The client should keep their feet flat on the floor (uncrossed), the cuff should be placed on a bare arm, and it should be deflated slowly (2-3 mm Hg per second).
</details>

### Question 104
A nurse is collecting data from a client admitted with an exacerbation of right-sided heart failure. Which clinical finding should the nurse interpret as a classic sign of this condition?

A) Crackles in the lung bases  
B) Persistent dry cough  
C) Peripheral pitting edema  
D) Shortness of breath when lying flat  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Data Interpretation<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Peripheral pitting edema is a classic sign of right-sided heart failure. When the right side of the heart fails, it cannot effectively pump blood into the lungs, causing fluid to back up into the systemic venous circulation, resulting in dependent edema, ascites, and jugular venous distention. Crackles, a dry cough, and orthopnea (shortness of breath when lying flat) are classic signs of left-sided heart failure due to pulmonary congestion.
</details>

---

### Question 105
A registered nurse (RN) is planning care for a stable client who is receiving oral medication for primary hypertension. Which task is appropriate for the RN to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assess the client for side effects of the newly prescribed blood pressure medication.  
B) Explain the purpose of a low-sodium diet to the client.  
C) Re-evaluate the client's blood pressure 30 minutes after medication administration.  
D) Obtain the client's routine morning blood pressure and record the reading.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Obtaining routine vital signs, such as a morning blood pressure on a stable client, is a standard, predictable task that is within the scope of practice for a UAP. Assessing for side effects, teaching about a low-sodium diet, and evaluating the effectiveness of a medication are all nursing responsibilities that require clinical judgment and cannot be delegated to UAP.
</details>
### Question 106
**Lens: Client Teaching**
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly diagnosed with primary hypertension. Which statement by the client indicates an accurate understanding of dietary safety instructions?

A) "I can eat canned soups as long as I do not add extra table salt."
B) "I will limit my daily sodium intake to 2,000 milligrams or less."
C) "I should avoid eating fresh fruits and vegetables to lower my blood pressure."
D) "I need to increase my intake of red meat for extra protein."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** A key component of managing primary hypertension is following a low-sodium diet, typically restricting intake to 2,000 mg or less per day to help lower blood pressure and prevent fluid retention. Option A is incorrect because canned soups are inherently high in sodium, even if extra table salt is not added. Option C is incorrect as the Dietary Approaches to Stop Hypertension (DASH) diet highly encourages the consumption of fresh fruits and vegetables. Option D is incorrect because clients with hypertension should choose lean proteins (like poultry and fish) rather than increasing red meat, which can be high in saturated fats.
</details>

---

### Question 107
**Lens: Data Interpretation**
The nurse is reviewing the electrocardiogram (EKG) of a healthy adult client during a routine physical examination. Which finding should the nurse document as a normal physiological characteristic?

A) An inverted T wave following every QRS complex.
B) A QRS complex duration of 0.24 seconds.
C) A PR interval measuring 0.16 seconds.
D) Absent P waves before the QRS complexes.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** A normal PR interval measures between 0.12 and 0.20 seconds, representing the time it takes for the electrical impulse to travel from the SA node to the AV node. Therefore, a PR interval of 0.16 seconds is a normal finding. Option A is incorrect because T waves should normally be upright; an inverted T wave can indicate ischemia. Option B is incorrect because a normal QRS complex lasts between 0.06 and 0.12 seconds; a duration of 0.24 seconds is prolonged. Option D is incorrect because a normal sinus rhythm requires a visible, upright P wave preceding every QRS complex.
</details>

---

### Question 108
**Lens: Expected vs Unexpected**
A client with a history of right-sided heart failure is being assessed at the outpatient clinic. Which assessment finding would the nurse expect as a typical manifestation of this specific condition?

A) Bilateral crackles auscultated in the lung bases.
B) Severe shortness of breath when lying flat in bed.
C) Frothy, pink-tinged sputum with a persistent cough.
D) Significant pitting edema in the lower extremities.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Right-sided heart failure leads to systemic venous congestion because the right ventricle cannot effectively pump blood into the lungs. This backup of fluid causes systemic signs such as peripheral pitting edema, jugular venous distention (JVD), and hepatomegaly. Options A, B, and C (crackles, orthopnea, and frothy pink sputum) are classic signs of *left-sided* heart failure, which causes fluid to back up into the pulmonary system.
</details>

### Question 109
**Lens: Delegation**
The registered nurse (RN) is caring for a client with primary hypertension who is receiving a new antihypertensive medication. Which task is most appropriate for the RN to safely delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Obtaining the client's morning sitting and standing blood pressures.
B) Evaluating the client's understanding of orthostatic hypotension.
C) Assessing the client for dizziness when changing positions.
D) Administering the client's scheduled oral blood pressure pill.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** UAPs are trained to perform standard, non-invasive vital sign measurements, including obtaining sitting and standing blood pressures (orthostatic vitals). Option B is incorrect because evaluating a client's understanding is an evaluation step of the nursing process, which cannot be delegated. Option C is incorrect because assessing a client for symptoms requires nursing judgment. Option D is incorrect because medication administration cannot be delegated to a UAP; it requires a licensed nurse (RN or LPN/LVN).
</details>

---

### Question 110
**Lens: Evaluation**
A client with left-sided heart failure has been receiving intravenous diuretic therapy for the past two days. Which clinical finding best indicates to the nurse that the treatment has been effective?

A) The client exhibits clear lung sounds bilaterally upon auscultation.
B) The client reports a daily weight gain of two pounds.
C) The client's resting heart rate increases to 115 beats per minute.
D) The client states that they are urinating much less frequently.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Left-sided heart failure causes pulmonary congestion, often presenting as crackles in the lungs. Diuretics are given to remove excess fluid from the body. Clear lung sounds indicate a reduction in pulmonary edema, demonstrating that the diuretic therapy is effective. Option B is incorrect because weight gain indicates worsening fluid retention, not effective treatment. Option C is incorrect as tachycardia can be a sign of worsening heart failure or dehydration. Option D is incorrect because effective diuretic therapy should increase, not decrease, urine output.
</details>
### Question 111
**Lens: Client Teaching**
**Focus: Hypertension Safety**

A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly diagnosed with primary hypertension. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of dietary safety modifications?

A. "I should start using salt substitutes that are high in potassium instead of regular salt."
B. "I need to eliminate all dairy products from my diet immediately."
C. "I will limit my daily sodium intake to less than 2,000 milligrams."
D. "I can eat as much canned soup as I want as long as I dilute it with water."

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. "I will limit my daily sodium intake to less than 2,000 milligrams."

**Rationale:** 
A fundamental safety and lifestyle modification for clients with hypertension is limiting sodium intake to help control blood pressure (typically recommended to be under 2,000 or 2,300 mg/day depending on the provider). Canned soups are notoriously high in sodium and should be avoided or limited. Salt substitutes often contain high levels of potassium, which can be dangerous if the client is prescribed potassium-sparing diuretics, ACE inhibitors, or ARBs. Dairy products do not need to be eliminated; in fact, the DASH diet encourages the consumption of low-fat dairy.
</details>

---

### Question 112
**Lens: Data Interpretation**
**Focus: Normal EKG**

A nurse is reviewing a baseline resting electrocardiogram (EKG) strip for a healthy adult client. Which finding should the nurse interpret as a characteristic of a normal sinus rhythm?

A. The presence of a P wave before every QRS complex.
B. A resting heart rate of 115 beats per minute.
C. A PR interval measuring 0.30 seconds.
D. An irregular distance between R-R intervals.

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. The presence of a P wave before every QRS complex.

**Rationale:** 
In a normal sinus rhythm, electrical impulses originate in the SA node, which is represented by one P wave preceding every QRS complex. A normal resting heart rate is between 60 and 100 beats per minute (115 bpm is sinus tachycardia). A normal PR interval is between 0.12 and 0.20 seconds (0.30 seconds indicates a first-degree heart block). A normal sinus rhythm has a regular, even distance between R-R intervals.
</details>

---

### Question 113
**Lens: Expected vs Unexpected**
**Focus: Basic Heart Failure**

A nurse is performing a routine assessment on a client with a known history of right-sided heart failure. Which of the following assessment findings is an expected manifestation of this specific condition?

A. Crackles auscultated in the lung bases
B. A persistent cough with pink, frothy sputum
C. The client reporting shortness of breath when lying flat
D. Pitting edema in the lower extremities

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. Pitting edema in the lower extremities

**Rationale:** 
Right-sided heart failure causes blood to back up into the systemic circulation. Expected manifestations include peripheral edema (such as pitting edema in the legs and ankles), jugular venous distention (JVD), and ascites. Crackles in the lungs, pink frothy sputum, and orthopnea (shortness of breath when lying flat) are classic, expected signs of left-sided heart failure, which causes pulmonary congestion.
</details>

### Question 114
**Lens: Delegation**
**Focus: Hypertension Safety**

A registered nurse (RN) is caring for a stable client who was admitted for hypertension management. Which of the following tasks is safest and most appropriate to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Assessing the client for dizziness when standing up.
B. Obtaining the client's morning blood pressure using an automated cuff.
C. Explaining the common side effects of the client's new blood pressure medication.
D. Evaluating if the client's blood pressure medication has been effective.

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Obtaining the client's morning blood pressure using an automated cuff.

**Rationale:** 
Unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) are trained to perform standard, routine tasks on stable clients, such as measuring vital signs like blood pressure. Assessing for orthostatic hypotension, providing client education, and evaluating the effectiveness of a pharmacological intervention all require the specialized training and clinical judgment of a registered nurse and cannot be delegated.
</details>

### Question 115
**Lens: Procedural Step**
**Focus: Normal EKG**

A nurse is preparing to perform a standard 12-lead EKG on a client in the telemetry unit. What is the correct procedural step the nurse must take to ensure an accurate reading?

A. Ask the client to hold their breath for 30 seconds during the recording.
B. Instruct the client to sit upright in a chair while the EKG is recorded.
C. Apply a moisturizing lotion to the client's chest to help the electrodes adhere.
D. Place the V1 electrode in the fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border.

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. Place the V1 electrode in the fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border.

**Rationale:** 
Proper lead placement is crucial for an accurate 12-lead EKG. The correct anatomical placement for the V1 electrode is the fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border. The client should be instructed to breathe normally (not hold their breath) and lie in a supine or semi-Fowler's position, not sit upright in a chair. The skin should be clean, dry, and free of lotions, as lotions and oils prevent the electrodes from adhering properly and can disrupt the electrical signal.
</details>
### Question 116
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
**Scenario:** The nurse is reviewing the electrocardiogram (EKG) tracing of a healthy adult client resting in bed. Which finding should the nurse document as an expected, normal finding?

A) Heart rate of 45 beats per minute
B) Heart rate of 75 beats per minute
C) Absent P waves before the QRS complexes
D) Irregularly irregular ventricular rhythm

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
A normal sinus rhythm has a heart rate between 60 and 100 beats per minute, a P wave preceding every QRS complex, and a regular rhythm. A heart rate of 75 beats per minute falls perfectly within this expected normal range. A rate of 45 is bradycardia, absent P waves or irregularly irregular rhythms indicate abnormal arrhythmias such as atrial fibrillation.
</details>

---

### Question 117
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
**Scenario:** The nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client recently diagnosed with basic heart failure. Which instruction regarding daily weight monitoring should the nurse include?

A) "Weigh yourself once a week on Sunday evenings before dinner."
B) "Use a different scale each time to ensure the readings are generally accurate."
C) "Weigh yourself at the same time every day, preferably in the morning after voiding."
D) "Weigh yourself fully clothed with your shoes on to account for daily activities."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Clients with heart failure should weigh themselves daily to monitor for fluid retention. To obtain the most accurate trend, they should weigh themselves at the same time each day (usually in the morning after voiding and before eating), wearing similar clothing, and using the exact same scale. 
</details>

---

### Question 118
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
**Scenario:** The nurse is preparing to measure a client's blood pressure during a routine hypertension clinic visit. What is the correct procedural step to ensure an accurate reading?

A) Keep the client's arm unsupported and resting freely at their side.
B) Apply the blood pressure cuff directly over the client's thick sweater.
C) Ensure the client has consumed a caffeinated beverage within the last 15 minutes.
D) Ask the client to keep their feet flat on the floor with their legs uncrossed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
To obtain an accurate blood pressure reading, the client should be seated quietly in a chair with both feet flat on the floor and legs uncrossed. The arm should be supported at heart level, the cuff should be placed on a bare arm, and caffeine or smoking should be avoided for at least 30 minutes prior to the measurement.
</details>

---

### Question 119
**Lens:** DELEGATION
**Scenario:** The registered nurse (RN) is caring for a stable client admitted with mild heart failure. Which task is most appropriate for the RN to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Recording the client's strict intake and output (I&O) totals for the shift.
B) Assessing the client's lung sounds for the presence of new crackles.
C) Evaluating the effectiveness of the client's morning diuretic medication.
D) Educating the client on the importance of a low-sodium diet plan.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) can safely perform standard, routine tasks such as measuring and recording intake and output, obtaining daily weights, and taking vital signs on stable clients. Assessing lung sounds, evaluating medication effectiveness, and providing dietary education require nursing judgment and are strictly within the scope of the RN.
</details>

---

### Question 120
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
**Scenario:** A client with hypertension has just been prescribed a new daily antihypertensive medication. Which statement by the client indicates a safety risk that the nurse must address?

A) "I will avoid adding extra table salt to my meals."
B) "I usually jump out of bed quickly as soon as my alarm goes off."
C) "I plan to check my blood pressure at home every single day."
D) "I know I should drink plenty of water throughout the day."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Antihypertensive medications often cause orthostatic (postural) hypotension, which is a sudden drop in blood pressure when changing positions. Jumping out of bed quickly puts the client at a high risk for dizziness, syncope, and falls. The nurse must teach the client to rise slowly and dangle their legs on the side of the bed before standing.
</details>
### Question 121
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
The nurse is reviewing a client's normal electrocardiogram (EKG) rhythm strip. The nurse notes a normal, upright P wave occurring before every QRS complex. What physiological event in the heart does the P wave indicate?

A. Ventricular depolarization
B. Ventricular repolarization
C. Atrial depolarization
D. Atrial repolarization

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** On a normal EKG, the P wave represents atrial depolarization, which is the electrical signal spreading through the atria causing them to contract. The QRS complex represents ventricular depolarization, and the T wave represents ventricular repolarization. Atrial repolarization is hidden within the QRS complex. 
</details>

---

### Question 122
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
A client is being evaluated in the clinic for chronic right-sided heart failure. Which clinical finding should the nurse expect to observe during the physical assessment?

A. Bilateral pitting edema in the lower extremities
B. Crackles auscultated in the lung bases
C. A cough producing pink, frothy sputum
D. Severe shortness of breath when lying flat

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Right-sided heart failure prevents the heart from effectively pumping blood into the lungs, causing fluid to back up into the systemic venous circulation. Expected findings include peripheral edema (such as bilateral pitting edema in the legs), jugular vein distention, and hepatomegaly. Crackles, pink frothy sputum, and orthopnea (shortness of breath when lying flat) are signs of fluid backing up into the lungs, which are expected findings for left-sided heart failure.
</details>

---

### Question 123
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
A client newly diagnosed with primary hypertension is prescribed a daily oral antihypertensive medication. Which instruction should the nurse include in the teaching plan to promote client safety?

A. "Take the medication with a full glass of grapefruit juice."
B. "Change positions slowly from lying down to standing up."
C. "Stop taking the medication as soon as your blood pressure is normal."
D. "Increase your daily intake of sodium to balance out the medication."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Antihypertensive medications commonly cause orthostatic (postural) hypotension, which can lead to dizziness and falls. Teaching the client to change positions slowly promotes safety. Grapefruit juice is contraindicated with many cardiovascular medications. Antihypertensives should never be stopped abruptly, even if blood pressure normalizes, as this can cause rebound hypertension. Clients with hypertension should decrease, not increase, their sodium intake.
</details>

---

### Question 124
**Lens:** DELEGATION
The nurse is planning morning care for a client admitted with a mild exacerbation of heart failure. Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A. Assess the client's lung sounds for crackles or wheezes.
B. Evaluate the client's urine output response to an administered diuretic.
C. Teach the client about the importance of a low-sodium diet.
D. Weigh the client on the standing scale at the same time each morning.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Daily weights are a routine, standardized procedure used to monitor fluid retention in heart failure clients and can be safely delegated to the UAP. Assessing lung sounds, evaluating therapeutic responses to medications, and providing client education all require the clinical judgment and specialized knowledge of a registered nurse and cannot be delegated.
</details>

---

### Question 125
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
The nurse is preparing to perform a routine 12-lead electrocardiogram (EKG) on a resting client. Which step should the nurse take to ensure proper electrode adhesion and an accurate reading?

A. Apply a moisturizing lotion to the client's chest to soothe the skin.
B. Clean the electrode sites with an alcohol swab and let them dry completely.
C. Place the chest electrodes directly over the client's hospital gown.
D. Instruct the client to take deep, rapid breaths during the recording.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** To obtain a clear and accurate EKG tracing, the skin must be clean and dry. Using an alcohol swab removes skin oils and debris, allowing the electrodes to adhere properly. Applying lotion will prevent the electrodes from sticking. Electrodes must be placed directly on the skin, not over clothing. The client should be instructed to lie still and breathe normally during the test, as deep or rapid breathing can create artifact on the tracing.
</details>

---
### Question 126
A nurse is teaching a client who was recently prescribed a daily oral antihypertensive medication. To ensure client safety, which instruction should the nurse include?

A) "Stop taking the medication immediately if you experience a mild headache."
B) "Change positions slowly from lying to sitting or standing."
C) "Double your dose if your blood pressure is slightly higher than normal."
D) "Restrict your daily fluid intake to less than one liter."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Client Teaching
**System Focus:** Hypertension Safety

Antihypertensive medications can cause orthostatic hypotension, a sudden drop in blood pressure when changing positions. Changing positions slowly allows the cardiovascular system time to adjust, preventing dizziness and falls, which is a key safety priority. Clients should never abruptly stop their medication (Option A) or adjust their own doses (Option C) without consulting a healthcare provider. Fluid restriction (Option D) is not a routine safety measure for basic hypertension without concurrent severe heart failure or kidney issues.
</details>

### Question 127
A nurse is assessing a client diagnosed with right-sided heart failure. Which clinical manifestation is an expected finding for this specific condition?

A) Crackles auscultated in the lung bases
B) A dry, persistent, hacking cough
C) Pitting edema in the bilateral lower extremities
D) Pink, frothy sputum production

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**System Focus:** Basic Heart Failure

Right-sided heart failure occurs when the right ventricle fails to pump effectively, causing blood to back up into the systemic venous system. This leads to expected findings such as peripheral pitting edema, ascites, and jugular venous distention. Crackles (Option A), a persistent cough (Option B), and pink, frothy sputum (Option D) are expected findings of *left-sided* heart failure, where fluid backs up into the lungs.
</details>

### Question 128
A nurse is reviewing an adult client's resting electrocardiogram (EKG) rhythm strip. The nurse notes a regular rhythm, a heart rate of 75 beats per minute, and a rounded P wave occurring right before every narrow QRS complex. How should the nurse interpret this data?

A) Normal sinus rhythm
B) Ventricular fibrillation
C) Sinus bradycardia
D) Atrial flutter

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
**System Focus:** Normal EKG

A normal sinus rhythm originates in the sinoatrial (SA) node and is characterized by a heart rate between 60 and 100 beats per minute, a regular rhythm, and a P wave preceding every QRS complex. The data provided perfectly matches these normal parameters. Ventricular fibrillation (Option B) is a chaotic, lethal rhythm. Sinus bradycardia (Option C) would present with a heart rate of less than 60 beats per minute. Atrial flutter (Option D) is characterized by a "sawtooth" pattern of P waves, not single rounded P waves before each QRS.
</details>

### Question 129
A registered nurse (RN) is managing the care of a stable client with a history of primary hypertension. Which task is most appropriate to safely delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Recommending over-the-counter pain relievers that do not elevate blood pressure
B) Assessing the client for signs of a hypertensive emergency
C) Teaching the client about the benefits of a low-sodium diet
D) Measuring and recording the client's routine morning vital signs

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Delegation
**System Focus:** Hypertension Safety

Measuring and recording routine vital signs, including blood pressure, is a standard task within the scope of practice for UAP caring for stable clients. Recommending medications (Option A), performing nursing assessments (Option B), and providing client education (Option C) all require nursing judgment and fall outside the scope of practice for a UAP; these must be performed by the RN.
</details>

### Question 130
A nurse is preparing to perform a routine 12-lead electrocardiogram (EKG) on a client experiencing mild palpitations. Which procedural step is correct for the nurse to take?

A) Place all six precordial (chest) leads on the right side of the sternum.
B) Inform the client that they will feel a mild shock during the test.
C) Ensure the client's skin is clean and dry before applying the electrodes.
D) Instruct the client to hold their breath continuously while the machine records.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Procedural Step
**System Focus:** Normal EKG

To obtain an accurate, artifact-free EKG tracing, the nurse must ensure the client's skin is clean, dry, and free of excessive oils or hair before applying the adhesive electrodes. Precordial leads are primarily placed on the left side of the chest, not the right (Option A). An EKG simply records the electrical activity of the heart; it does not deliver a shock (Option B). The client should be instructed to lie still and breathe normally, not hold their breath (Option D).
</details>
### Question 131
The nurse is providing discharge instructions to a client newly prescribed an antihypertensive medication. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching regarding safety?

A. "I should stop taking this medication if I feel dizzy."
B. "I will limit my fluid intake to one liter per day."
C. "I need to rise slowly from a sitting or lying position."
D. "I can skip a dose if my blood pressure is normal."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Lens:** Client Teaching  
Orthostatic hypotension is a common side effect of many antihypertensive medications. Changing positions slowly helps prevent dizziness and falls, ensuring client safety. Clients should never abruptly stop their medication or skip doses without consulting the healthcare provider. Fluid restriction is not a standard requirement for all antihypertensives unless specifically prescribed for a comorbidity.
</details>

---

### Question 132
The nurse is reviewing a normal electrocardiogram (EKG) rhythm strip for a healthy adult client. When analyzing the P wave on the tracing, the nurse understands that this waveform represents which physiological event?

A. Atrial depolarization
B. Ventricular depolarization
C. Atrial repolarization
D. Ventricular repolarization

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Lens:** Data Interpretation  
On a normal EKG, the P wave represents atrial depolarization, which is initiated by the firing of the sinoatrial (SA) node. Ventricular depolarization is represented by the QRS complex, and ventricular repolarization is represented by the T wave.
</details>

---

### Question 133
The nurse is assessing a client with a history of right-sided heart failure. Which clinical finding is an expected manifestation of this condition?

A. Persistent dry cough
B. Crackles in the lung bases
C. Pink, frothy sputum
D. Jugular vein distension

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected  
Right-sided heart failure causes systemic venous congestion, leading to expected findings such as jugular vein distension (JVD), peripheral edema, and hepatomegaly. Crackles, a persistent cough, and pink frothy sputum are unexpected in isolated right-sided failure, as they are classic manifestations of left-sided heart failure due to pulmonary congestion.
</details>

---

### Question 134
The registered nurse (RN) is caring for a client with a history of essential hypertension. Which task is most appropriate for the RN to safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Educating the client on a low-sodium diet plan.
B. Obtaining a routine morning blood pressure reading.
C. Assessing the client for signs of medication-induced dizziness.
D. Recommending an over-the-counter pain reliever that is safe for blood pressure.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Lens:** Delegation  
Obtaining routine vital signs, such as a morning blood pressure on a stable client, is within the scope of practice for the UAP. Teaching, assessing, and recommending medications require nursing judgment and must strictly be performed by the RN.
</details>

---

### Question 135
A client with heart failure has been receiving a daily loop diuretic to manage fluid overload. Which assessment finding best indicates to the nurse that the therapeutic intervention is effective?

A. The client reports a decrease in daily urination.
B. The client's blood pressure increased from baseline.
C. The client's daily morning weight decreased by 2 pounds.
D. The client demonstrates 3+ pitting edema in the lower extremities.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Lens:** Evaluation  
Daily weight is the most reliable indicator of fluid status in clients with heart failure. A decrease in weight indicates effective fluid removal by the diuretic. An increase in blood pressure, decreased urination, and significant pitting edema indicate worsening or unresolved fluid retention.
</details>
### Question 136
A nurse is reviewing a routine electrocardiogram (EKG) for a healthy young adult client. The tracing shows a normal sinus rhythm. While analyzing the complex, the nurse identifies the P wave. Based on basic cardiac electrophysiology, what does the P wave represent?

A) Ventricular repolarization  
B) Ventricular depolarization  
C) Atrial depolarization  
D) Atrial repolarization  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
<b>Lens:</b> Data Interpretation<br>
In a normal electrocardiogram (EKG), the P wave represents atrial depolarization, which is the electrical impulse that spreads through the atria and triggers their contraction. Ventricular depolarization is represented by the QRS complex, while ventricular repolarization is represented by the T wave. Atrial repolarization occurs simultaneously with ventricular depolarization and is hidden within the QRS complex.
</details>

---

### Question 137
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client newly diagnosed with hypertension who has been prescribed a daily antihypertensive medication. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of basic safety precautions?

A) "I should stop taking the medication if my blood pressure reads normal."  
B) "I will sit on the edge of the bed for a few minutes before standing up."  
C) "I can stop checking my blood pressure at home since I am on medication."  
D) "I need to double my dose if I miss a day to keep my blood pressure steady."  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
<b>Lens:</b> Client Teaching<br>
Antihypertensive medications can cause orthostatic (postural) hypotension, which places the client at a high risk for dizziness and falls. Sitting on the edge of the bed before standing (dangling) allows the cardiovascular system time to adjust to position changes, promoting safety. The client should never abruptly stop the medication, double a missed dose, or stop monitoring their blood pressure.
</details>

---

### Question 138
A nurse is assessing a client with a known history of right-sided heart failure. Which clinical finding is an expected manifestation of this specific condition?

A) Crackles in the lung bases  
B) Pink, frothy sputum  
C) Orthopnea  
D) Bilateral pitting pedal edema  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
<b>Lens:</b> Expected VS Unexpected<br>
Right-sided heart failure causes blood to back up into the systemic venous circulation. This results in expected systemic manifestations such as bilateral pitting pedal edema, ascites, jugular venous distention (JVD), and hepatomegaly. Crackles, pink frothy sputum, and orthopnea are unexpected for isolated right-sided failure, as they are classic signs of left-sided heart failure (pulmonary congestion).
</details>

---

### Question 139
A client with heart failure is receiving a daily loop diuretic to manage fluid retention. Which assessment finding best indicates to the nurse that the therapeutic intervention is effective?

A) The client has lost 2 pounds over the last 24 hours.  
B) The client's resting heart rate is 105 beats per minute.  
C) The client's urine output is consistently 20 mL per hour.  
D) The client reports a persistent dry cough at night.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
<b>Lens:</b> Evaluation<br>
The primary goal of diuretic therapy in heart failure is to reduce fluid overload. Daily weight is the most reliable indicator of fluid volume status. A weight loss of 2 pounds in 24 hours indicates effective diuresis (removal of approximately 1 liter of excess fluid). Tachycardia, oliguria (20 mL/hr), and a persistent cough indicate worsening heart failure or fluid retention, not an effective response to the medication.
</details>

---

### Question 140
The nurse is preparing to perform a routine 12-lead electrocardiogram (EKG) on a healthy client. When placing the precordial chest leads, where should the nurse position the V4 electrode?

A) Fourth intercostal space at the left sternal border  
B) Fifth intercostal space at the anterior axillary line  
C) Fourth intercostal space at the right sternal border  
D) Fifth intercostal space at the midclavicular line  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
<b>Lens:</b> Procedural Step<br>
When placing a 12-lead EKG, accurate electrode positioning is vital for a normal and correct tracing. The V4 electrode is correctly placed at the fifth intercostal space, at the left midclavicular line. V1 and V2 are placed at the fourth intercostal space at the right and left sternal borders, respectively. V5 is placed at the fifth intercostal space at the anterior axillary line.
</details>
