Drinking water, maintaining a low flow rate, and posting a "No Smoking" sign are all safe and expected actions for a client on oxygen.
</details>

---

### Question 285
A client with asthma has been prescribed an inhaled corticosteroid. During a follow-up visit, the nurse observes the client's post-administration routine. Which action by the client indicates that the therapeutic goals for preventing medication side effects have been met?

A. The client spits out the remaining medication immediately without closing their mouth.
B. The client blows their nose forcefully after using the inhaler to clear their airway.
C. The client takes a large drink of cold milk to soothe their throat.
D. The client rinses their mouth with water and spits it out into the sink.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** 
**Evaluation:** Rinsing the mouth with water and spitting it out after using an inhaled corticosteroid is essential to prevent oral candidiasis (thrush), a common local side effect of this medication. Observing the client performing this action correctly demonstrates that they have met the learning goal for self-care and prevention of complications. Blowing the nose, spitting out the medication immediately, or drinking milk do not prevent oral thrush.
</details>
### Question 286
A client is being discharged with home oxygen therapy. Which client statement indicates a need for further teaching regarding oxygen safety?

A) "I will put a 'No Smoking' sign on my front door."
B) "I will use 100% cotton blankets on my bed."
C) "I will apply petroleum jelly to my lips if they get dry."
D) "I will keep my oxygen equipment at least 5 feet away from the stove."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** Petroleum jelly is an oil-based, combustible product that should never be used on the face or inside the nares of a client using oxygen, as it increases the risk of burns and fire. The client should be taught to use water-based lubricants instead. Using cotton blankets (which do not create static electricity), placing "No Smoking" signs, and keeping oxygen away from heat sources like stoves are all correct and safe practices. 

**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
**Cognitive Level:** Application
**NCLEX Client Need:** Safety and Infection Control

</details>

---

### Question 287
The nurse is demonstrating how to use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) with a spacer. Which procedural step should the nurse instruct the client to perform right before activating the inhaler?

A) Exhale completely to empty the lungs.
B) Inhale rapidly and forcefully from the mouthpiece.
C) Press the canister down multiple times in a row.
D) Hold their breath for exactly thirty seconds.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** Before activating the inhaler, the client should exhale completely to empty their lungs. This prepares the lungs to take a slow, deep breath, ensuring maximum medication delivery deep into the lower respiratory tract. Inhaling should be slow and deep, not rapid. The canister should only be pressed once per breath, and the breath should typically be held for about 10 seconds, not 30.

**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
**Cognitive Level:** Application
**NCLEX Client Need:** Pharmacological and Parenteral Therapies

</details>

---

### Question 288
A client with bacterial pneumonia is placed on droplet precautions. Which task can the registered nurse (RN) safely delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client's lung sounds for crackles.
B) Restocking the isolation cart outside the room with surgical masks.
C) Teaching the client how to cough and deep breathe effectively.
D) Evaluating the client's oxygen saturation levels during ambulation.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Restocking supplies, such as surgical masks on an isolation cart, is a routine task that does not require nursing judgment and is safely within the scope of practice for a UAP. Assessing lung sounds, teaching breathing techniques, and evaluating a client's physiological response to activity are all tasks that require nursing judgment and must be performed by the RN.

**Lens:** DELEGATION
**Cognitive Level:** Application
**NCLEX Client Need:** Management of Care

</details>

---

### Question 289
The nurse is evaluating a client's understanding of how to use a dry powder inhaler (DPI) containing a corticosteroid. Which action by the client indicates that the teaching has been effective?

A) The client shakes the DPI vigorously for one minute before use.
B) The client breathes out forcefully directly into the mouthpiece.
C) The client washes the DPI device with soap and water after each dose.
D) The client rinses their mouth with water and spits it out after using the inhaler.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** Rinsing the mouth with water and spitting it out after using an inhaled corticosteroid is essential to prevent oral candidiasis (thrush). DPIs do not require shaking, unlike metered-dose inhalers (MDIs). The client should never exhale into the DPI, as the moisture from the breath can cause the powder to clump. DPIs should generally not be washed with soap and water; the mouthpiece should just be wiped clean with a dry cloth.

**Lens:** EVALUATION
**Cognitive Level:** Application
**NCLEX Client Need:** Pharmacological and Parenteral Therapies

</details>

---

### Question 290
A nurse observes the equipment for a client receiving continuous oxygen therapy via a nasal cannula with a humidifier. The nurse notes continuous bubbling in the humidifier bottle. How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A) Bubbling indicates the humidifier is working properly to moisten the oxygen.
B) Bubbling means the oxygen flow rate is set dangerously high.
C) Bubbling shows there is a leak in the oxygen tubing connections.
D) Bubbling means the humidifier is overfilled and needs to be emptied immediately.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** Bubbling in an oxygen humidifier bottle is a normal and expected finding. It demonstrates that the dry oxygen gas from the wall or tank is passing through the sterile water, which moistens the air before it is delivered to the client's airways, preventing drying of the mucous membranes. It does not indicate a leak, overfilling, or an excessively high flow rate.

**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION
**Cognitive Level:** Comprehension
**NCLEX Client Need:** Basic Care and Comfort

</details>
### Question 291
**Lens:** Risk Recognition
A home care nurse is evaluating the living environment of a client who is prescribed continuous oxygen therapy via nasal cannula. Which observation by the nurse indicates a safety risk?

A. The client applies petroleum jelly to their nares to soothe dryness. 
B. The client uses 100% cotton blankets and sheets on their bed. 
C. The client stores extra oxygen cylinders in a secured, upright position. 
D. The client places the oxygen concentrator 10 feet away from the space heater. 

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A 
**Rationale:** Petroleum jelly is a combustible, oil-based product. When combined with supplemental oxygen, which aggressively supports combustion, it creates a severe fire hazard. A water-soluble lubricant should be used instead. 
- Option B is an expected, safe action. Cotton is preferred because synthetic materials (like nylon or wool) can generate static electricity, causing sparks.
- Option C is an expected, safe action. Oxygen cylinders should be secured upright so they do not fall and rupture.
- Option D is an expected, safe action. Oxygen equipment should be kept well away (at least 5 to 10 feet) from open flames or heat sources.
</details>

---

### Question 292
**Lens:** Procedural Step
A nurse is observing a client self-administer a medication using a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. Which action by the client indicates the correct procedural technique?

A. Pressing the canister down down while exhaling completely.
B. Waiting 15 seconds before administering a second puff of the medication.
C. Holding their breath for 10 seconds after inhaling the medication.
D. Inhaling rapidly and shallowly through the mouth while pressing the canister.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** After inhaling the medication, the client should hold their breath for at least 10 seconds (or as long as comfortable) to allow the medication to settle deeply into the airways.
- Option A is incorrect. The client should exhale completely *before* pressing the canister, and then press the canister while inhaling slowly and deeply.
- Option B is incorrect. The client should wait at least 1 minute (60 seconds) between puffs of the same medication to allow the first dose to take effect and open the airways.
- Option D is incorrect. The inhalation should be slow and deep, not rapid and shallow, to ensure the medication reaches the lower airways.
</details>

---

### Question 293
**Lens:** Delegation
A nurse is caring for a client admitted with bacterial pneumonia who is placed on droplet precautions. Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A. Assessing the client's lung sounds for crackles or wheezes.
B. Providing fresh water to the client while wearing a surgical mask.
C. Adjusting the client's nasal cannula flow rate based on pulse oximetry.
D. Instructing the client's family on how to properly wear a surgical mask.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Providing fresh water is a standard, routine task within the UAP's scope of practice. The UAP can safely perform this task while adhering to droplet precautions by wearing a surgical mask.
- Option A is incorrect. Assessing lung sounds is a nursing assessment that cannot be delegated to a UAP.
- Option C is incorrect. Adjusting oxygen flow rates involves clinical judgment and administering a medical treatment, which cannot be delegated to a UAP.
- Option D is incorrect. Client and family education is the responsibility of the registered nurse and cannot be delegated.
</details>

---

### Question 294
**Lens:** Client Teaching
A nurse is providing discharge instructions to a client newly prescribed a fluticasone dry powder inhaler (DPI) for asthma maintenance. Which instruction should the nurse include in the teaching?

A. "Use this inhaler immediately if you experience sudden shortness of breath."
B. "Exhale directly into the mouthpiece before inhaling the medication."
C. "Shake the inhaler vigorously for 30 seconds before every use."
D. "Rinse your mouth with water and spit it out after using the inhaler."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Fluticasone is an inhaled corticosteroid. Rinsing the mouth and spitting out the water after use prevents the development of oral candidiasis (thrush), a common localized side effect of inhaled steroids.
- Option A is incorrect. Fluticasone is a maintenance controller medication, not a rescue inhaler. It should not be used for acute asthma attacks.
- Option B is incorrect. Clients should never exhale directly into a dry powder inhaler, as the moisture from the breath can cause the powder to clump and ruin the device.
- Option C is incorrect. Unlike metered-dose inhalers (MDIs), dry powder inhalers (DPIs) should not be shaken before use.
</details>

---

### Question 295
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
A client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with community-acquired pneumonia and is receiving oxygen via nasal cannula. Which clinical finding should the nurse identify as an expected presentation of this illness?

A. A productive cough with yellowish-green sputum.
B. An oxygen saturation of 85% on 2 liters of oxygen.
C. Absent breath sounds in the lower lung fields.
D. A sudden onset of confusion and extreme lethargy.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** A productive cough with purulent (yellow, green, or rust-colored) sputum is an expected clinical manifestation of bacterial pneumonia due to the inflammatory response and infection in the lungs.
- Option B is unexpected. An oxygen saturation of 85% indicates significant hypoxia and inadequate oxygenation, requiring immediate nursing intervention.
- Option C is unexpected. While crackles or diminished sounds are common, completely absent breath sounds indicate a complication such as a pleural effusion, pneumothorax, or severe atelectasis.
- Option D is unexpected. Sudden confusion and lethargy are signs of severe hypoxia or sepsis and represent a dangerous change in the client's condition.
</details>
### Question 296
A client is learning how to properly use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. After the client presses the canister and breathes in the medication, what is the correct procedural step the nurse should instruct the client to do next?

A. Exhale forcefully through the mouth.
B. Swallow repeatedly to clear the airway.
C. Hold the breath for about 10 seconds.
D. Pant quickly to absorb the medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
After inhaling the medication from a metered-dose inhaler (MDI), the client should hold their breath for approximately 10 seconds (or as long as comfortably possible). This allows the aerosolized medication to settle deep into the lungs rather than being immediately exhaled. Exhaling forcefully, swallowing, or panting will prevent the medication from achieving maximum therapeutic effect in the lower airways. 
<strong>Lens: PROCEDURAL STEP</strong>
</details>

---

### Question 297
The home health nurse is evaluating a client who requires continuous supplemental oxygen via nasal cannula. Which observation in the client's home indicates a significant risk associated with oxygen safety?

A. The client applies a water-based lubricant to their dry lips.
B. The oxygen concentrator is placed 10 inches away from the wall.
C. The client stores backup oxygen cylinders in an upright, secured stand.
D. The client sleeps with a thick wool blanket on the bed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Wool and synthetic blankets can generate static electricity, which can easily act as an ignition source in an oxygen-rich environment, leading to a severe fire hazard. Clients on oxygen should use 100% cotton bedding and clothing. Water-based lubricants are safe (petroleum-based ones are flammable and should be avoided), keeping the concentrator away from the wall ensures proper ventilation, and storing tanks upright and secured prevents physical injury and valve damage.
<strong>Lens: RISK RECOGNITION</strong>
</details>

---

### Question 298
The nurse is planning care for a client admitted with bacterial pneumonia. Which intervention related to pneumonia care and precautions can the nurse safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Evaluating the client's oxygen saturation levels during ambulation.
B. Assisting the client with routine oral hygiene after meals.
C. Teaching the client how to properly cough and deep breathe.
D. Listening to the client's lung sounds to check for clearing secretions.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Assisting with basic activities of daily living, such as oral hygiene, falls within the scope of practice for the UAP. Frequent oral care is an important precaution to reduce the bacterial load in the mouth and prevent further respiratory complications. Evaluating oxygen tolerance, teaching respiratory exercises, and assessing lung sounds are strictly nursing responsibilities requiring clinical judgment and cannot be delegated.
<strong>Lens: DELEGATION</strong>
</details>

---

### Question 299
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client who has been prescribed a dry powder inhaler (DPI) for maintenance asthma therapy. Which statement by the client indicates that the teaching was successful?

A. "I will shake the inhaler vigorously for ten seconds before using it."
B. "I need to inhale rapidly and deeply through the mouthpiece."
C. "I should wash the plastic mouthpiece with warm water every day."
D. "I will breathe out directly into the inhaler before I take my dose."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
To effectively use a dry powder inhaler (DPI), the client must inhale rapidly and deeply to pull the powder out of the device and into the lungs. Shaking the inhaler is only required for metered-dose inhalers (MDIs). Washing a DPI with water can cause the powder to clump and ruin the device; it should be wiped with a dry cloth. Exhaling into the DPI will blow the powdered medication out of the device or introduce moisture.
<strong>Lens: CLIENT TEACHING</strong>
</details>

---

### Question 300
A client is being evaluated in the clinic two weeks after an acute episode of viral pneumonia. Which assessment finding should the nurse identify as an expected part of the recovery process rather than a sign of a new complication?

A. Mild fatigue and a lingering dry cough.
B. A new onset of restlessness and confusion.
C. An oxygen saturation reading of 89% on room air.
D. A resting respiratory rate of 28 breaths per minute.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
It is expected for a client recovering from pneumonia to experience mild fatigue and a lingering cough for several weeks as the respiratory tract continues to heal. Restlessness and confusion, an oxygen saturation of 89%, and tachypnea (respiratory rate of 28) are all unexpected findings that suggest hypoxia or worsening respiratory failure and require immediate intervention.
<strong>Lens: EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED</strong>
</details>
### Question 301
The nurse is assisting a client who is learning to use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. What is the correct procedural step the client should take immediately after pressing the canister to release the medication?

A) Exhale forcefully to push the medication deep into the lungs.
B) Breathe in slowly and deeply through the mouth for 3 to 5 seconds.
C) Hold the breath tightly for a minimum of 30 seconds.
D) Rinse the mouth immediately with a warm saltwater solution.

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B
<strong>Rationale:</strong> When using a metered-dose inhaler, the client should begin to breathe in slowly and deeply through the mouth just as they press down on the canister. This ensures the medication is carried down into the lower airways rather than hitting the back of the throat. Holding the breath is done *after* inhaling, typically for about 10 seconds (not 30). Exhaling forcefully after pressing the canister would blow the medication away. Rinsing the mouth is done after the entire procedure is complete, specifically if the inhaler contains a corticosteroid.
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step
</details>

---

### Question 302
A nurse is performing a home safety assessment for a client who has been prescribed continuous home oxygen therapy via nasal cannula. Which observation in the client's bedroom presents the greatest safety risk?

A) The client has a thick wool blanket on the bed.
B) The oxygen tubing is secured and is 10 feet long.
C) The client uses a water-based lubricant for dry lips.
D) A functioning smoke detector is installed near the bedroom door.

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Oxygen heavily supports combustion. Wool and synthetic materials can generate static electricity, which can create a spark and ignite a fire in an oxygen-rich environment. Clients should be taught to use 100% cotton blankets and clothing instead. Using water-based lubricants is safe and recommended (petroleum-based products are flammable and a risk). A 10-foot tubing and a functioning smoke detector are both safe and expected findings.
<strong>Lens:</strong> Risk Recognition
</details>

---

### Question 303
A client is admitted with bacterial pneumonia and is placed on droplet precautions. Which task is most appropriate for the registered nurse (RN) to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Educating the client on how to properly cover their mouth when coughing.
B) Assessing the client's lung sounds for crackles or wheezes every 4 hours.
C) Restocking the isolation supply cart with surgical masks outside the client's room.
D) Adjusting the flow rate of the client's oxygen therapy to maintain optimal saturation.

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Restocking isolation supplies is a routine, non-clinical task that does not require nursing judgment, making it completely safe and appropriate to delegate to a UAP. Assessing lung sounds, educating the client, and adjusting oxygen flow rates are tasks that require nursing judgment and fall within the scope of practice of the RN or respiratory therapist. 
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation
</details>

---

### Question 304
The nurse is providing education to a client prescribed two puffs of a short-acting bronchodilator via a metered-dose inhaler (MDI). Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will inhale rapidly and forcefully to get the medicine deep into my lungs."
B) "I should wash the metal medication canister in warm soapy water every day."
C) "I will pump the inhaler twice quickly and take one big breath."
D) "I should wait about 1 minute between the first and second puff."

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Waiting about 1 minute between puffs of a bronchodilator allows the first puff to begin opening the airways, which makes the second puff penetrate deeper and work more effectively. The client should inhale slowly and deeply, not rapidly. The metal canister should never be washed in water; only the plastic mouthpiece should be cleaned. Puffs should be administered one at a time, not pumped quickly together.
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching
</details>

---

### Question 305
The nurse is evaluating the oxygen safety practices of a client receiving home oxygen via nasal cannula. Which action by the client indicates that the safety education was effective?

A) The client ensures they remain at least 5 to 10 feet away from the gas stove while wearing oxygen.
B) The client uses petroleum jelly ointment on the nares to soothe irritation from the nasal prongs.
C) The client stores extra oxygen cylinders lying flat on their sides under the bed to save space.
D) The client increases the oxygen flow rate by 2 liters when feeling slightly short of breath.

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Because oxygen supports combustion, clients must stay at least 5 to 10 feet away from open flames, such as gas stoves, fireplaces, or candles. This action shows the client understands the safety risks. Petroleum jelly is flammable and should be avoided (water-soluble lubricants should be used instead). Oxygen tanks must always be stored upright and secured, never flat under a bed. Finally, oxygen is a medication, and clients should not adjust their own flow rates without a provider's order.
<strong>Lens:</strong> Evaluation
</details>

---
### Question 306
A nurse is observing the room of a client who is receiving continuous oxygen therapy via a nasal cannula. Which observation represents a safety risk that requires immediate intervention?

A. The client is using a water-soluble lubricant for dry, cracked lips.
B. The oxygen tubing is secured to the client's hospital gown with a plastic clip.
C. The client's family member provides a 100% cotton blanket for extra warmth.
D. A visitor brings a wool blanket for the client to use while sitting in the chair.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Risk Recognition<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Oxygen safety<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Wool and synthetic fabrics can generate static electricity, which poses a severe fire hazard in an oxygen-enriched environment. Clients on oxygen should only use cotton blankets and gowns. Water-soluble lubricants (Option A) are safe, whereas petroleum-based products are flammable and should be avoided. Plastic clips (Option B) and cotton blankets (Option C) do not pose a static or fire risk.
</details>

---

### Question 307
A nurse is reviewing the steps for using a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) with a newly diagnosed asthma client. What is the correct action for the client to take immediately after pressing down on the canister?

A. Inhale slowly and deeply through the mouth for 3 to 5 seconds.
B. Exhale forcefully through pursed lips to push the medication into the lungs.
C. Swallow the medication as quickly as possible.
D. Hold the breath for at least 30 seconds before exhaling.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Inhaler technique<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> When using a metered-dose inhaler, the client should begin to breathe in slowly and deeply through the mouth just as they press down on the canister, continuing the inhalation for 3 to 5 seconds to ensure the medication reaches the lower airways. Exhaling (Option B) or swallowing (Option C) defeats the purpose of an inhaled medication. The breath should be held for about 10 seconds, not 30 seconds (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 308
A nurse is caring for a client diagnosed with bacterial pneumonia who is placed on droplet precautions. Which task can the nurse safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Educating the client on how to appropriately use the incentive spirometer.
B. Assessing the client's lung sounds prior to the administration of antibiotics.
C. Assisting the client with ambulation to the bathroom while wearing a surgical mask.
D. Adjusting the client's oxygen flow rate based on recent pulse oximetry readings.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Pneumonia precautions<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Assisting a stable client with ambulation and activities of daily living is within the scope of practice for a UAP, provided they follow the appropriate infection control precautions (wearing a surgical mask for droplet precautions). Client education (Option A), assessment (Option B), and adjusting oxygen flow rates/titration (Option D) are strictly nursing responsibilities and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 309
A client is prescribed a fluticasone metered-dose inhaler, a corticosteroid, for the prevention of asthma attacks. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the medication teaching?

A. "I only need to use this inhaler when I feel short of breath or start wheezing."
B. "I should rinse my mouth with water and spit it out after using this inhaler."
C. "I will keep the inhaler in my pocket so it stays at body temperature at all times."
D. "I should wash the metal canister in warm soapy water every evening."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Inhaler technique<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Inhaled corticosteroids like fluticasone can leave residue in the mouth that alters normal flora, leading to oral candidiasis (thrush). Rinsing the mouth and spitting the water out after use helps prevent this complication. Fluticasone is a controller medication, not a rescue inhaler for acute shortness of breath (Option A). The metal canister should never be submerged in water; only the plastic mouthpiece should be washed (Option D). Keeping the inhaler at body temperature is unnecessary; room temperature is standard (Option C).
</details>

---

### Question 310
The nurse is evaluating a client’s ability to use an incentive spirometer, which was prescribed to prevent pneumonia after abdominal surgery. Which action by the client indicates that the teaching has been effective?

A. The client blows rapidly and forcefully into the mouthpiece to raise the piston.
B. The client takes a quick, shallow breath in and removes the mouthpiece immediately.
C. The client inhales slowly and deeply, holding their breath for several seconds.
D. The client uses the spirometer device exactly once every four hours.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Evaluation<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Pneumonia precautions<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Correct use of an incentive spirometer involves the client wrapping their lips tightly around the mouthpiece and inhaling slowly and deeply to elevate the piston, then holding their breath for 2 to 3 seconds before exhaling. This promotes maximal alveolar expansion to prevent atelectasis and pneumonia. The client should inhale, not blow into the device (Option A). A quick, shallow breath does not expand the lungs properly (Option B). The device should be used frequently, typically 5 to 10 times every hour while awake, not just once every four hours (Option D).
</details>
### Question 311
A nurse is observing a client use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. What is the correct procedural step for the client to take immediately after pressing down on the canister?

A. Exhale quickly and forcefully through the mouth.
B. Breathe in slowly and deeply for 3 to 5 seconds. 
C. Hold their breath for exactly 30 seconds.
D. Swallow repeatedly to ensure the medication reaches the stomach.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Procedural Step
**System Focus:** Inhaler Technique

When using a metered-dose inhaler, the client should breathe in slowly and deeply for 3 to 5 seconds simultaneously while pressing down on the canister. This ensures the medication is drawn deep into the lower airways rather than resting in the mouth or throat. The client should then hold their breath for about 10 seconds (not 30 seconds). Swallowing the medication is incorrect, as it is meant for the lungs, not the gastrointestinal tract. Exhaling should occur before pressing the canister, not immediately after.
</details>

---

### Question 312
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client who will be using home oxygen therapy via nasal cannula. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A. "I can use petroleum jelly on my lips if they get dry from the oxygen."
B. "I should keep my oxygen tank at least 2 feet away from the space heater."
C. "I will make sure to use cotton blankets on my bed instead of wool." 
D. "I am allowed to smoke as long as I turn the oxygen off first."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Client Teaching
**System Focus:** Oxygen Safety

Oxygen supports combustion, meaning fires burn faster and hotter in an oxygen-rich environment. Cotton fabrics do not generate static electricity, whereas wool and synthetic fabrics do, making cotton the safest choice to prevent sparks. Petroleum jelly is oil-based and highly flammable; only water-soluble lubricants should be used. Oxygen tanks must be kept much further away from heat sources (typically at least 5 to 10 feet). Smoking is strictly prohibited at all times in a home where oxygen is used, even if the oxygen is temporarily turned off, due to residual oxygen trapped in clothing and hair.
</details>

---

### Question 313
A nurse is caring for a client admitted with community-acquired pneumonia. Which clinical finding should the nurse expect as a typical manifestation of this condition?

A. Bradycardia and low blood pressure.
B. Vesicular breath sounds over the lung bases.
C. Pale, cool, and clammy skin.
D. Productive cough with yellowish-green sputum. 

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**System Focus:** Pneumonia Precautions

Pneumonia is an infection that inflames the air sacs in the lungs, which may fill with fluid or pus. A productive cough with purulent (yellowish-green or rust-colored) sputum is an expected finding, particularly with bacterial pneumonia. Tachycardia and fever (warm, flushed skin) are expected, not bradycardia or pale/cool skin. Vesicular breath sounds are normal; a client with pneumonia would likely have adventitious sounds such as crackles, wheezes, or diminished breath sounds over the affected area.
</details>

---

### Question 314
A registered nurse (RN) is assigning tasks for a client diagnosed with pneumonia who is receiving continuous oxygen at 2 L/min via nasal cannula. Which task is appropriate to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Assisting the client with frequent oral care. 
B. Adjusting the oxygen flow rate to 3 L/min if the client reports shortness of breath.
C. Teaching the client how to use the incentive spirometer.
D. Assessing the client's lung sounds after a coughing episode.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Delegation
**System Focus:** Pneumonia Precautions & Oxygen Safety

Delegation to a UAP (unlicensed assistive personnel) involves tasks that are standard, unchanging, and do not require nursing judgment. Assisting with oral care is a basic hygiene task and a key pneumonia precaution to reduce bacterial buildup in the mouth. Adjusting oxygen flow rates requires a prescription and nursing judgment. Teaching and assessing (such as lung sounds) fall strictly within the scope of practice of the RN and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 315
A nurse is conducting a safety check in the room of a client receiving continuous oxygen therapy. Which item, if found in the room, presents the greatest risk for injury?

A. A water-soluble lubricant tube on the bedside table.
B. A cotton gown worn by the client.
C. An electric razor plugged into the wall outlet. 
D. A battery-operated pulse oximeter.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Risk Recognition
**System Focus:** Oxygen Safety

In an oxygen-enriched environment, any device that can generate a spark poses a severe fire hazard. Plug-in electrical appliances, such as electric razors or hair dryers, are high-risk for creating sparks and should not be used while oxygen is flowing. Water-soluble lubricants and cotton gowns are safe choices for clients on oxygen. A battery-operated pulse oximeter is a standard, low-risk medical device used safely in these environments.
</details>
### Question 316
A nurse is providing education to a client on how to use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. Which instruction should the nurse include in the teaching?

A) "Hold your breath for 2 seconds after inhaling the medication."
B) "Inhale as quickly and forcefully as you can after pressing the canister."
C) "Exhale completely before you press the canister and take a breath."
D) "Press the canister down only after you have finished breathing in."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Client Teaching

**Rationale:** When using a metered-dose inhaler (MDI), the client should exhale completely before initiating the breath. This empties the lungs, allowing for a deeper inhalation so the medication can reach deep into the airways. The client should inhale slowly and deeply (not quickly), press the canister at the exact beginning of the inhalation (not after), and hold their breath for about 10 seconds (not 2 seconds) to allow the medication to settle in the lungs.

</details>

---

### Question 317
The nurse is preparing to collect a sputum specimen from a client admitted with bacterial pneumonia. Which procedural step should the nurse implement?

A) Instruct the client to rinse their mouth with water before coughing.
B) Ask the client to spit saliva directly into the sterile specimen cup.
C) Have the client use an antibacterial mouthwash just before the collection.
D) Collect the specimen in the evening right before the client goes to sleep.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Procedural Step

**Rationale:** Rinsing the mouth with water prior to a sputum collection helps remove superficial oral flora and food particles, reducing the contamination of the sputum sample. The client should not use antibacterial mouthwash, as this can alter the flora and ruin the culture. The specimen should consist of sputum brought up from the lungs via a deep cough, not saliva. Sputum specimens are best collected early in the morning when secretions have pooled overnight, not in the evening.

</details>

---

### Question 318
A home health nurse is visiting a client who requires continuous oxygen therapy via nasal cannula. Which observation in the client's home indicates a severe safety risk?

A) The client is wearing a 100% cotton shirt while using oxygen.
B) A prominent "No Smoking" sign is placed on the front door of the house.
C) The extra oxygen tubing is draped smoothly over the back of a chair.
D) The client applies a petroleum-based ointment to soothe their dry nares.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Risk Recognition

**Rationale:** Oxygen supports combustion, meaning it causes things to burn more readily and rapidly. Petroleum-based products (like Vaseline) are highly flammable and should never be used on the face or nares of a client receiving oxygen therapy. Instead, a water-soluble lubricant should be used. Wearing a cotton shirt (which resists static electricity), displaying "No Smoking" signs, and keeping tubing safely draped to prevent tripping are all correct and safe practices.

</details>

---

### Question 319
A nurse is listening to the lung sounds of a client who is recovering from left lower lobe pneumonia. Which assessment finding is an expected characteristic for this client?

A) Tracheal deviation pointing toward the right side of the neck.
B) Fine crackles auscultated in the left lung base.
C) An oxygen saturation of 85% on room air.
D) A sudden onset of severe, stabbing chest pain upon inspiration.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected

**Rationale:** Fine crackles are a common, expected finding in a client with pneumonia due to the presence of fluid or exudate in the alveoli. As the pneumonia resolves, these crackles may persist for a time before completely clearing. Tracheal deviation is a sign of a medical emergency (such as a tension pneumothorax). An oxygen saturation of 85% indicates severe hypoxia and requires immediate intervention. Sudden, severe stabbing pain could indicate a pulmonary embolism or worsening pleural effusion, which is unexpected and requires immediate evaluation.

</details>

---

### Question 320
A nurse is caring for a client with pneumonia who is receiving oxygen via nasal cannula. Which task is most appropriate for the nurse to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assisting the client to a semi-Fowler's position in bed.
B) Administering a prescribed albuterol inhaler before meals.
C) Adjusting the oxygen flow rate based on the pulse oximetry reading.
D) Educating the client on the importance of coughing and deep breathing.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Delegation

**Rationale:** Assisting a client with positioning (such as raising the head of the bed to a semi-Fowler's position to ease the work of breathing) is a standard skill that falls within the UAP's scope of practice. Administering medication (inhaler), adjusting oxygen flow rates (which is considered a medication prescription), and providing client education are all nursing responsibilities that require a nursing license and cannot be delegated to a UAP.

</details>
### Question 321
A nurse is observing a client using a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. Which action indicates the correct procedural step for administering the inhaled medication?

A. Exhaling completely before pressing the canister.
B. Inhaling rapidly and shallowly while pressing the canister.
C. Holding the breath for 2 seconds after inhaling the medication.
D. Pressing the canister immediately after starting to exhale.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Inhaler technique<br>
To properly use an MDI, the client should exhale completely first to empty the lungs. Then, they should press the canister while taking a slow, deep breath in, and hold their breath for at least 10 seconds to allow the medication to properly deposit in the lungs. Inhaling rapidly, holding the breath for only 2 seconds, or pressing the canister while exhaling will prevent the medication from reaching the lower airways.
</details>

### Question 322
The nurse is providing discharge instructions to a client who will be using continuous home oxygen therapy via nasal cannula. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A. "I can smoke in the same room as long as the oxygen is turned off."
B. "I will use a cotton blanket on my bed instead of a wool one."
C. "I should apply petroleum jelly to my nose if it gets dry."
D. "I will keep the oxygen tank right next to the space heater for easy access."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Oxygen safety<br>
Cotton blankets are recommended because wool and synthetic materials can generate static electricity, which is a significant fire hazard in an oxygen-rich environment. Petroleum jelly is flammable; a water-based lubricant should be used to soothe a dry nose. Smoking is strictly prohibited near oxygen, even if turned off, as oxygen saturates clothing and furniture. Oxygen equipment must be kept at least 10 feet away from open flames and heat sources like space heaters.
</details>

---

### Question 323
A nurse is caring for a client with bacterial pneumonia who is currently on droplet precautions. Which task is most appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Assessing the client's lung sounds for crackles or wheezes.
B. Teaching the client how to use the incentive spirometer.
C. Restocking the surgical masks and gloves outside the client's room.
D. Evaluating the client's oxygen saturation after ambulation.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Pneumonia precautions<br>
Restocking isolation supplies (such as masks and gloves) is a routine, non-clinical task that falls well within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing lung sounds, providing initial teaching on medical devices, and evaluating clinical data (like oxygen saturation trends after activity) require nursing judgment and must be performed by the registered nurse.
</details>

---

### Question 324
The nurse is reviewing the morning data for a client diagnosed with viral pneumonia. Which finding is an expected clinical manifestation of this respiratory condition?

A. A resting heart rate of 50 beats per minute.
B. A respiratory rate of 12 breaths per minute.
C. Clear breath sounds throughout all lung fields.
D. A low-grade fever and a dry, nonproductive cough.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Pneumonia precautions<br>
Viral pneumonia typically presents with a low-grade fever, muscle aches, fatigue, and a dry, nonproductive cough. Bacterial pneumonia often presents with higher fevers and a productive cough with purulent sputum. Clear breath sounds and normal respiratory/heart rates are unexpected findings, as the client has an active respiratory infection that typically causes tachypnea, tachycardia, and adventitious breath sounds (such as crackles or wheezes).
</details>

### Question 325
A client with asthma is prescribed a fluticasone metered-dose inhaler (MDI). The nurse recognizes that the client is at risk for developing an oral candida infection (thrush) if they fail to perform which action?

A. Shaking the canister vigorously for 60 seconds before use.
B. Rinsing their mouth with water and spitting it out after use.
C. Taking the medication on an empty stomach.
D. Waiting 5 minutes between puffs of the medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Risk Recognition<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Inhaler technique<br>
Fluticasone is an inhaled corticosteroid. Rinsing the mouth with water and spitting it out after administration removes residual steroid medication from the oral cavity. This simple action significantly reduces the risk of developing oral candidiasis (thrush), a common localized fungal infection associated with inhaled steroids. The other options do not prevent this infection.
</details>
### Question 326
A nurse is teaching a client with asthma how to use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) with a spacer. Which instruction is correct for the nurse to include?

A. "Inhale rapidly and forcefully immediately after pressing the canister."
B. "Press the canister down and breathe in slowly and deeply."
C. "Remove the spacer if you hear a loud whistling sound."
D. "Exhale completely into the mouthpiece before pressing the canister."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
Using a spacer assists in delivering more medication to the lungs rather than the back of the throat. The client should press the canister and breathe in slowly and deeply. A whistling sound on some spacers indicates that the client is breathing in too fast, not that the spacer should be removed. The client should exhale completely *before* placing the mouthpiece in their mouth, not *into* the device.
</details>

### Question 327
The nurse is setting up continuous oxygen therapy via nasal cannula for a client. Which procedural step is correct to ensure the safe and effective delivery of oxygen?

A. Ensure the nasal prongs curve downward following the natural shape of the nostrils.
B. Apply a thin layer of petroleum jelly to the client's nares to prevent dryness.
C. Set the oxygen flow rate on the wall meter to 10 liters per minute.
D. Tuck the oxygen tubing tightly under the client's chin to prevent dislodgement.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
When applying a nasal cannula, the prongs should curve downward to follow the natural anatomical curve of the nasal passages, directing oxygen safely into the airway. Petroleum jelly is combustible and should never be used with oxygen; a water-soluble lubricant is required. A nasal cannula is designed for flow rates up to 6 liters per minute, not 10. The tubing should be snug but not uncomfortably tight under the chin, as this can cause skin breakdown.
</details>

### Question 328
The nurse is caring for a client who is recovering from bacterial pneumonia. Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A. Instruct the client on the proper technique for using the incentive spirometer.
B. Assess the client's lung sounds after they perform a coughing exercise.
C. Empty the client's emesis basin and evaluate the color of the sputum.
D. Assist the client with walking from the bed to the bathroom.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation<br>
Assisting a stable client with ambulation is a standard task that falls within the scope of practice for a UAP. Instructing on incentive spirometry, assessing lung sounds, and evaluating/documenting the clinical characteristics of sputum all require nursing judgment and must be performed by the registered nurse. 
</details>

### Question 329
The nurse is reviewing the morning assessment data for a client admitted with community-acquired pneumonia. Which clinical finding is expected for this diagnosis?

A. A slow, bounding radial pulse
B. A body temperature of 97.6°F (36.4°C)
C. A productive cough with thick, greenish-yellow sputum
D. Clear vesicular breath sounds over all lung fields

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected<br>
A productive cough with purulent (greenish-yellow) sputum is a classic expected manifestation of bacterial pneumonia due to the inflammatory response and infection in the lungs. Tachycardia (a fast pulse) and fever are expected, whereas a slow pulse and a normal body temperature are not typical active findings. Lung sounds would likely reveal crackles or rhonchi, not clear vesicular sounds.
</details>

### Question 330
A client with a severe respiratory infection keeps pulling off their oxygen mask. The client tells the nurse, "I hate wearing this thing; it feels like it's suffocating me." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A. "Tell me more about the suffocating feeling you are experiencing."
B. "You need to keep it on so your oxygen levels don't drop dangerously low."
C. "I will ask the doctor if we can safely remove the oxygen for a while."
D. "Try taking deep breaths through your nose; the feeling will go away soon."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Therapeutic Communication<br>
Using the open-ended statement "Tell me more about..." validates the client's feelings and encourages them to express their concerns without judgment. This builds trust and helps the nurse identify specific comfort measures that might help (e.g., switching to a nasal cannula if appropriate). The other options dismiss the client's feelings, offer false reassurance, or are inappropriately confrontational.
</details>
### Question 331
A nurse conducts a home visit for a client prescribed continuous home oxygen therapy. Which observation by the nurse represents the greatest safety risk?

A) The client wears 100% cotton clothing while resting in the house.
B) The client applies a petroleum-based ointment to treat chapped lips.
C) The client stores extra oxygen cylinders in an upright, secured stand.
D) The client keeps the oxygen concentrator at least 5 feet away from the heating vent.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Risk Recognition<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Oxygen safety<br>
Petroleum-based products (like Vaseline) are highly flammable. When combined with an oxygen-rich environment, they pose a significant fire hazard. Clients on oxygen should use water-soluble lubricants for dry lips or nasal passages. Wearing cotton clothing (Option A) is recommended because synthetic materials can generate static electricity. Storing tanks upright (Option C) and keeping equipment away from heat sources (Option D) are both safe and correct practices.
</details>

### Question 332
A nurse is instructing a client on the proper use of a metered-dose inhaler (MDI). What is the correct first procedural step the client should take before administering the medication?

A) Exhale completely to empty the lungs.
B) Press the canister down while inhaling deeply.
C) Remove the mouthpiece cap and shake the inhaler well.
D) Hold the breath for 10 seconds to allow the medication to settle.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Inhaler technique<br>
The correct first step when using a metered-dose inhaler is to remove the cap and shake the device well (usually for 3 to 5 seconds) to ensure the medication and propellant are properly mixed. Exhaling completely (Option A) is done immediately after shaking the inhaler. Pressing the canister while inhaling (Option B) and holding the breath (Option D) are subsequent steps in the process.
</details>

---

### Question 333
A nurse is providing discharge instructions to a client recovering from pneumonia. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of actions to prevent complications and relapse?

A) "I can stop taking my prescribed antibiotics as soon as my fever goes away."
B) "I should restrict my water intake to keep excess fluid out of my lungs."
C) "I will spend most of my day resting flat in bed to save my energy."
D) "I will use my incentive spirometer and practice deep breathing every hour."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Pneumonia precautions<br>
Using an incentive spirometer and practicing deep breathing exercises helps keep the airways open, prevents alveolar collapse (atelectasis), and clears secretions, which are vital pneumonia precautions. Antibiotics must be taken until the full course is completed, even if symptoms improve (Option A). Fluid intake should be increased, not restricted, to help thin respiratory secretions (Option B). Lying flat in bed for extended periods increases the risk of secretion pooling and further respiratory complications (Option C).
</details>

### Question 334
A nurse is evaluating a client being treated for bacterial pneumonia. Which clinical finding best indicates that airway clearance interventions have been effective?

A) The client's lung sounds are clear to auscultation bilaterally.
B) The client's respiratory rate has increased to 26 breaths per minute.
C) The client requires an increased flow of oxygen to maintain saturation.
D) The client reports a persistent, dry, hacking cough.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Evaluation<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Pneumonia precautions / Airway clearance<br>
Clear lung sounds bilaterally indicate that secretions have been successfully mobilized and cleared from the lower airways, demonstrating that interventions were effective. An increased respiratory rate (Option B), increased oxygen demand (Option C), and a persistent, dry cough (Option D) all indicate ongoing or worsening respiratory distress and poor airway clearance.
</details>

### Question 335
A client uses an inhaled corticosteroid daily for asthma control. During an oral assessment, the nurse observes white, curd-like patches on the client's tongue and inner cheeks. How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A) An expected, harmless side effect of the medication's chemical propellant.
B) An unexpected oral candidiasis infection due to poor inhaler hygiene technique.
C) An expected localized allergic reaction to the plastic inhaler mouthpiece.
D) An unexpected sign of systemic corticosteroid toxicity requiring emergency care.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected<br>
<strong>System Focus:</strong> Inhaler technique<br>
White, curd-like patches in the mouth indicate oral candidiasis (thrush), which is an unexpected complication resulting from the client not rinsing their mouth with water and spitting after using an inhaled corticosteroid. It is a localized fungal infection, not a harmless side effect (Option A), an allergic reaction (Option C), or a sign of systemic emergency toxicity (Option D). Rinsing the mouth helps prevent this common but preventable complication.
</details>
### Question 336
A nurse is conducting a home safety evaluation for a client who requires continuous supplemental oxygen. Which observation in the client's home presents the greatest fire risk and requires immediate intervention?

A) The client uses a 100% cotton blanket on the bed.
B) A water-based personal lubricant is kept on the nightstand.
C) The client applies petroleum jelly to their lips for dryness.
D) The oxygen concentrator is placed three feet away from the wall.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION

**Rationale:** Petroleum jelly is a combustible substance. When combined with supplemental oxygen, it greatly increases the risk of facial burns and fire. Clients should be taught to use water-based lubricants for dry lips and nasal passages. A cotton blanket (Option A) is safe, as synthetic or wool blankets are more likely to cause static electricity sparks. A water-based lubricant (Option B) is safe to use with oxygen. Placing the concentrator away from the wall (Option D) is proper safety practice to ensure adequate ventilation for the machine.
</details>

---

### Question 337
A nurse is guiding a client on the proper use of a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) with a spacer. After shaking the inhaler and attaching it to the spacer, what procedural step should the client perform next, immediately before pressing the canister?

A) Exhale completely to empty the lungs.
B) Inhale rapidly through the mouth.
C) Hold their breath for 10 seconds.
D) Take a shallow breath through the nose.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP

**Rationale:** The correct procedural step before activating a metered-dose inhaler is to exhale completely. Emptying the lungs allows the client to take a deep, slow inhalation immediately after pressing the canister, drawing the medication deep into the lower airways. Inhaling rapidly (Option B) is incorrect; the breath should be slow and deep. Holding the breath (Option C) is done *after* inhaling the medication, not before. Taking a shallow breath through the nose (Option D) does not prepare the lungs to receive the inhaled medication.
</details>

---

### Question 338
A client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with bacterial pneumonia and is placed on droplet precautions. Which task is appropriate for the registered nurse (RN) to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Auscultating the client's lung sounds for crackles.
B) Teaching the visiting family members how to properly wear a mask.
C) Evaluating the color and consistency of the client's sputum.
D) Refilling the client's bedside water pitcher.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Lens:** DELEGATION

**Rationale:** Refilling a water pitcher is a standard, routine task that falls within the UAP's scope of practice, provided they adhere to the required droplet precautions (wearing a mask) when entering the room. Auscultating lung sounds (Option A) and evaluating sputum (Option C) require nursing assessment, which cannot be delegated. Teaching family members about infection control protocols (Option B) is the responsibility of the RN.
</details>
