### Question 339
A nurse is assessing a client with a long history of Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease (COPD) who is receiving oxygen via nasal cannula at 2 L/min. Which assessment finding is considered an expected baseline for this client?

A) A respiratory rate of 32 breaths per minute.
B) A pulse oximetry reading of 90% on room air.
C) Sudden, new-onset confusion and restlessness.
D) Deep blue cyanosis around the lips and oral mucosa.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED

**Rationale:** For clients with severe COPD, a baseline oxygen saturation (SpO2) between 88% and 92% is generally considered an expected and acceptable finding due to chronic air trapping and altered respiratory drive. A respiratory rate of 32 (Option A) indicates tachypnea and respiratory distress. Sudden confusion (Option C) is an unexpected sign of acute hypoxia or hypercapnia requiring immediate intervention. Deep blue cyanosis (Option D) is an unexpected sign of severe, acute tissue hypoxia. 
</details>

---

### Question 340
A nurse is providing discharge instructions to a client who has been newly prescribed a fluticasone metered-dose inhaler for asthma control. Which instruction is most important to include to prevent a local complication associated with this medication?

A) "Store the inhaler in the refrigerator between uses."
B) "Use this inhaler immediately if you feel an asthma attack starting."
C) "Rinse your mouth with water and spit it out after each use."
D) "Take this medication on an empty stomach to increase absorption."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING

**Rationale:** Fluticasone is an inhaled corticosteroid. Rinsing the mouth with water and spitting it out after use removes residual medication from the oral cavity, significantly reducing the risk of developing oral candidiasis (thrush), a common local complication. The inhaler should be stored at room temperature (Option A). Corticosteroids are maintenance medications, not rescue inhalers for acute attacks (Option B). Taking the medication on an empty stomach (Option D) is irrelevant, as this is an inhaled medication, not an oral pill.
</details>
### Question 341
**Lens: Client Teaching**

A nurse is teaching a client how to properly use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. Which instruction should the nurse include in the teaching?

A) "Inhale quickly and shallowly while pressing down on the canister."
B) "Exhale completely, press the canister, and take a slow, deep breath."
C) "Hold the inhaler 5 inches away from your mouth before spraying."
D) "Swallow rapidly immediately after the medication enters your mouth."

<details>
<summary>Click to reveal answer and rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer: B**

**Rationale:** When using a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer, the client should breathe out completely, place the mouthpiece in their mouth, press the canister down once, and breathe in slowly and deeply. This ensures the medication reaches the lower airways. Inhaling quickly or shallowly causes the medication to hit the back of the throat instead of entering the lungs. The inhaler should be placed in the mouth or 1 to 2 inches away, not 5 inches. Swallowing the medication diverts it to the gastrointestinal tract rather than the lungs.
</details>

---

### Question 342
**Lens: Procedural Step**

A nurse is assisting a client with pneumonia to use an incentive spirometer to prevent atelectasis. Which procedural step is correct?

A) Tell the client to blow forcefully into the mouthpiece to raise the piston.
B) Encourage the client to take rapid, shallow breaths into the device.
C) Instruct the client to inhale slowly and deeply through the mouthpiece.
D) Remind the client to use the device only once a day before bedtime.

<details>
<summary>Click to reveal answer and rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer: C**

**Rationale:** An incentive spirometer is designed to mimic natural sighing or yawning to fully expand the lungs. The correct technique is to exhale normally, seal the lips around the mouthpiece, and inhale as slowly and deeply as possible to raise the piston or ball. The client should not blow into the device. Rapid, shallow breaths do not promote alveolar expansion. The device should be used frequently (e.g., 10 times an hour while awake), not just once a day.
</details>

---

### Question 343
**Lens: Risk Recognition**

A home health nurse is conducting a safety assessment for a client who requires continuous home oxygen therapy. Which observation poses the greatest safety risk?

A) A jar of petroleum jelly is on the nightstand to treat nasal dryness.
B) The oxygen tubing is secured alongside the baseboards of the hallway.
C) The client uses a battery-operated electric razor for grooming.
D) There is a "No Smoking" sign clearly posted on the front door.

<details>
<summary>Click to reveal answer and rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer: A**

**Rationale:** Petroleum jelly is a combustible, oil-based product that can ignite easily in the presence of concentrated oxygen. Clients should only use water-based lubricants for nasal dryness associated with oxygen use. Securing tubing to the baseboards prevents tripping hazards. A battery-operated electric razor is safe, whereas razors that plug into the wall might spark and pose a risk. Posting a "No Smoking" sign is an expected and correct safety measure.
</details>

---

### Question 344
**Lens: Delegation**

A nurse is assigning tasks for a client admitted with bacterial pneumonia who requires 2 L/min of oxygen via nasal cannula. Which task is most appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Evaluating the client's breath sounds after a coughing episode.
B) Adjusting the oxygen flow rate based on current pulse oximetry readings.
C) Teaching the client how to splint the chest with a pillow when coughing.
D) Assisting the client with frequent oral hygiene and mouth care.

<details>
<summary>Click to reveal answer and rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer: D**

**Rationale:** Assisting with oral hygiene is a standard, routine task that falls within the UAP's scope of practice and helps prevent further respiratory complications. Evaluating breath sounds (assessment), adjusting oxygen flow rates (intervention requiring clinical judgment), and teaching (education) are all nursing responsibilities that cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 345
**Lens: Expected vs Unexpected**

A nurse is assessing a client who was newly admitted with community-acquired pneumonia. Which clinical manifestation should the nurse recognize as an expected finding?

A) A resting heart rate of 55 beats per minute.
B) A productive cough with yellowish-green sputum.
C) Clear and equal breath sounds bilaterally in all lobes.
D) Tympany upon percussion of the anterior chest.

<details>
<summary>Click to reveal answer and rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer: B**

**Rationale:** A productive cough with purulent (yellowish-green) sputum is a classic, expected finding in a client with bacterial pneumonia due to the inflammatory response and infection in the lungs. Tachycardia (not bradycardia) is typically expected due to fever and hypoxia. Breath sounds are usually abnormal (e.g., crackles, wheezes, or diminished) rather than clear. Percussion over consolidated lung tissue typically yields dullness, not tympany.
</details>
### Question 346
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
A nurse is teaching a client the correct technique for using a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. Which procedural step should the nurse instruct the client to perform first?

A. Inhale quickly through the nose before pressing the canister.
B. Exhale completely just before pressing the canister.
C. Hold the breath for 3 seconds after receiving the medication.
D. Press the canister twice rapidly during a single inhalation.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Exhale completely just before pressing the canister.

**Rationale:** To correctly use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI), the client should exhale completely to empty the lungs, place the mouthpiece in the mouth, press the canister once, and inhale slowly and deeply. The client should hold their breath for about 10 seconds (not 3 seconds) to allow the medication to settle in the lungs. Pressing the canister twice rapidly does not allow for proper dosing and inhalation.

</details>

---

### Question 347
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client who will be using continuous home oxygen therapy via a nasal cannula. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of oxygen safety?

A. "I can use petroleum jelly on my nose if it feels dry."
B. "It is safe to smoke in the room if the oxygen is turned off."
C. "I should use a wool blanket to keep warm at night."
D. "I will wear cotton clothing to prevent static electricity."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. "I will wear cotton clothing to prevent static electricity."

**Rationale:** Oxygen strongly supports combustion. Clients should wear cotton clothing and use cotton bedding to prevent static electricity, which can generate sparks and cause a fire. Petroleum-based products (like petroleum jelly) are flammable and should be avoided; a water-soluble lubricant should be used instead. Wool and synthetic fabrics create static electricity. Smoking should never be permitted in the home where oxygen is used or stored, even if it is currently turned off.

</details>

---

### Question 348
**Lens:** EXPECTED VS UNEXPECTED
A nurse is assessing a client who was recently admitted with bacterial pneumonia. Which clinical finding is expected for this condition?

A. Clear, watery sputum with a mild, dry cough.
B. Tracheal deviation away from the affected lung.
C. Fever, chills, and a productive cough with thick sputum.
D. A resting heart rate of 50 beats per minute.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Fever, chills, and a productive cough with thick sputum.

**Rationale:** Expected findings of bacterial pneumonia include fever, chills, shortness of breath, pleuritic chest pain, and a productive cough with purulent (thick, yellow, green, or rust-colored) sputum. Clear, watery sputum is more commonly associated with allergies or mild viral infections. Tracheal deviation is an unexpected, emergency finding associated with a tension pneumothorax. Tachycardia (fast heart rate), rather than bradycardia (50 bpm), is expected due to fever and hypoxia.

</details>

---

### Question 349
**Lens:** DELEGATION
A nurse is caring for a client with pneumonia who is receiving oxygen at 2 L/min via nasal cannula. Which task is appropriate to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Providing frequent oral care to the client.
B. Adjusting the oxygen flow rate if the client feels short of breath.
C. Assessing the client's lung sounds after a coughing episode.
D. Educating the client on the importance of using the incentive spirometer.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. Providing frequent oral care to the client.

**Rationale:** Providing routine oral care is a standard daily living activity that falls safely within the scope of practice for unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP). Oral care is essential for clients with pneumonia to promote comfort and reduce the bacterial load in the mouth. Adjusting oxygen flow rates, assessing lung sounds, and providing education are clinical responsibilities that require a nursing license and cannot be delegated to a UAP.

</details>

---

### Question 350
**Lens:** EVALUATION
A nurse observes a client using a dry powder inhaler (DPI) for daily asthma maintenance. Which action by the client indicates that the teaching was successful?

A. The client shakes the device vigorously before administration.
B. The client exhales forcefully directly into the mouthpiece.
C. The client washes the inhaler with warm soapy water after use.
D. The client inhales rapidly and deeply through the mouthpiece.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. The client inhales rapidly and deeply through the mouthpiece.

**Rationale:** For a dry powder inhaler (DPI), the client must inhale rapidly and deeply to provide enough force to deliver the powdered medication into the lungs. Shaking the device is required for a metered-dose inhaler (MDI), but not a DPI. The client should never exhale into the mouthpiece, as the moisture from the breath can cause the powder to clump. DPIs should be kept dry and wiped with a clean, dry cloth; they should never be washed with water.

</details>
### Question 351
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client prescribed a fluticasone metered-dose inhaler (MDI) for asthma management. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will inhale as quickly as I can when I press the canister down."
B) "I should wash the plastic mouthpiece in the dishwasher every day."
C) "I need to rinse my mouth with water and spit it out after using this inhaler."
D) "I will take this medication immediately whenever I feel suddenly short of breath."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING
**System Focus:** Inhaler Technique

Fluticasone is an inhaled corticosteroid. Rinsing the mouth and spitting out the water after use removes residual medication from the oral cavity, significantly reducing the risk of oral candidiasis (thrush). Option A is incorrect because clients should be taught to breathe in slowly and deeply when using an MDI, not as quickly as possible. Option B is incorrect because washing the mouthpiece in the dishwasher can damage it; it should be cleaned with warm water and air-dried. Option D is incorrect because fluticasone is a daily maintenance medication, not a rescue inhaler for acute shortness of breath.
</details>

---

### Question 352
A nurse is setting up continuous oxygen therapy via nasal cannula for a client admitted with a respiratory infection. Which action should the nurse take as a primary safety measure during this procedure?

A) Post a "No Smoking" sign on the client's room door.
B) Apply a petroleum-based ointment to the client's nares to prevent dryness.
C) Instruct the client to take deep breaths exclusively through the mouth.
D) Humidify the oxygen to prevent static sparks inside the plastic tubing.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP
**System Focus:** Oxygen Safety

Oxygen supports and violently accelerates combustion. A fundamental procedural step when initiating oxygen therapy is placing a "No Smoking" or "Oxygen in Use" sign on the door to alert staff, visitors, and clients to the fire hazard. Option B is incorrect and dangerous; petroleum-based products are flammable and should never be used on the face or nares of a client on oxygen. Only water-soluble lubricants should be used. Option C is incorrect as the client needs to breathe through their nose to receive oxygen effectively via a nasal cannula. Option D is incorrect; humidification is used to prevent drying of the mucous membranes, not to prevent sparks.
</details>

---

### Question 353
A nurse is caring for a client recovering from bacterial pneumonia. Which intervention related to pneumonia care and infection precautions can the nurse safely delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client's lung sounds every 4 hours.
B) Teaching the client how to properly use the incentive spirometer.
C) Adjusting the client's oxygen flow rate during ambulation.
D) Assisting the client with frequent oral hygiene before and after meals.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** DELEGATION
**System Focus:** Pneumonia Precautions

Frequent oral hygiene helps reduce the bacterial count in the mouth, which is an important supportive measure for clients with pneumonia and helps prevent secondary respiratory complications. Assisting with basic hygiene is within the scope of practice for UAP. Options A, B, and C all require nursing judgment, assessment, or teaching, and therefore must be performed by the registered nurse and cannot be delegated. 
</details>

---

### Question 354
The home health nurse is visiting a client who requires continuous home oxygen therapy. Which observation in the home indicates an immediate safety risk that the nurse must address?

A) The client is wearing a 100% cotton long-sleeved shirt.
B) A jar of petroleum jelly is resting on the client's bedside table.
C) The oxygen concentrator is placed 10 feet away from the bed.
D) The client uses a battery-operated electric razor for shaving.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** RISK RECOGNITION
**System Focus:** Oxygen Safety

Petroleum jelly is a combustible substance. If applied to the lips or nares while a client is using oxygen, it creates a significant fire hazard. The nurse must recognize this risk and advise the client to use only water-soluble lubricants. Option A is a safe practice; cotton clothing does not generate static electricity like synthetic fabrics do. Option C is safe; keeping the concentrator at a distance is standard. Option D is safe; battery-operated razors are preferred over electrical corded ones around oxygen to reduce spark risks.
</details>

---

### Question 355
A postoperative client is using an incentive spirometer hourly to prevent pneumonia. Which finding best indicates to the nurse that this prophylactic intervention is achieving its intended outcome?

A) The client demonstrates the ability to exhale forcefully into the device.
B) The client reports a marked decrease in abdominal incisional pain.
C) The client maintains clear lung sounds bilaterally in all lobes.
D) The client effectively coughs up thick, green respiratory secretions.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** EVALUATION
**System Focus:** Pneumonia Precautions

The purpose of using an incentive spirometer is to promote deep breathing, fully expand the alveoli, and prevent atelectasis and pneumonia. Clear lung sounds in all lobes indicate that the lungs are fully expanded and free of fluid or consolidation, showing the intervention is effective. Option A is incorrect because the incentive spirometer is used for slow, deep inhalations, not forceful exhalations. Option B is unrelated to the primary goal of the spirometer, though deep breathing might be easier if pain is controlled. Option D indicates an active respiratory infection, not the successful prevention of one.
</details>
### Question 356
A nurse is assisting a client with asthma to use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. What is the correct procedural step for inhaling the medication?

A) Inhale quickly and deeply immediately before pressing the canister.
B) Press the canister, then take a slow, deep breath in for 3 to 5 seconds.
C) Exhale completely after pressing the canister to push the medication into the lungs.
D) Hold the breath for 3 seconds before exhaling rapidly through the nose.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Procedural Step
**System Focus:** Respiratory System (Inhaler technique)

**Rationale:** When using an MDI without a spacer, the correct technique is to exhale completely first, press the canister to release the medication, and simultaneously take a slow, deep breath in through the mouth for 3 to 5 seconds. This ensures the medication is drawn deep into the lungs. The client should then hold their breath for about 10 seconds to allow the medication to settle. Rapid inhalation or exhaling after pressing the canister will prevent the medication from reaching the lower airways. 
</details>

---

### Question 357
The nurse is providing discharge education to a client who will be using continuous nasal oxygen at home. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of oxygen safety?

A) "I can use petroleum jelly on my lips if they get dry from the oxygen."
B) "I should keep my oxygen tank at least 5 feet away from any open flames."
C) "I will wear a cotton sweater instead of my favorite wool sweater while using oxygen."
D) "It is safe to use my electric razor as long as the oxygen is turned down low."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Client Teaching
**System Focus:** Respiratory System (Oxygen safety)

**Rationale:** Cotton clothing is recommended for clients on oxygen therapy because synthetic and wool materials can generate static electricity, which poses a serious fire hazard in an oxygen-rich environment. Petroleum jelly is oil-based and combustible; water-soluble lubricants should be used instead. Oxygen should be kept at least 10 feet (not 5 feet) away from open flames or heat sources. Electric razors pose a risk of sparking and should not be used while oxygen is flowing.
</details>

---

### Question 358
A client admitted with bacterial pneumonia is placed on droplet precautions. Which task is most appropriate for the registered nurse (RN) to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Administering the client's scheduled oral antibiotic.
B) Instructing the client's family on how to properly wear surgical masks.
C) Assessing the client's breath sounds and respiratory rate.
D) Restocking the personal protective equipment (PPE) cart outside the client's room.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Delegation
**System Focus:** Respiratory System (Pneumonia precautions)

**Rationale:** Restocking PPE is a standard, routine task that does not require nursing judgment or assessment, making it entirely appropriate to delegate to a UAP. Administering medications, providing education to the family, and assessing breath sounds fall strictly within the scope of practice of licensed nursing personnel and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 359
The nurse is reviewing the morning vital signs and assessment data for a client recovering from community-acquired pneumonia. Which finding is an expected clinical manifestation of this condition?

A) A productive cough with greenish-yellow sputum.
B) A resting heart rate of 55 beats per minute.
C) A tympanic temperature of 97.6°F (36.4°C).
D) Clear, vesicular breath sounds in all lung fields.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**System Focus:** Respiratory System (Pneumonia)

**Rationale:** A productive cough with purulent (greenish-yellow) sputum is a classic expected finding in bacterial pneumonia due to the infection and inflammation in the lungs. Tachycardia (an elevated heart rate), rather than bradycardia (55 bpm), is expected due to fever and increased work of breathing. A fever is expected, so a normal or low temperature would be atypical during the acute phase. Adventitious breath sounds, such as crackles or rhonchi, are expected with pneumonia, whereas clear breath sounds would indicate resolved or absent disease.
</details>

---

### Question 360
A client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) is using a newly prescribed fluticasone corticosteroid inhaler. Which action by the client places them at the highest risk for a medication-related complication?

A) Storing the inhaler in a cool, dry place away from direct sunlight.
B) Rinsing the mouth with water and spitting it out after each use.
C) Using the corticosteroid inhaler immediately after using a bronchodilator.
D) Failing to wash the mouth out after administering the medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Risk Recognition
**System Focus:** Respiratory System (Inhaler technique)

**Rationale:** Inhaled corticosteroids, such as fluticasone, can alter the local flora of the mouth and throat, placing the client at a high risk for developing oral candidiasis (thrush). Failing to rinse the mouth and spit out the water after using the inhaler significantly increases this risk. Storing the inhaler properly, rinsing and spitting after use, and using a bronchodilator *before* a corticosteroid (to open the airways first) are all correct and safe practices.
</details>
### Question 361
A nurse is teaching a client how to use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) with a spacer. Which instruction should the nurse include in the teaching?

A. Exhale quickly and forcefully immediately after pressing the canister.
B. Inhale quickly and deeply right before the medication is released.
C. Breathe in slowly and deeply after pressing down on the canister.
D. Wash the spacer in hot, soapy water after every single use.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
When using a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) with a spacer, the client should press the canister to release the medication into the spacer, and then take a slow, deep breath to draw the medication into the lungs. Inhaling too quickly (Option B) or forcefully exhaling immediately (Option A) prevents the medication from reaching the lower airways. The spacer should be cleaned regularly, but not necessarily after every single use, and must be completely air-dried before use (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 362
A nurse is setting up oxygen therapy via nasal cannula for a client in a semi-private room. Which procedural safety step is required when administering continuous oxygen?

A. Ensure there are no open flames, sparks, or smoking materials in the room.
B. Apply petroleum jelly to the client's nares to prevent severe dryness.
C. Keep the oxygen flow rate at a minimum of 10 L/min to prevent tube kinking.
D. Store the spare oxygen cylinders lying flat on the floor under the bed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
Oxygen supports combustion, meaning it causes fires to burn hotter and faster. Therefore, ensuring there are no open flames, smoking materials, or sources of sparks in the room is a critical safety step. Petroleum jelly is oil-based and flammable; water-soluble lubricants should be used instead (Option B). A nasal cannula is typically used for 1 to 6 L/min; 10 L/min is inappropriate for this device (Option C). Oxygen cylinders must always be stored securely in an upright position, not flat on the floor, to prevent the valve from breaking and causing a projectile hazard (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 363
The nurse is caring for a client with bacterial pneumonia who is placed on droplet precautions. Which task can the nurse safely delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A. Auscultating the client's lower lung lobes to check for crackles.
B. Teaching the client how to properly use their incentive spirometer.
C. Evaluating the client's oxygen saturation to determine if oxygen can be decreased.
D. Refilling the client's water pitcher while wearing a surgical mask.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation<br>
Refilling a water pitcher is a standard activity of daily living (ADL) that falls within the UAP's scope of practice, provided they wear the appropriate personal protective equipment (a surgical mask) for droplet precautions. Auscultating lung sounds (Option A), providing initial teaching (Option B), and evaluating clinical data (Option C) all require the specialized knowledge and judgment of a registered nurse and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 364
A client is recovering from uncomplicated viral pneumonia. Which respiratory assessment finding should the nurse identify as an expected presentation during the recovery phase?

A. Tracheal deviation shifting to the right side of the neck.
B. A productive cough with clear or white sputum.
C. An oxygen saturation of 85% while breathing room air.
D. Completely absent breath sounds in the bilateral lower lung fields.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected<br>
A productive cough with clear or white sputum is an expected finding in viral pneumonia, as the body works to clear the respiratory tract of secretions. Tracheal deviation is a sign of a tension pneumothorax, which is a medical emergency (Option A). An oxygen saturation of 85% is hypoxia and requires immediate intervention (Option C). Absent breath sounds indicate a complete lack of airflow, such as from a severe pleural effusion, pneumothorax, or obstruction, and are not a normal or expected finding during recovery (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 365
A home health nurse is visiting a client who requires continuous home oxygen therapy. Which observation in the home indicates an immediate environmental risk to the client's safety?

A. The oxygen concentrator is placed firmly against the wall in the corner of the room.
B. The client wears 100% cotton clothing while sitting in the living room.
C. The client uses a heavy wool blanket on their bed at night to stay warm.
D. The client has securely taped the oxygen tubing to the floor to prevent tripping.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Risk Recognition<br>
Wool and synthetic materials can generate static electricity, which can create a spark and act as an ignition source in an oxygen-rich environment. This presents a major fire hazard. Clients on oxygen should use 100% cotton bedding and clothing (Option B). The oxygen concentrator should be kept away from the wall to allow for proper ventilation, making Option A a potential equipment issue, but the wool blanket (Option C) is a more direct and immediate fire risk. Taping down tubing is a good safety measure to prevent falls (Option D).
</details>

---
### Question 366
A nurse is observing a client use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) to administer asthma medication. Which action by the client demonstrates the correct procedural step for this device?

A) Exhaling quickly and forcefully immediately after pressing the canister
B) Shaking the inhaler vigorously for a few seconds before use
C) Holding the inhaler at least 5 inches away from the mouth
D) Taking short, shallow breaths while inhaling the medication

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The correct procedure for using an MDI involves shaking the canister vigorously to properly mix the medication and the propellant. The client should inhale slowly and deeply (not shallowly), hold their breath for about 10 seconds (not exhale quickly), and position the mouthpiece either 1 to 2 inches away from the mouth or directly in the mouth with lips sealed around it.
</details>

---

### Question 367
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client who will be using continuous supplemental oxygen at home. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of home oxygen safety?

A) "I can use petroleum jelly if my lips get dry from the oxygen."
B) "I should keep my oxygen concentrator at least 5 feet away from open flames."
C) "I will use a cotton blanket on my bed instead of a wool one."
D) "I can safely smoke in the same room as long as the oxygen is turned off."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Cotton blankets should be used because wool and synthetic fabrics can generate static electricity, which poses a fire hazard in an oxygen-rich environment. Petroleum jelly is oil-based and flammable; a water-based lubricant should be used instead. Oxygen equipment should be kept at least 10 feet away from open flames. Smoking is never permitted in a home where oxygen is in use, even if the device is temporarily turned off, due to residual oxygen in the air and on clothing.
</details>

### Question 368
The nurse is caring for an older adult client who is recovering from a stroke and has difficulty swallowing (dysphagia). Which action or finding places this client at the highest risk for developing aspiration pneumonia?

A) Elevating the head of the bed to 90 degrees during meals
B) Assisting the client with thorough oral care after eating
C) Encouraging the client to tuck their chin when swallowing
D) Providing regular, thin liquids with the client's meal tray

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D
<strong>Lens:</strong> Risk Recognition
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Thin liquids (like water or regular juice) move very quickly in the mouth and throat, making them the most difficult consistency for a client with dysphagia to control. This greatly increases the risk of the fluid entering the airway (aspiration), which can lead to aspiration pneumonia. Elevating the head of the bed, providing oral care, and using a chin-tuck maneuver are all safe precautions that reduce the risk of aspiration.
</details>

---

### Question 369
A client diagnosed with right-lower lobe pneumonia has been using an incentive spirometer every hour while awake. Which finding indicates to the nurse that this specific respiratory intervention is effective?

A) The client reports a decrease in pleuritic chest wall pain.
B) The client's respiratory rate increases to 24 breaths per minute.
C) The nurse hears clear breath sounds in the right lower lung fields.
D) The client coughs up thick, rust-colored sputum.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<strong>Lens:</strong> Evaluation
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The purpose of incentive spirometry is to promote deep breathing, ensure maximum alveolar lung expansion, and help clear respiratory secretions. Hearing clear breath sounds in the previously affected area indicates that the alveoli are open and secretions have been successfully mobilized, showing the treatment is highly effective. Rust-colored sputum is an expected symptom of pneumonia, but not a direct sign of spirometry effectiveness. Increased respiratory rate is a sign of respiratory distress.
</details>

### Question 370
A client with an acute asthma exacerbation receives a nebulizer treatment of albuterol. Following the treatment, the nurse assesses the client. Which finding is an expected, harmless effect of this medication?

A) Fine tremors in the hands
B) A sudden, severe drop in blood pressure
C) Swelling of the lips and tongue
D) A severe, new-onset headache

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Albuterol is a beta-2 adrenergic agonist used as a short-acting bronchodilator. Because it stimulates the sympathetic nervous system, expected and common side effects include mild fine tremors, tachycardia, and feelings of jitteriness. Swelling of the lips/tongue indicates an allergic reaction (anaphylaxis). Severe drops in blood pressure or sudden severe headaches are unexpected and require prompt medical investigation.
</details>
### Question 371
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client going home with continuous oxygen therapy. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of home oxygen safety?

A. "I will keep my oxygen tank at least 2 feet away from my space heater."
B. "I can use petroleum jelly on my lips if they get dry from the oxygen."
C. "I should place a 'No Smoking' sign on the front door of my home."
D. "I will wear synthetic clothing to help prevent static electricity."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
<b>Lens:</b> Client Teaching<br>
Placing a "No Smoking" sign on the front door alerts visitors and emergency personnel that oxygen is in use, which is a critical fire safety measure. Oxygen supports combustion, meaning it makes fires burn hotter and faster. Space heaters should be kept at least 5 to 10 feet away from oxygen sources (Option A). Petroleum jelly is combustible and should never be used with oxygen; a water-based lubricant should be used instead (Option B). Clients should wear cotton clothing, not synthetic materials, to prevent sparks from static electricity (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 372
A nurse is observing a client use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI). Which action indicates the correct procedural step for preparing and using this device?

A. Shaking the canister vigorously for 3 to 5 seconds before each use.
B. Inhaling rapidly and forcefully while pressing down on the canister.
C. Exhaling immediately after inhaling the medication to clear the lungs.
D. Pressing the canister down fully before bringing the mouthpiece to the lips.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
<b>Lens:</b> Procedural Step<br>
Shaking the MDI canister for 3 to 5 seconds before use is an essential procedural step to ensure the medication and propellant are properly mixed. The client should inhale slowly and deeply, not rapidly (Option B). After inhaling, the client should hold their breath for about 10 seconds to allow the medication to settle in the lungs, not exhale immediately (Option C). The canister should be pressed simultaneously as the client begins to inhale slowly through the mouth, not before bringing it to the lips (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 373
A nurse is caring for a client recovering from bacterial pneumonia. Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Listening to the client's lung sounds after a severe coughing spell.
B. Reminding the client to use the incentive spirometer every hour while awake.
C. Teaching the client how to splint the chest with a pillow when coughing.
D. Adjusting the client's oxygen flow rate based on the morning pulse oximetry reading.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
<b>Lens:</b> Delegation<br>
Unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) can remind or encourage a client to perform tasks that the nurse has already taught, such as using an incentive spirometer. Assessing lung sounds (Option A), teaching chest splinting techniques (Option C), and adjusting oxygen flow rates (Option D) all require nursing judgment, assessment, or initial education, and therefore cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 374
The nurse evaluates a client's understanding of how to use a newly prescribed fluticasone (corticosteroid) inhaler. Which action by the client demonstrates that the teaching has been effective?

A. The client uses the fluticasone inhaler immediately before their albuterol inhaler.
B. The client checks their radial pulse for 60 seconds before taking the medication.
C. The client takes short, shallow breaths while actively using the inhaler device.
D. The client rinses their mouth with water and spits it out after using the inhaler.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
<b>Lens:</b> Evaluation<br>
Fluticasone is an inhaled corticosteroid. Rinsing the mouth with water and spitting it out after use is essential to prevent oral candidiasis (thrush), a common localized side effect of inhaled steroids. If using both a bronchodilator (albuterol) and a steroid (fluticasone), the bronchodilator should be used first to open the airways, allowing the steroid to reach deeper into the lungs (Option A). Checking the pulse is relevant for beta-agonists like albuterol, not specifically for corticosteroids (Option B). The client should take slow, deep breaths, not short, shallow ones (Option C).
</details>

---

### Question 375
A nurse is assessing a client who is receiving supplemental oxygen via a nasal cannula. The nurse observes the client's family member applying a localized moisturizing product to the client's dry nares. Which product poses a severe safety risk and requires immediate nurse intervention?

A. Petroleum jelly
B. Normal saline drops
C. Water-based lubricant
D. Aloe vera gel

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
<b>Lens:</b> Risk Recognition<br>
Petroleum jelly is a petroleum-based, combustible substance. When used in the presence of supplemental oxygen, it creates a significant fire hazard. The nurse must intervene immediately to recognize and remove this risk. Normal saline drops, water-based lubricants, and pure aloe vera are non-combustible and are considered safe alternatives to moisturize dry nasal passages for clients on oxygen therapy (Options B, C, and D).
</details>
### Question 376
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client who will be going home on continuous oxygen therapy via nasal cannula. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of oxygen safety?

A) "I can use a little bit of petroleum jelly if the inside of my nose gets dry."
B) "I will keep my oxygen concentrator at least 5 feet away from the fireplace."
C) "I should wear synthetic clothing to keep warm while using my oxygen."
D) "It is completely safe to use wool blankets on my bed during the winter."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B (Client Teaching lens)

**Reasoning:** Oxygen supports combustion, making things catch fire more easily and burn more rapidly. Clients must keep oxygen delivery systems at least 5 feet (and ideally up to 10 feet) away from any open flames or heat sources, such as fireplaces, gas stoves, or space heaters. 

**Incorrect Options:**
A: Petroleum jelly is a combustible substance and should never be used near oxygen; water-soluble lubricants are recommended instead.
C & D: Synthetic materials and wool can generate static electricity, which could cause a spark. Clients should be taught to use 100% cotton clothing and bedding to prevent static sparks.
</details>

---

### Question 377
The nurse is reviewing the procedural steps for using a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. Which action should the nurse instruct the client to take immediately BEFORE bringing the inhaler mouthpiece to their lips?

A) Press the canister to release the medication into the air.
B) Take a quick, shallow breath inward.
C) Exhale completely to empty the lungs.
D) Hold the breath for at least 10 seconds.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C (Procedural Step lens)

**Reasoning:** To ensure maximum medication delivery to the lower airways, the client must first exhale completely to empty the lungs. Once the lungs are empty, the client brings the mouthpiece to the mouth, presses the canister, and takes a slow, deep inhalation.

**Incorrect Options:**
A: Pressing the canister into the air wastes medication and does not prep the lungs.
B: Breathing in quickly and shallowly prior to using the inhaler prevents the deep inhalation necessary for the medication to reach the lungs.
D: Holding the breath is done *after* inhaling the medication (usually for 10 seconds), not before bringing the mouthpiece to the lips.
</details>

---

### Question 378
A nurse is caring for a client admitted with bacterial pneumonia who has been placed on droplet precautions. Which task is most appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A) Placing a box of surgical masks outside the client's door.
B) Teaching the visiting family members about droplet precautions.
C) Monitoring the client's respiratory rate and effort.
D) Evaluating the client's lung sounds after a coughing episode.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A (Delegation lens)

**Reasoning:** Placing personal protective equipment (PPE), such as surgical masks, outside a client's room is a routine, non-clinical task that falls well within the scope of practice for a UAP. 

**Incorrect Options:**
B: Teaching family members requires nursing knowledge and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
C: Monitoring and assessing a client's respiratory status (rate and effort) is a nursing assessment and cannot be delegated.
D: Evaluating lung sounds is a clinical assessment skill reserved for licensed nursing personnel (RN or LPN/LVN).
</details>

---

### Question 379
A client with asthma has been prescribed two metered-dose inhalers: albuterol (a bronchodilator) and fluticasone (a corticosteroid). The nurse is evaluating the client's understanding of how to use these medications. Which statement by the client indicates that the teaching was successful?

A) "I will use the fluticasone inhaler first to open up my airways."
B) "I only need to rinse my mouth if the medicine tastes bitter."
C) "I will inhale the albuterol and immediately take a puff of the fluticasone."
D) "I will use my albuterol inhaler 5 minutes before my fluticasone inhaler."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D (Evaluation lens)

**Reasoning:** When a client is prescribed both a bronchodilator (albuterol) and a corticosteroid (fluticasone), the bronchodilator should be administered first. This opens the airways, allowing the corticosteroid to reach deeper into the lungs. The client should wait 5 minutes between the two different medications.

**Incorrect Options:**
A: The bronchodilator (albuterol) must be used first, not the corticosteroid.
B: The client must rinse their mouth with water and spit after every use of a corticosteroid inhaler to prevent oral candidiasis (thrush), regardless of taste.
C: The client must wait 5 minutes between taking different inhaled medications to allow the first medication to take effect.
</details>

---

### Question 380
A nurse is assessing a client recently admitted with community-acquired pneumonia. Which clinical manifestation is an EXPECTED finding for this condition?

A) Tracheal deviation to the unaffected side.
B) A productive cough with yellowish-green sputum.
C) High-pitched stridor upon inspiration.
D) A barrel-shaped chest with an increased anterior-posterior diameter.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B (Expected vs Unexpected lens)

**Reasoning:** Pneumonia is an infection that inflames the air sacs in the lungs, which may fill with fluid or purulent material. An expected clinical finding is a productive cough yielding yellowish-green or rust-colored sputum, along with fever, chills, and dyspnea.

**Incorrect Options:**
A: Tracheal deviation is an unexpected, emergency finding that typically indicates a tension pneumothorax, not pneumonia.
C: Stridor is a high-pitched, whistling sound caused by upper airway obstruction (such as choking or anaphylaxis) and is a medical emergency, not an expected finding of pneumonia.
D: A barrel-shaped chest is an expected finding of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) due to chronic air trapping, not an acute infection like pneumonia.
</details>
### Question 381
A nurse is observing a client use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. What procedural step should the nurse instruct the client to take immediately after pressing down on the canister and inhaling the medication?

A. Exhale rapidly through pursed lips.
B. Hold the breath for about 10 seconds.
C. Swallow the medication forcefully.
D. Rinse the mouth immediately with mouthwash.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Procedural Step</strong><br>
Holding the breath for about 10 seconds after inhaling the medication allows the aerosolized drug to settle deep in the airways rather than being immediately exhaled. Exhaling rapidly (Option A) blows the medication out before it can be absorbed. Swallowing (Option C) routes the medication to the stomach rather than the lungs. Rinsing the mouth (Option D) is done after the entire procedure is complete, specifically for corticosteroid inhalers, but is not the immediate next step after inhaling the puff.
</details>

---

### Question 382
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client who will be using home oxygen therapy via nasal cannula. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of oxygen safety?

A. "I will use a synthetic blanket on my bed to keep warm."
B. "I can apply petroleum jelly to my nose if it gets dry."
C. "I will keep the oxygen concentrator at least 5 feet away from the fireplace."
D. "I need to put a 'No Smoking' sign on the front door of my house."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Client Teaching</strong><br>
Placing a "No Smoking" sign on the door alerts visitors and emergency personnel to the fire hazard of oxygen in the home. Oxygen strongly supports combustion. Cotton blankets are preferred over synthetic ones (Option A) to prevent static electricity, which can cause a spark. Petroleum jelly is oil-based and flammable; only water-soluble lubricants should be used for nasal dryness (Option B). Oxygen equipment should be kept at least 10 feet away from open flames, not 5 feet (Option C).
</details>

---

### Question 383
A nurse is caring for a client who is recovering from bacterial pneumonia and requires Droplet Precautions. Which task can the nurse safely delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Instructing the client on how to appropriately use the incentive spirometer.
B. Restocking the personal protective equipment (PPE) cart outside the client's room.
C. Assessing the client's lung sounds after a productive coughing episode.
D. Determining if the client needs an as-needed (PRN) cough suppressant.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Delegation</strong><br>
Restocking personal protective equipment (PPE) is a routine, non-clinical task that does not require nursing judgment, making it safe to delegate to UAP. Instructing the client (Option A), assessing lung sounds (Option C), and determining the need for PRN medication (Option D) all require the specialized knowledge, skill, and judgment of a registered nurse and cannot be delegated to UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 384
A nurse is assessing a client recently admitted with right lower lobe pneumonia. Which clinical finding is an expected manifestation of this condition?

A. Fine crackles heard over the right lower lung field.
B. A sudden drop in oxygen saturation to 82% on room air.
C. Hyperresonance to percussion over the right lower lobe.
D. Tracheal deviation to the left side of the neck.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Expected vs Unexpected</strong><br>
Fine or coarse crackles are an expected finding in pneumonia due to fluid, mucus, and inflammatory exudate filling the alveoli. An oxygen saturation of 82% (Option B) is a life-threatening, unexpected finding requiring immediate emergency intervention. Hyperresonance (Option C) indicates trapped air (as seen in emphysema or pneumothorax), whereas pneumonia typically presents with dullness to percussion due to consolidation. Tracheal deviation (Option D) is a sign of a tension pneumothorax, not pneumonia.
</details>

---

### Question 385
A nurse is evaluating a client's ability to use a dry powder inhaler (DPI) for daily asthma maintenance. Which action by the client indicates that further teaching is necessary?

A. Breathing in rapidly and deeply through the mouthpiece.
B. Keeping the inhaler device in a dry drawer at room temperature.
C. Shaking the inhaler vigorously right before taking the dose.
D. Rinsing the mouth with water after using a corticosteroid DPI.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Evaluation</strong><br>
Shaking a dry powder inhaler (DPI) is incorrect because it can cause the powder to spill out of the device, resulting in an inaccurate dose. Only metered-dose inhalers (MDIs) containing liquid propellant need to be shaken. The client correctly demonstrates breathing in rapidly and deeply (Option A) to pull the powder into the lungs, storing the device away from humidity (Option B) to prevent the powder from clumping, and rinsing the mouth (Option D) to prevent oral candidiasis (thrush).
</details>
### Question 386
A nurse is educating a client who will be discharged with home oxygen therapy. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of oxygen safety?

A. "I can use petroleum jelly on my lips if they get dry from the oxygen."
B. "I am allowed to use woolen blankets on my bed to stay warm at night."
C. "I will make sure my oxygen tank is secured in an upright position."
D. "I can smoke a cigarette as long as I turn the oxygen off first."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. "I will make sure my oxygen tank is secured in an upright position."

**Lens:** Client Teaching

**Rationale:** Oxygen tanks must be secured in an upright position to prevent them from falling, which could cause damage to the valve and result in a dangerous, pressurized leak. Petroleum jelly (Option A) is combustible and should never be used with oxygen; water-based lubricants are recommended. Woolen blankets (Option B) can generate static electricity, which poses a fire hazard in an oxygen-rich environment; cotton blankets should be used instead. Smoking (Option D) is strictly prohibited in the home where oxygen is used, even if the oxygen is turned off, due to the high risk of fire.
</details>

---

### Question 387
The nurse is instructing a client on how to properly use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) for asthma management. Which step should the nurse instruct the client to perform *first*?

A. Vigorously shake the inhaler canister for 3 to 5 seconds.
B. Exhale completely to empty the lungs.
C. Press down on the canister to release the medication.
D. Hold the breath for 10 seconds after inhaling the dose.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. Vigorously shake the inhaler canister for 3 to 5 seconds.

**Lens:** Procedural Step

**Rationale:** The very first step when preparing to use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) is to remove the cap and shake the canister vigorously. This ensures that the medication and the propellant are properly mixed so the correct dose is delivered. After shaking, the client should exhale completely (Option B), place the mouthpiece in their mouth, press down on the canister while inhaling deeply (Option C), and then hold their breath for about 10 seconds (Option D) to allow the medication to settle in the lungs.
</details>

---

### Question 388
A nurse is caring for a client newly admitted with bacterial pneumonia. Based on the client's diagnosis, which clinical finding is expected?

A. Bradycardia
B. Clear, thin sputum
C. Vesicular breath sounds over the affected lobe
D. Elevated oral temperature

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. Elevated oral temperature

**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected

**Rationale:** An elevated temperature (fever) is an expected systemic inflammatory response to an infection like bacterial pneumonia. Tachycardia (not bradycardia) is typically expected due to fever and increased work of breathing. Sputum in bacterial pneumonia is usually thick, discolored (yellow, green, or rust-colored), rather than clear and thin. Vesicular breath sounds are normal breath sounds; over an area of pneumonia consolidation, a nurse would expect to hear crackles or bronchial breath sounds.
</details>

---

### Question 389
A nurse is assigning tasks for a client receiving continuous oxygen therapy via nasal cannula for community-acquired pneumonia. Which task is appropriate to delegate to Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A. Assessing the client's respiratory rate and work of breathing.
B. Checking for skin breakdown around the client's ears and repositioning the tubing.
C. Adjusting the oxygen flow rate based on the client's pulse oximetry reading.
D. Educating the client on the importance of incentive spirometry.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Checking for skin breakdown around the client's ears and repositioning the tubing.

**Lens:** Delegation

**Rationale:** Checking for skin breakdown and assisting with basic comfort measures, such as repositioning oxygen tubing to prevent pressure injuries around the ears, falls within the scope of practice for a UAP. Assessing respiratory status (Option A), adjusting oxygen flow rates (Option C), and providing client education (Option D) require nursing judgment and must be performed by the registered nurse (RN). 
</details>

---

### Question 390
The nurse is evaluating a client's technique for using a dry powder inhaler (DPI). Which action by the client indicates that further teaching is needed?

A. The client inhales rapidly and deeply through the mouthpiece.
B. The client exhales directly into the inhaler mouthpiece before taking the dose.
C. The client holds their breath for 10 seconds after inhaling the medication.
D. The client rinses their mouth with water after using a corticosteroid medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. The client exhales directly into the inhaler mouthpiece before taking the dose.

**Lens:** Evaluation

**Rationale:** Exhaling directly into a dry powder inhaler (DPI) introduces moisture from the breath into the device, which can cause the powder medication to clump and ruin the dose. This action requires correction. The client should exhale away from the device before placing it in their mouth. Inhaling rapidly and deeply (Option A), holding the breath for 10 seconds to allow the drug to settle (Option C), and rinsing the mouth after a corticosteroid to prevent oral candidiasis (Option D) are all correct techniques for DPI administration.
</details>

---
### Question 391
A nurse is educating a client who is being discharged with home continuous oxygen therapy. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I can use petroleum jelly on my lips if they get dry from the oxygen."
B) "I will keep the oxygen tank at least 2 feet away from the space heater."
C) "I will place 'No Smoking' signs on the front door of my house."
D) "I can use synthetic blankets on my bed since they are warmer."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) "I will place 'No Smoking' signs on the front door of my house."

**Lens:** Client Teaching
**System Focus:** Oxygen Safety

**Rationale:** Oxygen is highly combustible and supports fire. Placing "No Smoking" signs on the door alerts visitors and family members of the danger and promotes a safe home environment. Petroleum jelly (Option A) is a flammable, oil-based product and should be avoided; a water-soluble lubricant should be used instead. Oxygen tanks (Option B) should be kept at least 8 to 10 feet away from any heat source. Synthetic and wool blankets (Option D) can generate static electricity, which can ignite in an oxygen-rich environment; cotton blankets are the safe alternative.
</details>

---

### Question 392
A nurse is teaching a client how to use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. Which action should the client take immediately after completely exhaling?

A) Hold the breath for 10 seconds.
B) Place the mouthpiece in the mouth and seal the lips tightly around it.
C) Exhale forcefully through pursed lips.
D) Shake the inhaler vigorously for 30 seconds.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Place the mouthpiece in the mouth and seal the lips tightly around it.

**Lens:** Procedural Step
**System Focus:** Inhaler Technique

**Rationale:** The correct sequence for using an MDI involves shaking the inhaler, exhaling completely to empty the lungs, placing the mouthpiece in the mouth (or holding it 1-2 inches in front of the open mouth), pressing down on the canister while taking a slow, deep breath, and holding the breath for 10 seconds. Option A is done *after* inhaling the medication. Option C is incorrect because the client has already exhaled. Option D should be done *before* exhaling to prepare the medication.
</details>

---

### Question 393
A nurse is caring for a client admitted with bacterial pneumonia who is placed on droplet precautions. Which task is most appropriate for the nurse to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Teaching the client how to properly use the incentive spirometer.
B) Assessing the client's lung sounds for the presence of crackles.
C) Recommending an increase in the client's oxygen flow rate.
D) Ensuring the client has a box of tissues and a trash can near the bed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Ensuring the client has a box of tissues and a trash can near the bed.

**Lens:** Delegation
**System Focus:** Pneumonia Precautions

**Rationale:** Setting up the room with non-clinical supplies such as tissues and a trash can is a routine task that falls safely within the scope of practice for UAP. It aids in infection control without requiring nursing judgment. Teaching (Option A), physical assessment (Option B), and making clinical recommendations (Option C) require the specialized knowledge, skill, and judgment of a registered nurse and cannot be delegated to UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 394
A nurse observes a client using a dry powder inhaler (DPI) for the maintenance treatment of asthma. Which action by the client evaluates as a correct technique?

A) Inhaling deeply and forcefully through the mouthpiece.
B) Shaking the device vigorously right before administration.
C) Breathing out directly into the mouthpiece before inhaling the medication.
D) Using a spacer attachment with the device to improve delivery.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Inhaling deeply and forcefully through the mouthpiece.

**Lens:** Evaluation
**System Focus:** Inhaler Technique

**Rationale:** Dry powder inhalers (DPIs) rely on the force of the client's inhalation to deliver the fine powder into the lungs. Therefore, inhaling deeply, strongly, and forcefully is the correct technique. DPIs do not contain propellant, so they should not be shaken (Option B). Exhaling into the mouthpiece (Option C) introduces moisture, which can clump the powder and ruin the dose. Spacers (Option D) are only used with metered-dose inhalers (MDIs), not DPIs.
</details>

---

### Question 395
A nurse is visiting the home of a client who uses oxygen via nasal cannula at 2 L/min. Which observation in the home presents the greatest safety risk to the client?

A) The client has cotton sheets on the bed.
B) The client is wearing a wool sweater while using the nasal cannula.
C) The oxygen concentrator is plugged directly into a wall outlet.
D) A water-soluble lubricant is stored on the nightstand.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) The client is wearing a wool sweater while using the nasal cannula.

**Lens:** Risk Recognition
**System Focus:** Oxygen Safety

**Rationale:** Wool fabrics, as well as synthetic materials like nylon or polyester, can generate static electricity. In an oxygen-rich environment, a static spark can ignite a fire, posing a severe safety risk to the client. Cotton fabrics (Option A) are safe because they do not easily generate static electricity. Plugging the oxygen concentrator directly into the wall (Option C) is safe and preferred over using extension cords, which can overheat. Water-soluble lubricants (Option D) are safe to use for dry nares, unlike petroleum-based products which are flammable.
</details>
### Question 396
A nurse is observing a client using a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. What is the correct procedural step for the client to take immediately after pressing down on the canister to release the medication?

A) Exhale quickly and forcefully through pursed lips.
B) Hold the breath for at least 10 seconds.
C) Swallow several times to clear the medication from the throat.
D) Inhale rapidly and shallowly through the nose.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> After inhaling the medication from an MDI, the client should hold their breath for 10 seconds (or as long as comfortable). This allows the medication particles to settle deep into the lung airways rather than being immediately exhaled. Inhaling should be slow and deep through the mouth, not rapid through the nose. Exhaling quickly or swallowing immediately reduces the effectiveness of the inhaled dose.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step
</details>

---

### Question 397
A nurse is providing discharge instructions to a client who will be using continuous supplemental oxygen at home. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of safe oxygen use?

A) "I should keep my oxygen concentrator at least 3 feet away from open flames."
B) "I will apply a petroleum-based jelly to my lips if they become dry and cracked."
C) "I will use cotton blankets on my bed instead of wool or synthetic ones."
D) "I can safely store spare oxygen cylinders lying flat on the floor under my bed."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Cotton bedding is recommended because wool and synthetic materials can generate static electricity, which poses a spark hazard in an oxygen-enriched environment. Petroleum jelly is flammable and should be avoided; a water-based lubricant should be used instead. Oxygen equipment should be kept at least 10 to 15 feet away from open flames, not just 3 feet. Oxygen cylinders must always be stored securely in an upright position to prevent damage to the valve.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching
</details>

---

### Question 398
A nurse is caring for a client admitted with community-acquired pneumonia who requires droplet precautions. Which task is most appropriate to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A) Restocking personal protective equipment (PPE) outside the client's room.
B) Teaching the client how to use the incentive spirometer to prevent atelectasis.
C) Assessing the client's breath sounds after a coughing episode.
D) Determining the correct type of isolation signage needed for the client's door.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Restocking PPE is a standard, non-clinical task that does not require nursing judgment and is highly appropriate to delegate to a UAP. Teaching the client, assessing breath sounds, and determining the appropriate infection control precautions fall under the nursing scope of practice and cannot be delegated to unlicensed personnel.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation
</details>

---

### Question 399
A client with bacterial pneumonia is receiving intravenous antibiotics. During the morning assessment, the nurse notes several clinical findings. Which finding is an expected manifestation of pneumonia?

A) A resting heart rate of 55 beats per minute.
B) A capillary refill time of 5 seconds.
C) Clear, thin, and watery sputum production.
D) Fine crackles auscultated in the lung bases.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Fine or coarse crackles are an expected assessment finding in pneumonia due to the accumulation of fluid, inflammatory exudate, and mucus in the alveoli and smaller airways. Tachycardia (elevated heart rate), not bradycardia (heart rate of 55), is expected due to fever and increased work of breathing. Sputum in bacterial pneumonia is typically thick, purulent, or rust-colored, rather than clear and watery. A capillary refill time of 5 seconds is prolonged and unexpected, indicating poor perfusion.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected
</details>

---

### Question 400
The nurse enters the room of a client receiving oxygen therapy via nasal cannula at 4 L/min. Which observation presents the greatest safety risk to the client?

A) The client is using an electric razor to shave their face in bed.
B) The nasal cannula prongs are curving downward into the client's nares.
C) The humidifier bottle attached to the oxygen flow meter is actively bubbling.
D) The oxygen tubing is resting lightly over the top of the client's ears.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The use of an electric razor poses a significant safety risk in an oxygen-enriched environment. Electrical devices can generate a spark, which can ignite a fire. Clients on oxygen should use a safety blade razor instead. Prongs curving downward, tubing over the ears, and a bubbling humidifier (appropriate for flow rates of 4 L/min or higher to prevent mucosal drying) are all correct and safe expected observations.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Risk Recognition
</details>
### Question 401
A nurse is observing a client use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. What procedural step should the nurse instruct the client to perform immediately *after* inhaling the medication?

A) Exhale quickly through the nose.
B) Rinse the mouth with warm water.
C) Hold the breath for 10 seconds.
D) Take another puff immediately.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
Holding the breath for about 10 seconds after inhaling the medication allows the aerosolized medication to settle deep into the lower airways, maximizing its effectiveness. Exhaling quickly prevents the medication from settling. Rinsing the mouth is done after the entire treatment is complete (especially for corticosteroids). If a second puff is prescribed, the client should wait at least 1 minute between puffs.
<b>Lens:</b> Procedural Step
</details>

---

### Question 402
A nurse is providing discharge instructions to a client who will be using continuous home oxygen therapy via nasal cannula. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of oxygen safety?

A) "I should wear cotton clothing to prevent static electricity."
B) "I can use petroleum jelly if my nose gets dry."
C) "It is safe to smoke as long as I stay 3 feet away from the tank."
D) "I will store the oxygen cylinders horizontally under my bed."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
Cotton clothing helps prevent static electricity, which can act as a spark and create a severe fire hazard in an oxygen-rich environment. Synthetic or wool fabrics should be avoided. Petroleum jelly is oil-based and highly flammable; water-soluble lubricants must be used instead. Smoking is absolutely prohibited anywhere near oxygen. Oxygen cylinders should be stored upright and secured, not lying down under a bed.
<b>Lens:</b> Client Teaching
</details>

---

### Question 403
A nurse is caring for a client recovering from bacterial pneumonia. Which task is most appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A) Instructing the client on how to properly use the incentive spirometer.
B) Assessing the client's lung sounds for the presence of crackles.
C) Determining the oxygen flow rate needed to keep saturations above 92%.
D) Assisting the client with frequent oral care.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
Assisting with routine activities of daily living, including frequent oral care, is a standard task within the scope of practice for a UAP. Oral hygiene is a crucial pneumonia precaution to prevent further bacterial growth. Assessing lung sounds, teaching the use of an incentive spirometer, and determining or adjusting oxygen flow rates are nursing responsibilities that require clinical judgment and cannot be delegated.
<b>Lens:</b> Delegation
</details>

---

### Question 404
A nurse is assessing a client who was admitted two days ago with lobar pneumonia. Which of the following findings is an expected manifestation of this condition rather than an unexpected sign of a new complication?

A) A sudden drop in oxygen saturation to 82% on room air.
B) Productive cough with rust-colored sputum.
C) Asymmetrical chest expansion with absent breath sounds on one side.
D) Confusion and a new onset of severe lethargy.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
A productive cough with rust-colored, green, or yellow sputum is a classic, expected clinical manifestation of pneumonia due to the inflammatory response and infection in the lungs. A sudden drop in oxygen saturation, asymmetrical chest expansion (which may indicate a pneumothorax), and new-onset confusion are unexpected findings that indicate deterioration, hypoxia, or severe complications requiring immediate intervention.
<b>Lens:</b> Expected vs Unexpected
</details>

---

### Question 405
A nurse is evaluating a client’s ability to use a dry powder inhaler (DPI). Which action by the client indicates that further teaching is necessary?

A) The client loads the dose into the device before using.
B) The client inhales deeply and forcefully through the mouthpiece.
C) The client shakes the inhaler vigorously before taking the dose.
D) The client breathes out fully away from the inhaler before inhaling.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
Dry powder inhalers (DPIs) should *not* be shaken, as shaking can cause the pre-measured powder dose to spill out of the device. Metered-dose inhalers (MDIs) require shaking to mix the propellant and medication, but DPIs do not. The other actions are correct: the dose must be loaded, the client should breathe out away from the device so moisture doesn't clump the powder, and the inhalation should be deep and forceful to pull the powder into the lungs.
<b>Lens:</b> Evaluation
</details>
### Question 406
A nurse is conducting a home safety evaluation for a client prescribed continuous oxygen therapy via nasal cannula. Which observation by the nurse represents a safety risk?

A. The client uses 100% cotton blankets on the bed.
B. The oxygen concentrator is placed 10 feet away from the fireplace.
C. The client applies petroleum jelly to the nares to prevent dryness.
D. The client stores extra oxygen cylinders in an upright, secured position.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Risk Recognition
<br><br>
<strong>Explanation:</strong> Oxygen supports combustion, making it a fire hazard if near flammable materials. Petroleum jelly is an oil-based, combustible substance and should never be used on the nares or face of a client receiving oxygen therapy. The nurse should recommend a water-soluble lubricant instead. Using cotton blankets (which do not create static electricity like synthetic materials), keeping oxygen at least 10 feet from heat sources, and storing cylinders upright are all correct and safe practices.
</details>

---

### Question 407
A nurse is guiding a client on the correct use of a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) with a spacer. After shaking the inhaler and pressing down on the canister once to release the medication into the spacer, what should the client do next?

A. Exhale forcefully through the mouth into the spacer.
B. Inhale slowly and deeply through the mouth.
C. Hold their breath for a maximum of 2 seconds.
D. Swallow repeatedly to ensure the medication goes down.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step
<br><br>
<strong>Explanation:</strong> After actuating the metered-dose inhaler into the spacer, the next correct procedural step is to inhale the medication slowly and deeply through the mouth. The client should never exhale into the spacer, as this would blow the medication away. Holding the breath for only 2 seconds is not long enough; the breath should ideally be held for 10 seconds to allow the medication to settle in the lungs. Swallowing does not direct the medication into the respiratory tract.
</details>

---

### Question 408
A nurse is providing discharge instructions to a client recovering from bacterial pneumonia. Which statement by the client indicates that the teaching has been effective?

A. "I will restrict my fluid intake so I don't cough as much."
B. "I should stay on strict bed rest for the next two weeks."
C. "I will stop taking my antibiotics once my fever completely goes away."
D. "I will use my incentive spirometer and practice deep breathing every hour."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching
<br><br>
<strong>Explanation:</strong> Using an incentive spirometer and practicing deep breathing exercises hourly while awake are vital pneumonia precautions to help expand the alveoli, clear secretions, and prevent complications like atelectasis. Clients should increase (not restrict) fluid intake to help thin respiratory secretions. Bed rest should be balanced with progressive ambulation to promote lung expansion. Antibiotics must be taken for the entirely prescribed course, even if symptoms like fever resolve.
</details>

---

### Question 409
A client with a respiratory infection is receiving oxygen therapy at 2 L/min via nasal cannula. Which task is appropriate for the registered nurse (RN) to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Assisting the client to a semi-Fowler's position to ease breathing.
B. Increasing the oxygen flow rate if the client reports shortness of breath.
C. Assessing the skin behind the client's ears for pressure ulcers from the tubing.
D. Evaluating the client's need for a humidification device.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation
<br><br>
<strong>Explanation:</strong> Assisting a client with positioning to promote comfort and ease of breathing is a standard, routine task that falls within the UAP's scope of practice. Adjusting oxygen flow rates (which is administering a medication), assessing skin integrity, and evaluating clinical needs all require nursing judgment and must be performed by the RN.
</details>

---

### Question 410
A nurse evaluates a client's ability to self-administer medication using a dry powder inhaler (DPI). Which action by the client requires the nurse to intervene and provide further education?

A. Exhaling completely away from the mouthpiece before inhaling.
