B. Breathing in quickly and forcefully through the mouthpiece.
C. Shaking the device vigorously for several seconds before use.
D. Rinsing the mouth with water after inhaling a corticosteroid medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Evaluation
<br><br>
<strong>Explanation:</strong> A dry powder inhaler (DPI) should *never* be shaken, as shaking can scatter the dry powder medication and result in the client receiving an incorrect dose. Only metered-dose inhalers (MDIs) need to be shaken. Exhaling away from the device, breathing in quickly and forcefully (which is required to pull the powder into the lungs), and rinsing the mouth after corticosteroid use to prevent thrush are all correct expected techniques for a DPI.
</details>
### Question 411
**Lens:** Client Teaching
**System Focus:** Oxygen safety

A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client who will be using continuous home oxygen therapy via nasal cannula. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of home oxygen safety?

A) "I can use petroleum jelly if my nose gets dry from the oxygen."
B) "I will make sure my home has working smoke detectors installed."
C) "I can safely smoke in the house as long as I turn off the oxygen first."
D) "I should keep the oxygen concentrator pushed tightly against the wall to save space."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "I will make sure my home has working smoke detectors installed."

**Rationale:** 
Oxygen vigorously supports combustion, making fire safety a top priority. Having working smoke detectors and fire extinguishers is essential in a home where oxygen is used. Petroleum jelly (Option A) is a flammable, oil-based product and should never be used on the nares of a client receiving oxygen; a water-soluble lubricant should be used instead. Smoking is strictly prohibited in the house and near the client (Option C), even if the oxygen is temporarily turned off, because oxygen can saturate hair and clothing. An oxygen concentrator needs proper airflow to prevent overheating and should be kept away from walls and curtains, not pushed tightly against them (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 412
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**System Focus:** Pneumonia precautions

A nurse is assessing a client who was admitted with community-acquired pneumonia two days ago and is currently receiving antibiotic therapy. Which assessment finding should the nurse recognize as an *expected* symptom of this condition?

A) Inspiratory stridor
B) Tracheal deviation
C) Productive cough with yellowish-green sputum
D) Sudden absence of breath sounds on one side

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Productive cough with yellowish-green sputum

**Rationale:** 
A productive cough with purulent (yellowish-green) sputum, fever, and crackles in the lungs are expected findings in a client with bacterial pneumonia. Inspiratory stridor (Option A) suggests an upper airway obstruction and is a medical emergency. Tracheal deviation (Option B) and a sudden absence of breath sounds on one side (Option D) are signs of a tension pneumothorax, which is an unexpected and life-threatening complication, not an expected symptom of pneumonia.
</details>

---

### Question 413
**Lens:** Procedural Step
**System Focus:** Inhaler technique

A nurse is assisting a client who is learning to use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. What is the correct procedural step the client should take immediately upon pressing down on the inhaler canister?

A) Breathe in slowly and deeply for 3 to 5 seconds.
B) Exhale rapidly through pursed lips.
C) Hold their breath for exactly 30 seconds.
D) Swallow repeatedly to ensure the medication reaches the throat.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Breathe in slowly and deeply for 3 to 5 seconds.

**Rationale:** 
When using an MDI without a spacer, the client should coordinate pressing the canister with breathing in slowly and deeply for 3 to 5 seconds to draw the medication directly into the lungs. The client should exhale fully *before* bringing the inhaler to their mouth (Option B). After inhaling the medication, the client should hold their breath for about 10 seconds to allow the medication to settle in the lungs, not 30 seconds (Option C). Swallowing the medication (Option D) defeats the purpose of the inhaler, as the medication needs to reach the lower airways, not the gastrointestinal tract.
</details>

---

### Question 414
**Lens:** Delegation
**System Focus:** Pneumonia precautions

The registered nurse (RN) is caring for a client recovering from pneumonia who requires frequent pulmonary hygiene. Which task is appropriate for the RN to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client's lung sounds after a coughing episode.
B) Teaching the client how to properly use the incentive spirometer.
C) Adjusting the oxygen flow rate based on morning pulse oximetry readings.
D) Reminding the client to use the incentive spirometer every hour while awake.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Reminding the client to use the incentive spirometer every hour while awake.

**Rationale:** 
Reminding or encouraging a client to perform a previously taught, safe skill—such as using an incentive spirometer or performing deep breathing exercises—is well within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing lung sounds (Option A), providing initial teaching on how to use medical equipment (Option B), and adjusting oxygen flow rates or making clinical decisions based on vital signs (Option C) are all tasks that require nursing judgment and must be performed by the RN or LPN/LVN.
</details>

---

### Question 415
**Lens:** Risk Recognition
**System Focus:** Oxygen safety

A nurse enters the room of a client receiving oxygen therapy at 4 L/min via a simple face mask. Which observation in the client's room presents an immediate safety risk?

A) A 100% cotton blanket is placed on the client's bed.
B) A family member is plugging in a frayed electrical phone charger.
C) A water-soluble lip balm is sitting on the client's bedside table.
D) The oxygen tubing is draped loosely over the back of the client's pillow.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) A family member is plugging in a frayed electrical phone charger.

**Rationale:** 
Oxygen creates a highly combustible environment where fires can ignite easily and burn rapidly. A frayed electrical cord poses a severe sparking hazard and is an immediate fire risk in an oxygen-rich environment. Cotton blankets (Option A) are safe and recommended, whereas wool or synthetic blankets should be avoided due to the risk of static electricity. Water-soluble lip balm (Option C) is safe and recommended over petroleum-based products. Loosely draped oxygen tubing (Option D) does not pose an immediate risk as long as it is not kinked or creating a strangulation hazard.
</details>
### Question 416
A nurse is observing a client use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. Which procedural step should the client perform first to ensure proper medication delivery?

A) Inhale deeply and rapidly before pressing the canister.
B) Shake the inhaler vigorously for 3 to 5 seconds.
C) Hold the breath for 10 seconds after exhaling.
D) Press the canister down while exhaling slowly.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Shaking the inhaler vigorously for 3 to 5 seconds is an essential first procedural step because it mixes the medication evenly with the propellant inside the canister. Exhaling completely should happen before pressing the canister, and inhaling should be slow and deep <em>while</em> pressing the canister (not rapid or before pressing). Holding the breath occurs after the medication has been inhaled to allow it to settle in the lungs.
</details>

---

### Question 417
The nurse is providing discharge teaching to a client going home with continuous nasal oxygen therapy. Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of oxygen safety precautions?

A) "I can use petroleum jelly on my nose if it gets dry."
B) "I will keep my oxygen tank at least 5 feet away from open flames."
C) "I should wear 100% cotton clothing to prevent static electricity."
D) "I can smoke in the same room as long as the oxygen is turned off."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Cotton clothing reduces static electricity, which is important because sparks can easily ignite materials in an oxygen-rich environment. Petroleum jelly (Option A) is a combustible substance and should be avoided; a water-soluble lubricant should be used instead. Oxygen tanks must be kept a minimum of 10 feet away from open flames (Option B). Smoking is never permitted around oxygen tanks or in the home of a client using oxygen due to the severe fire hazard (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 418
A client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with community-acquired pneumonia. The nurse reviews the client's assessment findings. Which finding is an expected clinical manifestation of this respiratory infection?

A) Pleuritic chest pain that worsens with coughing
B) Expectoration of clear, odorless sputum
C) A resting heart rate of 55 beats per minute
D) Normal vesicular lung sounds in the affected lobe

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Pleuritic chest pain (pain with coughing or deep breathing) is an expected finding in a client with pneumonia due to inflammation of the parietal pleura. Sputum in pneumonia is typically purulent, yellow, green, or rust-colored, rather than clear (Option B). Tachycardia, rather than bradycardia (Option C), is expected due to fever, hypoxemia, and increased metabolic demand. Lung sounds over the affected area would likely reveal crackles, rhonchi, or bronchial breath sounds, not normal vesicular sounds (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 419
The registered nurse (RN) is assigning tasks for a client recovering from pneumonia who is receiving continuous oxygen via nasal cannula. Which task is appropriate to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Adjusting the oxygen flow rate based on the client's oxygen saturation reading
B) Assessing the client's lung sounds after a coughing episode
C) Educating the client on how to use the incentive spirometer
D) Assisting the client with frequent oral care to prevent infection

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Assisting a client with oral care is a standard, routine hygiene task that falls well within the scope of practice for a UAP. Frequent oral care helps prevent bacterial overgrowth that can exacerbate pneumonia. Adjusting oxygen flow rates (Option A), assessing lung sounds (Option B), and providing initial client education (Option C) require clinical judgment and are strictly the responsibility of licensed nursing personnel (RN or LPN/LVN depending on the state's scope of practice).
</details>

---

### Question 420
A nurse is caring for an older adult client who is on bed rest following orthopedic surgery. Which assessment finding places this client at the highest risk for developing hospital-acquired pneumonia?

A) Using an incentive spirometer every hour while awake
B) Consuming 2 liters of oral fluids daily
C) Experiencing a depressed or absent gag reflex
D) Performing frequent deep breathing and coughing exercises

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
A depressed or absent gag reflex significantly increases the client's risk for aspiration, which can quickly lead to aspiration pneumonia—a serious form of hospital-acquired pneumonia. Using an incentive spirometer (Option A), adequate hydration to thin secretions (Option B), and deep breathing/coughing exercises (Option D) are all positive, protective interventions that actively reduce the risk of respiratory complications such as atelectasis and pneumonia.
</details>


# SECTION: Neurological System (Count: 140 Questions)


### Question 421
A nurse is educating a community group about the early signs of a stroke using the FAST acronym. Which statement by a participant indicates correct understanding of the "S" in FAST?

A) "It stands for Stomach pain, which means I should check for nausea."
B) "It stands for Speech difficulty, such as slurring words."
C) "It stands for Sensation, meaning a sudden loss of feeling in the toes."
D) "It stands for Seizure, which is always the first sign of a stroke."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong>

<strong>Lens: Client Teaching</strong>
The FAST acronym is a widely used tool for recognizing early signs of a stroke. It stands for Face drooping, Arm weakness, Speech difficulty (such as slurred speech or inability to repeat a simple sentence), and Time to call emergency services. Nausea, altered toe sensation, and seizures are not parts of the standard FAST stroke screening acronym.
</details>

---

### Question 422
A nurse is preparing a room for a newly admitted client with a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which action is an essential procedural step when setting up seizure precautions?

A) Placing a padded tongue blade on the bedside table.
B) Removing the oxygen and suction equipment to provide more space.
C) Ensuring the bed is in the lowest position with the side rails padded.
D) Applying soft wrist restraints securely to the client's bed frame.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong>

<strong>Lens: Procedural Step</strong>
Basic seizure precautions involve ensuring the client's environment is safe to prevent injury during a seizure. This includes keeping the bed in the lowest possible position and padding the side rails. Inserting a tongue blade into the mouth and applying restraints are contraindicated during a seizure as they can cause severe injury. Oxygen and suction equipment should always be readily available in the room, not removed.
</details>

---

### Question 423
A nurse is performing a basic neurological assessment on a client using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). The client opens their eyes spontaneously, is fully oriented to time, place, and person, and obeys all motor commands. What GCS score should the nurse document?

A) 15
B) 12
C) 8
D) 3

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong>

<strong>Lens: Data Interpretation</strong>
The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) evaluates a client's neurological status based on three categories: eye opening, verbal response, and motor response. The maximum score in each category is 4 for spontaneous eye opening, 5 for an oriented verbal response, and 6 for obeying motor commands. A perfect, fully alert score is 15 (4 + 5 + 6). 
</details>

---

### Question 424
A client with a seizure disorder is placed on strict seizure precautions. Which task can the registered nurse (RN) safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Teaching the client's family how to position the client if a seizure occurs.
B) Assessing the client's respiratory status immediately following a seizure.
C) Determining the specific type of seizure the client is experiencing.
D) Placing padded blankets over the side rails of the client's bed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong>

<strong>Lens: Delegation</strong>
Placing pads or blankets on side rails is a standard, non-invasive safety task that does not require nursing judgment, making it safe to delegate to the UAP. Teaching the family, assessing the client's airway, and identifying the type of seizure all require the specialized knowledge and judgment of an RN and cannot be delegated to assistive personnel.
</details>

---

### Question 425
A client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with a suspected right-sided ischemic stroke. Which physical finding is an expected characteristic for this condition?

A) Weakness or paralysis on the left side of the body.
B) Sudden onset of a high-grade fever.
C) Hyperactivity and an unusually fast walking pace.
D) Weakness or paralysis on the right side of the body.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong>

<strong>Lens: Expected vs Unexpected</strong>
Because motor nerve fibers cross over in the medulla of the brainstem, a stroke occurring in the right hemisphere of the brain typically causes motor deficits, such as weakness or paralysis, on the contralateral (opposite) side of the body. Therefore, left-sided weakness is an expected finding for a right-sided stroke. High-grade fevers and hyperactivity are unexpected and not classic signs of a basic stroke.
</details>
### Question 426
A nurse is admitting a client with a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which action is included in the standard setup for seizure precautions?

A. Padding the side rails of the bed.
B. Placing a padded tongue blade at the bedside.
C. Keeping the bed in the highest position.
D. Applying soft wrist restraints to the client.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A.</strong><br>
Padding the side rails of the bed is a standard procedural step for seizure precautions to prevent trauma if the client has a seizure in bed. Placing a tongue blade in the mouth during a seizure is contraindicated as it can cause airway obstruction or dental injury. The bed should be kept in the lowest position, and restraints are strictly contraindicated as they can cause severe injury during a seizure.
<br><br>
<em>Lens: Procedural Step</em>
</details>

---

### Question 427
The nurse is assessing a client for a suspected stroke using the FAST acronym. Which finding is expected to be assessed when checking the "S" in FAST?

A. Sudden severe headache.
B. Slurred speech.
C. Symmetrical smile.
D. Shuffling gait.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B.</strong><br>
The FAST acronym stands for Face drooping, Arm weakness, Speech difficulty, and Time to call 911. The "S" represents Speech difficulty, such as slurred speech, inability to repeat a simple sentence, or difficulty finding words. A symmetrical smile is a normal finding (expected when assessing "F"), and while a headache or abnormal gait can occur with a stroke, they are not represented by the "S" in the FAST acronym.
<br><br>
<em>Lens: Expected vs Unexpected</em>
</details>

---

### Question 428
A nurse is calculating a client's Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) score during a routine neurological check. The client opens their eyes spontaneously, is oriented to person, place, and time, and readily obeys motor commands. What is the client's GCS score?

A. 12
B. 9
C. 15
D. 3

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C.</strong><br>
The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) measures three areas: Eye Opening, Verbal Response, and Motor Response. Spontaneous eye opening scores 4 points. Being fully oriented scores 5 points. Obeying motor commands scores 6 points. Adding these together (4 + 5 + 6) gives a maximum and normal GCS score of 15, indicating the client is fully alert and neurologically intact. 
<br><br>
<em>Lens: Data Interpretation</em>
</details>

---

### Question 429
The registered nurse (RN) is assigning care for a client placed on active seizure precautions. Which task is appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Teaching the client about potential seizure triggers.
B. Assessing the client's neurological status immediately after a seizure.
C. Administering a scheduled oral antiepileptic medication.
D. Setting up oxygen and suction equipment at the bedside.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D.</strong><br>
Setting up standard equipment, such as oxygen and suction at the bedside, is a basic task within the scope of practice for a UAP. Client teaching, neurological assessment, and medication administration require clinical judgment and are strictly the responsibility of the registered nurse or a licensed practical nurse.
<br><br>
<em>Lens: Delegation</em>
</details>

---

### Question 430
A nurse is teaching a community class about recognizing the early signs of a stroke. Which statement by a participant indicates a correct understanding of the "A" in the FAST acronym?

A. "I will check if the person has an altered level of consciousness."
B. "I should ask the person to raise both arms to see if one drifts downward."
C. "I need to ask the person their age to determine their risk."
D. "I should assess if the person's airway is clear."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B.</strong><br>
In the FAST acronym used for public stroke recognition, "A" stands for Arm weakness. The correct action is to ask the person to raise both arms to check if one arm drifts downward or is unable to be raised, which indicates unilateral weakness. While assessing airway and consciousness are important general first aid steps, they are not what "A" stands for in this specific stroke assessment tool.
<br><br>
<em>Lens: Client Teaching</em>
</details>
### Question 431
A nurse is setting up the room for a newly admitted client who has a history of frequent generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which action is an appropriate procedural step when implementing seizure precautions?

A. Placing a hard plastic bite block on the bedside table.
B. Keeping the client's bed in the highest position.
C. Padding the side rails of the client's bed.
D. Securing soft wrist restraints to the bed frame.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Padding the side rails of the client's bed.<br>
**Lens:** Procedural Step<br>
**System Focus:** Seizure Precautions<br>

Padding the side rails is a fundamental step in seizure precautions to protect the client from head trauma and bodily injury during the involuntary movements of a seizure. A bite block or tongue depressor (Option A) should never be placed in a client's mouth during a seizure due to the risk of choking and dental damage. The bed should be kept in the lowest position (Option B), not the highest, to minimize injury risk if the client falls. Restraints (Option D) are strictly contraindicated, as holding down a client actively seizing can cause severe musculoskeletal injuries. 
</details>

---

### Question 432
A nurse is providing education to a community group about recognizing the early signs of a stroke using the FAST acronym. The nurse should teach the group that the "A" in FAST stands for which of the following?

A. Arm weakness or drifting
B. Altered level of consciousness
C. Absent peripheral pulses
D. Aphasia or trouble speaking

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. Arm weakness or drifting<br>
**Lens:** Client Teaching<br>
**System Focus:** Basic Stroke Signs (FAST)<br>

The FAST acronym is a widely used tool for teaching the public to recognize strokes early. It stands for **F**ace drooping, **A**rm weakness, **S**peech difficulty, and **T**ime to call 911. Therefore, "A" refers to arm weakness, where one arm may drift downward when the person tries to raise both arms. Altered consciousness (Option B) and absent pulses (Option C) are not part of the FAST acronym. Aphasia or trouble speaking (Option D) represents the "S" (Speech) in the acronym.
</details>

---

### Question 433
The nurse completes a neurological assessment on a client and calculates the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) score. The client opens their eyes spontaneously, obeys all motor commands, and is fully oriented in conversation. What GCS score should the nurse document?

A. 3
B. 9
C. 12
D. 15

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. 15<br>
**Lens:** Data Interpretation<br>
**System Focus:** GCS Basics<br>

The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) assesses three areas: eye opening (up to 4 points), verbal response (up to 5 points), and motor response (up to 6 points). A fully alert, completely oriented client who opens eyes spontaneously (4) obeys commands (6), and has oriented speech (5) achieves the maximum possible score of 15. A score of 3 (Option A) is the lowest possible score, indicating deep coma or brain death. Scores of 9 and 12 (Options B and C) indicate varying degrees of neurological impairment.
</details>

---

### Question 434
A nurse is caring for a client placed on strict seizure precautions. Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Educating the client's family on what to do if a seizure occurs.
B. Setting up the oxygen and suction equipment at the bedside.
C. Administering oral antiepileptic medications as prescribed.
D. Assessing the client's airway patency after a seizure stops.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Setting up the oxygen and suction equipment at the bedside.<br>
**Lens:** Delegation<br>
**System Focus:** Seizure Precautions<br>

Delegating the setup of standard environmental equipment, such as placing oxygen and suction apparatus at the bedside, is well within the scope of practice for a UAP. Educating the family (Option A), administering medications (Option C), and performing post-seizure clinical assessments (Option D) require the clinical judgment, knowledge, and licensure of a nurse and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 435
A client presents to the emergency department triage desk. During the initial visual assessment, the nurse looks for expected versus unexpected neurological signs. Which finding is considered an unexpected sign requiring immediate stroke protocol activation?

A. Bilateral equal pupil constriction to light.
B. Sudden onset of one-sided facial drooping.
C. Clear, articulate, and appropriate speech.
D. Symmetrical arm movement against resistance.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Sudden onset of one-sided facial drooping.<br>
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected<br>
**System Focus:** Basic Stroke Signs (FAST)<br>

Sudden onset of one-sided facial drooping is an unexpected, abnormal neurological finding and a hallmark sign of a stroke (the "F" in the FAST acronym). It warrants immediate medical evaluation and activation of the stroke protocol. Bilateral equal pupil constriction (Option A), clear speech (Option C), and symmetrical arm movement (Option D) are all expected, normal neurological findings.
</details>
### Question 436
A nurse is setting up the room for a client with a newly diagnosed seizure disorder. Which procedural step is included in standard seizure precautions?

A) Placing a padded tongue blade on the bedside table.
B) Keeping the bed in the highest position to assist with quick assessment.
C) Ensuring suction equipment and oxygen are set up at the bedside.
D) Applying soft wrist restraints to prevent the client from thrashing.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Standard seizure precautions include ensuring that oxygen and functioning suction equipment are readily available at the bedside to help maintain airway patency if a seizure occurs. The bed should be kept in the lowest position to prevent falls, and restraints are strictly contraindicated. Padded tongue blades should never be placed in a seizing client's mouth.
</details>

---

### Question 437
A nurse is providing community education on recognizing the early signs of a stroke using the FAST acronym. Which statement by a participant indicates correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "If I notice someone's speech is slurred, I should call for emergency help."
B) "The 'F' means I should check if the person has a sudden fever."
C) "I should wait a few hours to see if the symptoms go away before calling 911."
D) "The 'A' stands for airway, so I must check their breathing."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The FAST acronym stands for Facial drooping, Arm weakness, Speech difficulties, and Time to call emergency services. Slurred or strange speech is a classic warning sign of a stroke, requiring immediate emergency intervention. The "F" stands for Face, the "A" stands for Arm, and one should never wait to see if stroke symptoms resolve.
</details>

---

### Question 438
The nurse is performing a routine neurological assessment on a healthy, alert young adult client during a physical exam. What total Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) score is an expected finding for this client?

A) 3
B) 8
C) 12
D) 15

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> A Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) score of 15 is the maximum and expected score for a fully alert, responsive, and neurologically intact individual. A score of 8 or less typically indicates a coma, and 3 is the lowest possible score, indicating deep coma or brain death. 
</details>

---

### Question 439
A client begins to experience a tonic-clonic seizure while lying in bed. Which action by the nurse is strongly contraindicated during the seizure event?

A) Turning the client onto their side.
B) Inserting a bite block into the client's mouth.
C) Loosening restrictive clothing around the neck.
D) Moving hard objects away from the client's body.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Contraindication<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Inserting any object (like a bite block, tongue blade, or fingers) into the mouth of a seizing client is strictly contraindicated, as it can cause broken teeth, severe airway obstruction, or physical injury to the nurse. Correct actions include turning the client on their side to prevent aspiration, loosening tight clothing, and clearing the area of hard objects.
</details>

---

### Question 440
A client arrives at the clinic, and the nurse observes that the left side of the client's mouth is severely drooping and the client is unable to lift their left arm. Based on these assessment findings, how should the nurse interpret this data?

A) The client is experiencing a focal motor seizure.
B) The client is exhibiting early signs of Parkinson's disease.
C) The client is displaying classic signs of a stroke.
D) The client is showing symptoms of viral meningitis.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Data Interpretation<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Unilateral facial drooping and unilateral arm weakness are classic, emergent signs of a stroke (as recognized in the FAST acronym). The nurse must interpret these signs as a neurological emergency requiring immediate medical evaluation and intervention.
</details>
### Question 441
A nurse is educating a community group about the early warning signs of a stroke using the FAST acronym. Which statement by a participant indicates a correct understanding of the "S" in FAST?

A) "I should look for severe stomach pain."
B) "I should listen for slurred speech or difficulty speaking."
C) "I should check if the person has a sudden spike in fever."
D) "I should observe for a sudden sensitivity to light."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
In the FAST acronym for stroke recognition, "S" stands for Speech. Slurred speech, difficulty speaking, or an inability to repeat a simple sentence are primary warning signs of a stroke. "F" stands for Face drooping, "A" stands for Arm weakness, and "T" stands for Time to call 911. Stomach pain, fever, and light sensitivity are not part of the FAST acronym.
</details>

---

### Question 442
A nurse is preparing a room for a client who has a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which specific procedural step is most appropriate when setting up seizure precautions in the client's room?

A) Placing a padded tongue blade at the bedside for easy access.
B) Removing the call bell to prevent accidental pulling during a seizure.
C) Applying padding to the side rails of the client's bed.
D) Raising all four side rails and keeping the bed in the highest position.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
Padding the side rails is a standard procedural step for seizure precautions to protect the client from head and limb injury during a seizure. Tongue blades are strictly contraindicated, as inserting objects into the mouth during a seizure can cause broken teeth or airway obstruction. The bed should be kept in the lowest position, and raising all four side rails is often considered a restraint and poses an entrapment risk. The call bell should always be within reach.
</details>

---

### Question 443
The nurse assesses a client's level of consciousness using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). The client opens their eyes spontaneously, obeys motor commands, and is fully oriented and conversing normally. How should the nurse interpret these findings?

A) GCS score of 3, indicating deep coma.
B) GCS score of 9, indicating moderate impairment.
C) GCS score of 12, indicating mild impairment.
D) GCS score of 15, indicating normal neurological function.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Data Interpretation<br>
The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) measures eye opening, verbal response, and motor response. The maximum score is 15, which consists of spontaneous eye opening (4 points), a fully oriented verbal response (5 points), and obeying motor commands (6 points). A score of 15 interprets as a fully alert, responsive, and neurologically intact individual. A score of 3 is the lowest possible score and indicates a deep coma.
</details>

---

### Question 444
A registered nurse (RN) is assigning tasks for a client on seizure precautions. Which task is most appropriate to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Gathering blankets and padding to place on the bed's side rails.
B) Instructing the client on what to do if a seizure aura occurs.
C) Administering a PRN dose of lorazepam during a seizure.
D) Assessing the client's airway and breathing after a seizure stops.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation<br>
The UAP can safely assist with environmental safety measures, such as gathering padding and applying it to the bed's side rails under the nurse's direction. Assessing the airway, administering medications, and providing client teaching are outside the UAP's scope of practice and must be performed by the registered nurse.
</details>

---

### Question 445
A nurse is assessing an older adult client who is recovering from a recent ischemic stroke affecting the right hemisphere of the brain. Based on the client's diagnosis, which finding is an expected clinical manifestation?

A) Complete loss of hearing in both ears.
B) Weakness or paralysis on the left side of the body.
C) A sudden onset of a pinpoint, non-reactive pupil.
D) Weakness or paralysis on the right side of the body.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected<br>
Due to the crossing of motor pathways (decussation) in the brainstem, an injury or stroke in the right hemisphere of the brain typically causes motor deficits—such as weakness (paresis) or paralysis (plegia)—on the contralateral (opposite) side, which is the left side of the body. Weakness on the right side would be expected with a left-hemisphere stroke. Pinpoint pupils or complete bilateral deafness are not standard expected findings of a unilateral ischemic stroke.
</details>
### Question 446
**Lens: Procedural Step**

A nurse is admitting a client with a history of tonic-clonic seizures to the medical-surgical unit. Which of the following is an essential procedural step when setting up the client's room for seizure precautions?

A) Placing the bed in the highest position to allow for quick assessment.  
B) Keeping a hard plastic bite block on the bedside table.  
C) Padding the side rails of the client's bed.  
D) Ensuring all four side rails are kept down at all times to prevent entrapment.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Padding the side rails is a fundamental step in initiating seizure precautions to protect the client from physical trauma during a seizure. The bed should be kept in the lowest position, not the highest. Bite blocks or tongue depressors should never be inserted into a client's mouth during a seizure, as they can cause dental damage or airway obstruction. While keeping four side rails up can be considered a restraint in some contexts, keeping them all down does not provide a safe environment for a client at risk for falls during a seizure; standard precautions typically involve using padded side rails as permitted by facility policy.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step
</details>

### Question 447
**Lens: Client Teaching**

A nurse is teaching a community health class about the early recognition of a stroke using the FAST acronym. The nurse should explain to the participants that the "A" in FAST stands for which of the following?

A) Arm weakness  
B) Airway obstruction  
C) Altered consciousness  
D) Aphasia  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The FAST acronym is a widely used tool for stroke recognition. It stands for Facial drooping, Arm weakness, Speech difficulties, and Time to call emergency services. While aphasia (difficulty speaking) is a sign of a stroke, it falls under the "S" (Speech) component of the acronym.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching
</details>

### Question 448
**Lens: Data Interpretation**

A nurse is calculating the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) score for a client who sustained a minor head injury. The nurse observes that the client's eyes remain closed until the nurse says their name loudly, at which point the client opens them. How should the nurse score the "Eye Opening" component of the GCS?

A) 4  
B) 3  
C) 2  
D) 1  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The Glasgow Coma Scale evaluates three components: Eye Opening, Verbal Response, and Motor Response. For the Eye Opening component, a score of 4 is given for spontaneous opening, 3 for opening to sound/speech, 2 for opening to pressure/pain, and 1 for no response. Because the client opened their eyes to the sound of their name, the correct score for this component is 3.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Data Interpretation
</details>

### Question 449
**Lens: Delegation**

A client with epilepsy is admitted to the unit and placed on seizure precautions. Which task is appropriate for the registered nurse (RN) to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client for signs of a pre-seizure aura.  
B) Documenting the duration and physical characteristics of a seizure.  
C) Administering a scheduled oral antiepileptic medication.  
D) Ensuring functioning oxygen and suction equipment are at the bedside.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The UAP can be delegated the task of gathering and setting up standard environmental safety equipment, such as ensuring oxygen and suction are present in the room. Assessing for an aura, evaluating or documenting the clinical characteristics of a seizure, and administering medications are strictly within the scope of practice of the licensed nurse and cannot be delegated to a UAP.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation
</details>

### Question 450
**Lens: Expected vs Unexpected**

A nurse is performing a neurological assessment on a neurologically intact, healthy adult client presenting for a routine physical exam. Which Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) score is expected for this client?

A) 9  
B) 12  
C) 15  
D) 3  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C <br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The maximum and expected Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) score for a healthy, fully awake, and oriented client is 15. This score means the client exhibits spontaneous eye opening (4), is fully oriented verbally (5), and obeys motor commands (6). A score of 3 is the lowest possible score, indicating a deep coma or unresponsiveness.<br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected
</details>
### Question 451
**Lens: Procedural Step**

A nurse is admitting a client with a known history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures to the medical-surgical unit. Which procedural step is most appropriate when preparing the client's room for safety?

A) Place a padded tongue blade on the bedside table for easy access.
B) Keep the bed in the lowest position with the side rails padded.
C) Ensure four-point restraints are attached to the bed frame.
D) Remove the suction equipment from the room to prevent accidental injury.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Details</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** B) Keep the bed in the lowest position with the side rails padded.

**Rationale:** Standard seizure precautions include keeping the bed in the lowest position to prevent falls and padding the side rails to prevent physical trauma during motor activity. Placing objects in the mouth, such as a tongue blade, is contraindicated during a seizure. Restraints should never be used during a seizure as they can cause severe musculoskeletal injury. Suction equipment should be set up and readily available in the room, not removed, to clear the airway of secretions after the seizure.
</details>

---

### Question 452
**Lens: Client Teaching**

A nurse is educating a community senior group on how to quickly recognize the signs of a stroke using the "FAST" acronym. The nurse should teach the group that the "S" stands for which of the following?

A) Severe headache
B) Sudden numbness
C) Speech difficulty
D) Symmetrical smile

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Details</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** C) Speech difficulty

**Rationale:** The "FAST" acronym is a simple tool used to educate the public on early stroke recognition. It stands for **F**ace drooping, **A**rm weakness, **S**peech difficulty (slurred speech or inability to speak), and **T**ime to call emergency services. While sudden numbness and severe headache can be signs of a stroke, they are not what the "S" in FAST represents. A symmetrical smile is a normal finding, whereas an asymmetrical smile represents facial drooping.
</details>

---

### Question 453
**Lens: Data Interpretation**

The nurse is assessing a client's level of consciousness using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). During the assessment, the client opens their eyes spontaneously, is fully oriented to person, place, and time, and obeys all motor commands. How should the nurse interpret these assessment data?

A) A GCS score of 15, indicating normal neurological functioning.
B) A GCS score of 9, indicating moderate brain injury.
C) A GCS score of 3, indicating a deep coma.
D) A GCS score of 12, indicating mild cognitive impairment.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Details</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** A) A GCS score of 15, indicating normal neurological functioning.

**Rationale:** The maximum and normal score on the Glasgow Coma Scale is 15. It is calculated by adding the scores of three categories: Eye Opening (spontaneous = 4), Verbal Response (oriented = 5), and Motor Response (obeys commands = 6). 4 + 5 + 6 = 15. A score of 3 is the lowest possible score, indicating a deep coma or unresponsiveness.
</details>

---

### Question 454
**Lens: Contraindication**

A client unexpectedly begins to experience a generalized tonic-clonic seizure while in bed. The nurse rushes to the bedside to assist. Which action by the nurse is strictly contraindicated during the active phase of the seizure?

A) Loosening tight clothing around the client's neck.
B) Turning the client onto their side to prevent aspiration.
C) Inserting a bite block into the client's mouth.
D) Moving nearby hard objects away from the client.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Details</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** C) Inserting a bite block into the client's mouth.

**Rationale:** Inserting any object (such as a bite block, tongue blade, or fingers) into a client's mouth during an active seizure is strictly contraindicated. It can cause severe dental damage, broken objects can become an airway obstruction, and the caregiver's fingers can be severely bitten. Safe interventions include loosening restrictive clothing, turning the client on their side to maintain an open airway and prevent aspiration, and clearing the area of hard objects to prevent injury.
</details>

---

### Question 455
**Lens: Delegation**

A client with a history of seizures is being admitted to the unit. The registered nurse (RN) is working with an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) to prepare the room. Which task is appropriate for the RN to delegate to the UAP?

A) Assessing the client's pupillary reaction to light.
B) Educating the client on the importance of taking antiepileptic medications.
C) Evaluating the client's swallow reflex before providing a cup of water.
D) Obtaining padded side rail covers from the supply room and placing them on the bed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Details</strong></summary>
**Correct Answer:** D) Obtaining padded side rail covers from the supply room and placing them on the bed.

**Rationale:** Gathering supplies and setting up basic environmental safety measures, such as placing padded side rail covers on a bed for seizure precautions, are within the scope of practice for a UAP. The RN cannot delegate any tasks that fall under the nursing process, which includes assessing (pupillary reaction), evaluating (swallow reflex), or educating (medication teaching).
</details>
### Question 456
**Lens:** Procedural Step
**System Focus:** Seizure Precautions

A nurse is admitting a client with a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. What procedural step must the nurse include when setting up seizure precautions in the client's room?

A. Placing padded tongue blades on the bedside table.
B. Keeping the bed in the highest position to facilitate easy assessment.
C. Ensuring functioning suction equipment and oxygen are at the bedside.
D. Applying soft wrist restraints to prevent injury during a seizure.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Setting up seizure precautions requires ensuring that suction equipment and oxygen are readily available at the bedside to help maintain airway patency if a seizure occurs. Placing anything in the client's mouth (like a tongue blade) during a seizure is strictly contraindicated as it can cause injury or airway obstruction. The bed should always be kept in the lowest position with pads on the side rails to prevent falls and trauma. Restraints are contraindicated because tying down a convulsing client can result in severe musculoskeletal injuries.
</details>

---

### Question 457
**Lens:** Client Teaching
**System Focus:** Basic Stroke Signs (FAST)

The nurse is teaching a community health class about recognizing the early signs of a stroke using the "FAST" acronym. Which statement by a participant indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A. "The 'A' stands for altered airway, meaning the person will stop breathing."
B. "The 'F' stands for facial drooping, usually noticed when the person tries to smile."
C. "The 'S' stands for sudden sweating, which is a key sign of a stroke."
D. "The 'T' stands for taking the person's temperature immediately."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** The "FAST" acronym is an easy tool used to identify basic stroke signs. "F" stands for Facial drooping (often seen when the person is asked to smile). "A" stands for Arm weakness (one arm drifts downward when both are raised). "S" stands for Speech difficulties (slurred speech or difficulty repeating a simple sentence). "T" stands for Time to call emergency services immediately if any of these signs are observed.
</details>

---

### Question 458
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
**System Focus:** GCS Basics

The nurse is assessing a client's level of consciousness using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). The client opens their eyes spontaneously when the nurse enters the room, perfectly obeys all motor commands, and is fully oriented and conversing. Which GCS score should the nurse document?

A. 15
B. 12
C. 9
D. 3

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** A GCS score of 15 is the maximum possible score and indicates a fully alert, awake, and oriented client. The score is calculated based on three categories: Eye opening (spontaneous = 4), Verbal response (oriented = 5), and Motor response (obeys commands = 6). A score of 3 is the lowest possible score, indicating deep coma or brain death.
</details>

### Question 459
**Lens:** Delegation
**System Focus:** Basic Stroke Signs/Care

A client who recently experienced an ischemic stroke has residual left-sided weakness. Which task is appropriate for the registered nurse (RN) to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Assessing the client's gag reflex before providing a cup of water.
B. Teaching the client how to use a walker for stability while ambulating.
C. Evaluating the client's grip strength in the left arm compared to the right.
D. Assisting the client with eating their meal while sitting upright in a chair.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Assisting a stable client with eating is a standard activity of daily living (ADL) that falls within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing reflexes, evaluating muscle strength, and providing initial teaching require nursing judgment, specialized knowledge, and assessment skills, which are solely the responsibility of the RN and cannot be delegated.
</details>

### Question 460
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**System Focus:** Seizure Precautions/Post-Seizure Care

The nurse is observing a client who just experienced a 2-minute generalized tonic-clonic seizure. Which finding should the nurse identify as an expected finding during the immediate postictal phase?

A. Deep sleep, confusion, and lethargy.
B. Spastic muscle contractions and jaw clenching.
C. Expressing a clear, detailed memory of the seizure event.
D. Sudden hyperactivity, pacing, and talkativeness.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** The postictal phase is the recovery period immediately following a seizure. It is expected for the client to be very tired, lethargic, confused, or fall into a deep sleep as the brain recovers from the intense electrical activity. Spastic muscle contractions are expected during the ictal (active) phase of a seizure, not the postictal phase. Clients rarely have any memory of the seizure itself, and hyperactivity is highly unexpected following a generalized tonic-clonic episode.
</details>
### Question 461
**Procedural Step**

A nurse is observing a client who begins to have a tonic-clonic seizure while sitting in a chair. Which action is the most appropriate procedural step for the nurse to take first?

A) Insert a tongue blade into the client's mouth.
B) Restrain the client's arms to prevent injury.
C) Carefully guide the client to the floor.
D) Leave the room to call for emergency help.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Carefully guide the client to the floor.

**Rationale:** The primary goal during a seizure is to protect the client from injury. If a client is in a chair or standing, the nurse should safely guide them to the floor, clear the area of hazardous objects, and protect their head. The nurse should never insert anything into the mouth (A) or restrain the client (B), as these actions can cause severe trauma. The nurse should stay with the client and call for help, rather than leaving the room (D).
</details>

---

### Question 462
**Client Teaching**

The nurse is teaching the family of a client at high risk for a stroke about the "FAST" acronym. Which statement by a family member indicates an accurate understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should look for a fever, asthma, sweating, and tremors."
B) "I will check for facial drooping, arm weakness, and slurred speech."
C) "FAST means to check their feet, arms, stomach, and toes."
D) "It reminds me to act fast by giving the person water immediately."

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "I will check for facial drooping, arm weakness, and slurred speech."

**Rationale:** The "FAST" acronym is a widely used tool for stroke recognition. It stands for Facial drooping, Arm weakness, Speech difficulties, and Time to call emergency services. Recognizing these signs allows for prompt medical intervention. The other statements do not accurately reflect the FAST stroke assessment tool. Furthermore, giving water to someone experiencing a stroke (D) is contraindicated due to the high risk of dysphagia and aspiration.
</details>

---

### Question 463
**Data Interpretation**

The nurse is reviewing the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) score of a client admitted to the neurological unit. The client is fully awake, alert, oriented to person, place, and time, and follows all motor commands. Which GCS score accurately reflects this client's neurological status?

A) 15
B) 3
C) 9
D) 12

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) 15

**Rationale:** The Glasgow Coma Scale assesses eye-opening (max 4), verbal response (max 5), and motor response (max 6). A fully alert, oriented client who follows commands will achieve the maximum score of 15. A score of 3 is the lowest possible score, indicating deep coma or brain death. Scores of 9 and 12 indicate moderate to mild cognitive impairment or altered levels of consciousness.
</details>

---

### Question 464
**Delegation**

A nurse is admitting a client with a history of frequent seizures. Which task related to implementing seizure precautions can the nurse safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assess the client's risk factors for having a seizure.
B) Teach the client how to use the call bell when feeling an aura.
C) Administer the scheduled oral anti-seizure medication.
D) Place padded covers on the client's bed side rails.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Place padded covers on the client's bed side rails.

**Rationale:** Placing padding on side rails is a standard, non-invasive safety procedure that falls within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing risk factors (A), providing client teaching (B), and administering medication (C) all require nursing judgment and a nursing license; therefore, they cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 465
**Expected vs Unexpected**

The nurse is assessing an older adult client during a routine clinic visit. Which finding is an unexpected neurological sign that requires immediate evaluation for a potential stroke?

A) Symmetrical smiling when asked to show teeth.
B) Sudden onset of left-sided facial drooping.
C) Clear and understandable speech.
D) Equal grip strength in both hands.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Sudden onset of left-sided facial drooping.

**Rationale:** Facial drooping on one side is an unexpected, abnormal finding and a classic cardinal sign of a stroke (represented by the 'F' in the FAST acronym). Symmetrical smiling, clear speech, and equal grip strength are all expected, normal neurological findings indicating intact cranial nerve and motor function.
</details>

---
### Question 466
**Lens:** Procedural Step
A nurse is admitting a client with a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures to the medical-surgical unit. What is an essential procedural step when setting up the client's room for seizure precautions?

A) Placing a padded tongue blade at the bedside.
B) Keeping the bed in the highest possible position.
C) Ensuring the suction equipment is set up and functioning.
D) Removing all pillows from the client's room.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** When implementing seizure precautions, the nurse must ensure that functioning suction equipment and oxygen are readily available at the bedside to help maintain a patent airway and clear secretions during or after a seizure. A padded tongue blade (Option A) should never be placed in a client's mouth during a seizure, as it can cause airway obstruction or dental trauma. The bed should be kept in the lowest position (Option B) to prevent fall injuries. While a pillow might be placed under the client's head for protection during a seizure, removing all pillows from the room (Option D) is unnecessary and decreases client comfort.
</details>

---

### Question 467
**Lens:** Client Teaching
A nurse is teaching a community health class about the early warning signs of a stroke using the FAST acronym. The nurse should explain to the participants that the "S" in FAST stands for which of the following?

A) Severe sweating
B) Sensations of tingling
C) Sudden syncope
D) Speech difficulty

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** The FAST acronym is a simple tool used to educate the public on recognizing stroke symptoms quickly. It stands for Face drooping, Arm weakness, Speech difficulty, and Time to call emergency services. Severe sweating, tingling, and syncope (Options A, B, and C) can occur with various medical conditions but are not what the "S" represents in the FAST mnemonic.
</details>

### Question 468
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
The nurse is assessing the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) of a client who experienced a minor head injury. Upon entering the room, the nurse notes that the client has their eyes wide open and is looking around the room. Which score should the nurse assign for the eye-opening component of the GCS?

A) 2
B) 4
C) 3
D) 1

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** The Glasgow Coma Scale assesses three parameters: eye opening, verbal response, and motor response. For the eye-opening component, a score of 4 is given for spontaneous eye opening. A score of 3 is given if eyes open to speech/sound, 2 if eyes open to pain, and 1 if there is no eye opening. Because the client opened their eyes spontaneously, the correct score is 4.
</details>

### Question 469
**Lens:** Delegation

A) Padding the client's bed rails.
B) Assessing the client's aura before a seizure.
C) Administering PRN diazepam if a seizure occurs.
D) Documenting the duration and characteristics of a seizure.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** Padding the bed rails is a routine environmental safety measure that falls within the scope of practice for a UAP. Assessing a client (Option B), administering medication (Option C), and documenting clinical characteristics of a medical event (Option D) require nursing judgment and are strictly the responsibility of the registered nurse.
</details>

### Question 470
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
A nurse is performing a routine assessment on a client resting in bed. Which of the following findings is considered an unexpected neurological sign that requires immediate medical evaluation for a possible stroke?

A) The client displays equal grip strength in both hands.
B) The client exhibits a new, unilateral facial droop when smiling.
C) The client reports a mild headache after reading for two hours.
D) The client demonstrates intact pupillary constriction to light.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** A new, unilateral facial droop is an unexpected, abnormal neurological finding and a classic warning sign of a stroke (the "F" in the FAST acronym). It requires immediate intervention. Equal grip strength (Option A) and intact pupillary constriction (Option D) are expected, normal neurological findings. A mild headache after reading (Option C) is a common, expected occurrence due to eye strain and does not immediately suggest a stroke.
</details>

### Question 471
**Lens: Procedural Step**

A nurse is admitting a client with a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which procedural step should the nurse take when setting up seizure precautions in the client's room?

A. Place a padded tongue blade at the bedside.
B. Request an order for four-point physical restraints.
C. Pad the side rails of the client's bed.
D. Keep the bed in the highest position to ease nursing access.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Procedural Step<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Padding the side rails is a standard procedural step for seizure precautions to protect the client from head and extremity injuries during a seizure. Placing anything in the mouth (like a tongue blade) or using physical restraints is strictly contraindicated as it can cause severe injury. The bed should always be kept in the lowest possible position, not the highest.
</details>

### Question 472
**Lens: Client Teaching**

A nurse is educating a community group about recognizing the early signs of a stroke using the "FAST" acronym. Which statement by a participant indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A. "The 'A' stands for arm weakness, so I should check if one arm drifts downward."
B. "The 'F' stands for foot drop, so I need to watch for tripping while walking."
C. "The 'S' stands for stomach pain, which is an early warning sign."
D. "The 'T' stands for temperature, so I should check for a sudden high fever."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The "FAST" acronym stands for Facial drooping, Arm weakness, Speech difficulties, and Time to call 911. Checking for arm weakness by asking the person to raise both arms and observing if one drifts downward is a correct application of this tool. Foot drop, stomach pain, and temperature are not part of the FAST acronym.
</details>

### Question 473
**Lens: Data Interpretation**

The nurse assesses a client's level of consciousness using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). The client opens their eyes spontaneously, is oriented to person, place, and time, and obeys motor commands. How should the nurse interpret these findings?

A. The client has a GCS score of 3, indicating a deep coma.
B. The client has a GCS score of 9, indicating moderate neurological impairment.
C. The client has a GCS score of 12, indicating mild neurological impairment.
D. The client has a GCS score of 15, indicating normal neurological function.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Data Interpretation<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) measures eye opening, verbal response, and motor response. Spontaneous eye opening scores 4, an oriented verbal response scores 5, and obeying motor commands scores 6. Together, these equal a perfect score of 15, which indicates a fully alert and neurologically intact client. A score of 3 is the lowest possible score, indicating a coma.
</details>

### Question 474
**Lens: Delegation**

A client with epilepsy is placed on seizure precautions. Which task is most appropriate for the registered nurse (RN) to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

</details>

### Question 475
**Lens: Expected vs Unexpected**

A nurse is observing a client who was admitted for monitoring after a minor fall. Which assessment finding is an unexpected neurological change that requires the nurse to immediately activate a stroke code?

A. The client complains of mild localized pain at the fall site.
B. The client develops sudden slurred speech and facial drooping.
C. The client is easily awakened from a nap to answer questions.
D. The client's pupils are equal, round, and reactive to light.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B <br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Sudden slurred speech and facial drooping are classic, unexpected findings indicative of an acute stroke (matching the FAST acronym). This requires immediate medical intervention to determine if the client is a candidate for thrombolytic therapy. Mild pain at the fall site, being easily arousable, and having equal and reactive pupils are expected or normal findings.
</details>
### Question 476
A nurse is educating a community group about recognizing the early signs of a stroke using the FAST acronym. What does the "A" stand for in this acronym?

A) Airway maintenance 
B) Ambulation difficulty
C) Arm weakness
D) Aphasia recognition

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Arm weakness

**Lens:** Client Teaching
**System Focus:** Basic Stroke signs (FAST)

**Rationale:** The FAST acronym is a simple tool used to teach the public how to recognize the sudden signs of a stroke. It stands for **F**ace drooping, **A**rm weakness, **S**peech difficulty, and **T**ime to call emergency services (911). While airway, ambulation, and aphasia are all relevant to neurological assessments, "Arm weakness" is the specific phrase used for the "A" in the FAST acronym to keep public education simple and actionable.
</details>

### Question 477
A client in a hospital bed begins to experience a tonic-clonic seizure. What is the nurse's immediate action to protect the client from injury?

A) Insert a padded tongue blade into the client's mouth to prevent biting the tongue.
B) Turn the client to the side and loosen any restrictive clothing around the neck.
C) Hold the client's arms and legs firmly to prevent them from flailing and striking the bed.
D) Elevate the head of the bed to 90 degrees to promote optimal oxygenation.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Turn the client to the side and loosen any restrictive clothing around the neck.

**Lens:** Procedural Step
**System Focus:** Seizure Precautions

**Rationale:** During a seizure, the nurse's priority is client safety and airway protection. Turning the client to the side allows saliva and any emesis to drain out of the mouth, preventing aspiration. Loosening restrictive clothing helps maintain an open airway. The nurse should never restrain a seizing client (which can cause musculoskeletal injuries) or insert anything into the mouth (which can cause dental or airway trauma). 
</details>

---

### Question 478
The nurse is assessing a client's level of consciousness using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). Upon entering the room, the nurse notes that the client opens their eyes spontaneously without any verbal prompt. What score should the nurse document for the "Eye Opening" component of the GCS?

A) 1
B) 2
C) 3
D) 4

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) 4

**Lens:** Data Interpretation
**System Focus:** GCS Basics

**Rationale:** The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) evaluates three categories: Eye Opening, Verbal Response, and Motor Response. For the Eye Opening component, the scores are: 4 points for spontaneous eye opening, 3 points for opening eyes to speech/sound, 2 points for opening eyes to a painful stimulus, and 1 point for no eye opening. Because the client opens their eyes spontaneously, they receive a score of 4.
</details>

### Question 479
The registered nurse (RN) is assigning tasks for a client who is on strict seizure precautions. Which task is most appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Ensure the bed is in the lowest position and the side rails are padded.
B) Educate the client about the importance of calling for help before standing up.
C) Assess the client's pupil size and level of consciousness every 4 hours.
D) Administer prescribed supplemental oxygen if a seizure occurs.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Ensure the bed is in the lowest position and the side rails are padded.

**Lens:** Delegation
**System Focus:** Seizure Precautions

**Rationale:** The UAP can safely perform standard environmental safety measures, such as keeping the bed in the lowest position and ensuring protective pads are secured on the side rails. Client education, neurological assessments, and the administration of oxygen or medications require nursing judgment and must be performed by the RN or LPN/VN.
</details>

### Question 480
A client has just finished experiencing a generalized tonic-clonic seizure that lasted 2 minutes. The nurse is observing the client immediately afterward. Which finding is an expected characteristic of this postictal phase?

A) Immediate return to a normal baseline level of awareness
B) Pinpoint pupils and shallow, rapid respirations
C) Drowsiness, confusion, and a desire to sleep deeply
D) Sudden outbursts of severe aggression and shouting

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Drowsiness, confusion, and a desire to sleep deeply

**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**System Focus:** Seizure Precautions / Postictal state

**Rationale:** The postictal phase is the recovery period immediately following a generalized seizure. During this time, it is highly expected for the client to experience drowsiness, confusion, fatigue, and a deep sleep, as the brain recovers from the intense electrical activity. An immediate return to baseline awareness is unexpected after a tonic-clonic seizure. Pinpoint pupils and shallow respirations are more commonly associated with opioid toxicity, not the postictal state. While mild agitation can sometimes occur due to confusion, severe aggression and shouting are not typical expected findings.
</details>
### Question 481
A nurse is preparing a room for a client with a history of generalized seizures. Which procedural step is essential for the nurse to include in the room setup?

A) Placing a padded tongue blade at the bedside.
B) Securing padded blankets or commercial pads to the bed rails.
C) Ensuring a mechanical ventilator is available in the room.
D) Keeping the bed in the highest position for easy access.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Procedural Step
**Rationale:** Padding the side rails is an essential step in setting up seizure precautions to prevent bodily injury if a seizure occurs while the client is in bed. The bed should always be kept in the lowest position, not the highest. Placing a tongue blade at the bedside is an outdated and unsafe practice, and a mechanical ventilator is not standard for basic seizure precautions (though suction and oxygen should be available).
</details>

### Question 482

B) Swallow impairment

### Question 483
The nurse is documenting a client's Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) score. The nurse notes that the client opens their eyes spontaneously, obeys motor commands, and is fully oriented. Which total GCS score should the nurse document?

D) 3

**Correct Answer:** C

Spontaneous eye opening scores a 4, being fully oriented scores a 5 for verbal response, and obeying commands scores a 6 for motor response. Combined, these indicate a perfectly normal GCS score of 15.
</details>

### Question 484
A client with epilepsy begins to have a tonic-clonic seizure while in bed. Which action by the nurse is strongly contraindicated?

A) Turning the client gently onto their side.
B) Loosening restrictive clothing around the neck.
C) Moving nearby hard objects away from the bed.
D) Placing a padded bite block between the client's teeth.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Contraindication
**Rationale:** It is strictly contraindicated to place anything—including a bite block, tongue blade, or fingers—into the mouth of a client experiencing a seizure. Doing so can cause severe dental damage, airway obstruction, or injury to the nurse. Turning the client on their side, loosening clothing, and clearing hard objects are all correct safety interventions.
</details>

### Question 485

### Question 486
**Lens:** Procedural Step
A nurse is preparing a room for a newly admitted client who requires strict seizure precautions. Which of the following is the most appropriate step for the nurse to take when setting up the room?

A) Tape a padded tongue blade to the wall above the headboard. 
B) Ensure the bed is in the lowest position with padded side rails. 
C) Secure soft wrist restraints to the bed frame to prevent thrashing. 
D) Keep the room completely dark and the door closed at all times.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B 
**Rationale:** Standard seizure precautions include keeping the bed in the lowest possible position and padding the side rails to prevent trauma if a seizure occurs. Nothing should ever be placed in the client's mouth (such as a tongue blade) during a seizure, making option A incorrect. Restraints (option C) are contraindicated because they can cause severe injury during the involuntary muscle contractions of a seizure. Keeping the room completely dark (option D) is an unnecessary safety hazard, though reducing excessive stimulation is helpful. 
</details>

### Question 494
A client begins having a tonic-clonic seizure while lying in bed. To protect the client's airway and prevent injury, which procedural step should the nurse take immediately?

A. Insert a padded tongue blade into the client's mouth.
B. Restrain the client's arms and legs.
C. Elevate the head of the bed to a high-Fowler's position.
D. Turn the client onto their side.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Procedural Step</strong><br>
Turning the client onto their side (the recovery position) is a crucial procedural step during a seizure. It helps keep the airway clear and prevents the aspiration of saliva or vomitus. Option A is contraindicated; nothing should ever be placed inside the mouth of a seizing client, as it can cause choking or severe dental trauma. Option B is contraindicated because restraining actively seizing limbs can cause bone fractures or muscle injuries. Option C is unsafe as the client may slump over or fall; laying them flat on their side is the safest position.
</details>

---

### Question 495
The nurse is assessing a client brought to the emergency department for a suspected stroke. When applying the "FAST" assessment tool, which of the following is an expected clinical finding associated with this screening?

A. Facial drooping on one side
B. Chest pain that radiates to the jaw
C. Bilateral pupil dilation
D. Sudden shortness of breath

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Expected vs Unexpected</strong><br>
Facial drooping (usually unilateral) is an expected neurological deficit assessed in a client experiencing a stroke, representing the "F" in the FAST acronym. Option B is an expected finding of a myocardial infarction (heart attack). Option C can indicate various conditions (such as drug use, severe hypoxia, or brain death) but is not a primary screening sign on the FAST tool. Option D is typically associated with respiratory or cardiac distress, such as a pulmonary embolism or heart failure, rather than a primary FAST stroke sign.
</details>
### Question 496
A nurse is preparing the room for a newly admitted client who has a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which procedural step should the nurse take to ensure the client's safety?

A) Place a padded tongue blade on the bedside table.
B) Ensure the bed is equipped with padded side rails.
C) Keep the bed in the highest position to facilitate airway access.
D) Apply soft wrist restraints to prevent injury during a seizure.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Padding the side rails is a standard seizure precaution to prevent the client from suffering trauma or injury if they strike the rails during a seizure. Placing objects in the mouth, such as a tongue blade, is contraindicated. The bed should be kept in the lowest position, and restraints should never be used on a client during a seizure, as they can cause severe injury. 
<br><em>Lens: Procedural Step</em>
</details>
