### Question 497
A nurse is teaching a community health class about how to quickly recognize the warning signs of a stroke using the "FAST" acronym. The nurse explains that the "A" in this acronym stands for which of the following?

A) Airway patency
B) Altered alertness
C) Arm weakness
D) Aphasia severity

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
The "FAST" acronym stands for Face drooping, Arm weakness, Speech difficulty, and Time to call 911. Teaching the public this simple acronym helps in the rapid identification and treatment of strokes, which is crucial for minimizing brain damage.
<br><em>Lens: Client Teaching</em>
</details>

---

### Question 498
The nurse is performing a neurological assessment on a client and calculates the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) score. The client opens their eyes spontaneously, is fully oriented to person, place, and time, and obeys motor commands. Which GCS score is expected for this client?

A) 3
B) 8
C) 12
D) 15

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
The Glasgow Coma Scale assesses eye-opening (up to 4 points), verbal response (up to 5 points), and motor response (up to 6 points). A client who is fully alert, oriented, and responsive will receive the maximum score of 15. The lowest possible score is 3, which indicates deep coma. 
<br><em>Lens: Expected vs Unexpected</em>
</details>

---

### Question 499
A nurse is working with an Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP) to care for a client placed on strict seizure precautions. Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the UAP?

A) Placing the required suction equipment and oxygen tubing at the bedside.
B) Assessing the client's neurological status every 4 hours.
C) Educating the client's family on what to do if a seizure occurs.
D) Administering the client's scheduled oral anticonvulsant medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Delegating the setup of basic safety equipment, such as gathering and placing suction canisters and oxygen tubing at the bedside, is within the scope of practice for a UAP. Assessing neurological status, providing client education, and administering medications are strictly nursing responsibilities.
<br><em>Lens: Delegation</em>
</details>

### Question 500
A nurse is observing a client eating breakfast in the dining room. Which sudden finding is a strong clinical indicator of a potential stroke, requiring immediate medical intervention?

A) The client asks for a different type of juice.
B) The client sneezes multiple times in a row.
C) The client suddenly develops a drooping smile on the right side.
D) The client complains of mild indigestion after drinking coffee.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
A sudden drooping on one side of the face (the "F" in the FAST acronym) is a hallmark warning sign of a stroke. The nurse must recognize this risk immediately and activate emergency stroke protocols. Sneezing, indigestion, or changing drink preferences are not signs of a stroke.
<br><em>Lens: Risk Recognition</em>
</details>
### Question 501
A nurse is preparing a room for a client with a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which procedural step is essential for implementing standard seizure precautions?

A) Positioning the bed in the highest position.
B) Applying soft wrist restraints to the client.
C) Padding the side rails of the client's bed.
D) Placing a padded tongue blade on the bedside table.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Padding the side rails of the client's bed.

**Rationale:** 
- **C is correct:** Padding the side rails is a standard procedural step in seizure precautions to prevent physical injury if the client begins to thrash during a seizure.
- **A is incorrect:** The bed should be kept in the *lowest* position, not the highest, to minimize the risk of injury from falls.
- **B is incorrect:** Restraints should never be applied to a client during a seizure, as restraining forceful movements can cause fractures and muscle damage. 
- **D is incorrect:** Nothing should ever be placed in the client's mouth (including padded tongue blades) during a seizure due to the risk of choking and broken teeth.

**Lens:** Procedural Step
</details>

---

### Question 502
The nurse is teaching a community health class about the "FAST" acronym for early stroke recognition. Which statement by a participant indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "F stands for faintness, meaning the person feels dizzy."
B) "A stands for arms, meaning I should check if one arm drifts downward."
C) "S stands for stomach, meaning severe nausea is a common sign."
D) "T stands for temperature, meaning I should check for a sudden fever."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "A stands for arms, meaning I should check if one arm drifts downward."

**Rationale:** 
- **B is correct:** In the FAST acronym, "A" stands for Arms. The person is asked to raise both arms to see if one arm drifts downward, which indicates unilateral weakness.
- **A is incorrect:** "F" stands for Face, specifically looking for an uneven smile or facial drooping, not faintness.
- **C is incorrect:** "S" stands for Speech, observing for slurred speech or difficulty speaking, not stomach/nausea.
- **D is incorrect:** "T" stands for Time, meaning it is time to call 911 immediately if any of these signs are observed, not temperature.

**Lens:** Client Teaching
</details>

---

### Question 503
The nurse is assessing a client's neurological status using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). The client opens their eyes spontaneously, easily obeys commands to move their arms, and is completely oriented to person, place, and time. What total GCS score should the nurse document?

A) 15
B) 12
C) 9
D) 3

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) 15

**Rationale:** 
- **A is correct:** The Glasgow Coma Scale evaluates three responses: Eye opening (max 4), Verbal response (max 5), and Motor response (max 6). A fully alert, oriented, and cooperative client scores the maximum of 15 points (4 + 5 + 6 = 15).
- **B & C are incorrect:** These scores indicate mild to moderate neurological impairment, which does not match the client's fully alert and oriented presentation.
- **D is incorrect:** A score of 3 is the lowest possible score on the GCS, indicating deep coma and a complete lack of responsiveness.

**Lens:** Data Interpretation
</details>

---

### Question 504
A client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit on strict seizure precautions. Which task is most appropriate for the registered nurse (RN) to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client's neurological status immediately after a seizure.
B) Explaining the purpose of the seizure precautions to the client's family.
C) Administering a scheduled oral antiepileptic medication.
D) Ensuring the bedside suction and oxygen equipment are set up.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Ensuring the bedside suction and oxygen equipment are set up.

**Rationale:** 
- **D is correct:** Setting up standard equipment at the bedside, such as oxygen and suction tubing, is a routine, non-invasive task that falls well within the UAP's scope of practice.
- **A is incorrect:** Assessing a client requires nursing judgment and must be performed by the RN.
- **B is incorrect:** Providing education is the responsibility of the RN and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
- **C is incorrect:** Administering medications is outside the scope of practice for a standard UAP and must be done by a licensed nurse.

**Lens:** Delegation
</details>

---

### Question 505
A nurse is assessing a client who is suspected of having a left-sided ischemic stroke. Based on the understanding of basic brain anatomy, which finding is an expected clinical manifestation for this client?

A) Right-sided arm weakness.
B) Left-sided leg paralysis.
C) Sudden high fever.
D) Chest pain radiating to the jaw.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Right-sided arm weakness.

**Rationale:** 
- **A is correct:** Motor nerve fibers cross over (decussate) in the medulla. Therefore, a stroke in the left hemisphere of the brain will typically cause motor deficits, such as weakness or paralysis, on the *right* side of the body.
- **B is incorrect:** A left-sided stroke would cause right-sided deficits, not left-sided deficits.
- **C is incorrect:** A sudden high fever is a sign of infection, not a primary expected manifestation of a basic ischemic stroke.
- **D is incorrect:** Chest pain radiating to the jaw is a classic sign of a myocardial infarction (heart attack), not a stroke.

**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
</details>
### Question 506
A nurse is preparing a hospital room for a client who has a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which action is an appropriate procedural step when setting up seizure precautions for this client?

A) Place soft limb restraints on the bed frame in case of a seizure.
B) Keep a padded tongue blade on the bedside table.
C) Ensure bedside oxygen and suction equipment are set up and functioning.
D) Maintain the client's bed in the highest position to allow for easy access.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Procedural Step
**Rationale:** Setting up bedside oxygen and suction is a standard procedural step for seizure precautions, as the airway may become compromised during or immediately following a seizure due to secretions or altered breathing patterns. Restraints (A) should never be used during a seizure as they can cause severe injury. Padded tongue blades (B) are no longer recommended because forcing an object into a seizing client's mouth can break their teeth or block the airway. The bed should be kept in the lowest, not highest, position (D) to prevent injury if the client falls.
</details>

### Question 507
A nurse is educating a community group about the early warning signs of a stroke using the "FAST" acronym. The nurse should teach the group that the "S" in FAST stands for which of the following?

A) Sudden seizures
B) Slurred speech
C) Severe sweating
D) Slowed swallowing

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Client Teaching
**Rationale:** The FAST acronym is used to educate the public on quickly recognizing basic stroke symptoms: Facial drooping, Arm weakness, Speech difficulties (such as slurred speech), and Time to call emergency services. Seizures, sweating, and swallowing are not represented by the "S" in this widely taught tool.
</details>

### Question 508
The nurse is assessing the neurological status of a newly admitted client using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). When the nurse walks into the room, the client's eyes are closed, but they open immediately when the nurse says the client's name. How should the nurse score the "eye-opening" component of the GCS?

A) 1 - No response
B) 2 - To pain
C) 3 - To speech
D) 4 - Spontaneous

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
**Rationale:** The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) evaluates eye opening on a scale of 1 to 4. Opening eyes to a verbal command or when the name is called is scored as a 3 ("To speech" or "To sound"). A score of 4 would mean the eyes open spontaneously without any stimulation. A score of 2 requires a painful stimulus, and a score of 1 indicates no eye opening at all.
</details>

### Question 509
A client with epilepsy has just experienced a generalized tonic-clonic seizure that lasted 2 minutes. The seizure activity has now stopped. Which assessment finding is an expected characteristic of the postictal phase?

A) Sudden onset of a severe, localized headache
B) Extreme physical combativeness and paranoia
C) Bilateral pupil dilation that is unresponsive to light
D) A period of deep sleep and transient confusion

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**Rationale:** The postictal phase is the recovery period immediately following a seizure. It is highly expected for clients to be profoundly tired, fall into a deep sleep, and experience transient confusion, lethargy, or disorientation upon waking. A severe localized headache, extreme paranoia, or fixed and dilated pupils are unexpected findings that warrant immediate further neurological investigation.
</details>

---

### Question 510
The nurse is planning care for a client admitted with a recent stroke who is also on strict seizure precautions. Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Placing protective padding on the client's bed rails
B) Assessing the client's level of consciousness using the GCS
C) Administering a PRN dose of oral lorazepam for muscle spasms
D) Educating the client's family on how to protect the airway during a seizure

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Delegation
**Rationale:** Placing protective pads on the bed rails is a routine, non-invasive task that ensures environmental safety and is entirely within the scope of practice for a UAP. Assessing neurological status (B), administering medications (C), and providing client or family education (D) all require the clinical judgment and specialized knowledge of a licensed nurse and cannot strictly be delegated to a UAP.
</details>
### Question 511
**Lens:** Procedural Step
A nurse is preparing a room for a client newly admitted with a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which procedural step is essential when setting up seizure precautions for this client?

A) Placing a rigid bite block on the bedside table
B) Keeping the bed in the highest position to facilitate rapid assessment
C) Verifying that oxygen and suction equipment are set up and functioning
D) Securing all four side rails in the raised and locked position

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Rationale:** A critical procedural step in setting up seizure precautions is ensuring that oxygen and suction equipment are present, assembled, and functioning at the bedside. This allows the nurse to quickly clear the airway and administer oxygen if the client experiences a seizure. Placing a bite block in a client's mouth during a seizure is an outdated and unsafe practice (Option A). The bed should be kept in the lowest position, not the highest, to minimize injury risk if the client falls (Option B). Raising all four side rails is generally considered a physical restraint and can increase the risk of injury; typically, 2 to 3 rails are padded and raised depending on facility policy (Option D).

</details>

### Question 512
**Lens:** Client Teaching
A nurse is conducting a health education class at a local community center about identifying the early signs of a stroke. The nurse teaches the acronym "FAST." What does the "A" in this acronym represent?

A) Arm weakness
B) Airway patency
C) Altered mental status
D) Apical pulse

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Rationale:** The "FAST" acronym is widely used to teach the public how to recognize a stroke quickly. It stands for **F**acial drooping, **A**rm weakness, **S**peech difficulties, and **T**ime to call emergency services. While assessing the airway, mental status, and pulse are important nursing assessments, they are not part of the basic FAST acronym taught to the public for quick stroke recognition.

</details>

### Question 513
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
The nurse is assessing the neurological status of a client using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). As the nurse walks into the room, the client spontaneously opens their eyes and looks at the nurse. How should the nurse interpret and score the "Eye Opening" component of the GCS?

A) 1
B) 2
C) 3
D) 4

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Rationale:** The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) assesses three categories: Eye Opening, Verbal Response, and Motor Response. For the Eye Opening component, the maximum score is 4, which is given when the client opens their eyes spontaneously. A score of 3 is given for eye opening to verbal command, 2 for eye opening to pain, and 1 for no eye opening.

</details>

### Question 514
**Lens:** Contraindication
A nurse enters a client's room and witnesses the client experiencing a generalized tonic-clonic seizure in bed. Which action by the nurse is contraindicated?

A) Noting the exact time the seizure started
B) Restraining the client's limbs to prevent them from hitting the bed frame
C) Placing a soft pillow or folded blanket under the client's head
D) Turning the client onto their side to protect the airway

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Rationale:** Restraining a client's limbs during a seizure is strictly contraindicated. Attempting to hold the client down can result in severe musculoskeletal injuries, including fractures or dislocations. The correct actions are to protect the client from injury by clearing the area, placing something soft under their head (Option C), turning them onto their side to prevent aspiration (Option D), and timing the seizure (Option A).

</details>

---

### Question 515
**Lens:** Delegation
A client with epilepsy is admitted to the medical-surgical unit and placed on strict seizure precautions. Which task is appropriate for the registered nurse (RN) to delegate to unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Placing protective padding on the side rails of the client's bed
B) Determining the client's baseline Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) score
C) Documenting the exact movements and duration observed during a seizure
D) Explaining the purpose of the bedside suction equipment to the family

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Rationale:** Placing protective padding on the side rails is a standard safety procedure that does not require nursing assessment or clinical judgment, making it safe and appropriate to delegate to a UAP. Determining a GCS score (Option B) requires nursing assessment. Documenting the clinical characteristics of a seizure (Option C) requires nursing observation and evaluation. Explaining the purpose of medical equipment (Option D) falls under client education, which is the responsibility of the RN.

</details>
### Question 516
**Lens:** Procedural Step
A nurse is preparing a room for a newly admitted client who has a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which procedural action must the nurse take to ensure proper seizure precautions are in place?

A. Place a padded tongue blade on the bedside table.
B. Secure all four extremities to the bed frame with soft restraints.
C. Ensure functioning oxygen and suction equipment are at the bedside.
D. Keep the bed in the highest position to allow for easy access.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Ensure functioning oxygen and suction equipment are at the bedside.

**Rationale:** 
Standard seizure precautions include having functioning oxygen and suction equipment readily available at the bedside to maintain a patent airway and prevent aspiration if the client vomits or has excessive secretions during a seizure. Tongue blades (padded or unpadded) should never be placed in a client's mouth during a seizure due to the risk of dental damage or airway obstruction. Restraints should never be used during a seizure, as they can cause severe musculoskeletal injury. The bed should be kept in the lowest position, not the highest, to minimize the risk of injury if the client falls.

</details>

---

### Question 517
**Lens:** Client Teaching
A nurse is teaching the family of a client with hypertension about recognizing the early signs of a stroke using the "FAST" mnemonic. Which statement by a family member indicates a correct understanding of the "A" in FAST?

A. "I will check if one of their arms drifts downward when they try to raise both."
B. "I will ask them what day it is to see if they are experiencing amnesia."
C. "I will check their airway to ensure they are breathing properly."
D. "I will ask them to walk a straight line to check their alignment."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. "I will check if one of their arms drifts downward when they try to raise both."

**Rationale:** 
The "FAST" mnemonic is a tool used to quickly identify signs of a stroke. "A" stands for Arms (or Arm weakness). The person should be asked to raise both arms; if one arm drifts downward or cannot be raised, it is a key sign of a stroke. The other options (amnesia, airway, alignment) do not reflect the "A" in the FAST stroke assessment tool, which focuses on Face, Arms, Speech, and Time.

</details>

### Question 518
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
The nurse is assessing a client's level of consciousness using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). When the nurse walks into the client's room, the client immediately opens their eyes and looks at the nurse without being spoken to or touched. How should the nurse score the "eye opening" response?

A. 1
B. 2
C. 3
D. 4

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. 4

**Rationale:** 
The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) eye-opening response is scored from 1 to 4. A score of 4 indicates spontaneous eye opening, which is an expected, normal finding when a person is awake and alert. A score of 3 indicates eye opening to speech or verbal command. A score of 2 indicates eye opening to pain. A score of 1 indicates no eye opening at all.

</details>

### Question 519
**Lens:** Delegation
A nurse is caring for a client who requires strict seizure precautions. Which task is most appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Teach the client's family what to do if a seizure occurs.
B. Pad the side rails of the client's bed.
C. Assess the client's neurological status every 4 hours.
D. Administer the scheduled oral antiseizure medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Pad the side rails of the client's bed.

**Rationale:** 
Padding the side rails is a safe, routine, and standardized task that falls within the scope of practice for unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) when setting up a room for seizure precautions. The registered nurse cannot delegate teaching, nursing assessment (such as neurological checks), or medication administration to a UAP, as these require nursing judgment and a licensed scope of practice.

</details>

### Question 520
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
A nurse is observing a client eating lunch in the dining room. The client suddenly drops their fork, begins to slur their words, and develops a noticeable droop on the right side of their mouth. Based on the "FAST" assessment tool, which neurological event should the nurse suspect the client is experiencing?

A. An acute stroke
B. Hypoglycemia
C. Bell's palsy
D. A generalized seizure

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. An acute stroke

**Rationale:** 
The client is exhibiting sudden facial drooping, arm weakness (dropping the fork), and slurred speech. These perfectly align with the "F" (Face), "A" (Arms), and "S" (Speech) components of the FAST mnemonic, strongly indicating an acute stroke. While Bell's palsy causes facial drooping, it does not typically cause sudden slurred speech and arm weakness. Hypoglycemia can mimic stroke symptoms but is not the primary interpretation of classic FAST signs without a blood glucose check. A generalized seizure typically presents with altered consciousness and rhythmic jerking or stiffening.

</details>
### Question 521
A nurse is admitting a client with a history of frequent generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which action is the most appropriate for the nurse to take when preparing the client's room for seizure precautions?

A) Placing padded tongue blades on the bedside table for easy access.
B) Ensuring the bed is in the lowest position with the side rails padded.
C) Keeping all four side rails raised at all times to prevent falls.
D) Providing soft wrist restraints at the bedside to use during a seizure.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Ensuring the bed is in the lowest position with the side rails padded.<br><br>
**Lens:** Procedural Step<br>
**Rationale:** Standard seizure precautions include keeping the bed in the lowest position, placing a mat on the floor (if appropriate), and padding the side rails to prevent injury if the client experiences a seizure. Using padded tongue blades (Option A) is an outdated and unsafe practice, as inserting anything into the mouth during a seizure can cause dental damage or airway obstruction. Keeping all four side rails up (Option C) is considered a physical restraint and can be dangerous if the client attempts to climb over them. Restraints (Option D) should never be used during a seizure, as restraining actively contracting muscles can cause severe musculoskeletal injury.
</details>

### Question 522
The nurse is conducting a community education class about the early recognition of a stroke using the FAST acronym. Which statement by a participant indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "F stands for faintness, so I should check if the person feels dizzy."
B) "A means checking the airway to see if they are breathing."
C) "S stands for speech, meaning I should listen for slurred words."
D) "T means taking the person's temperature immediately."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) "S stands for speech, meaning I should listen for slurred words."<br><br>
**Lens:** Client Teaching<br>
**Rationale:** The FAST acronym stands for Facial drooping, Arm weakness, Speech difficulties, and Time to call emergency services. Checking for slurred speech or difficulty speaking represents the "S" in FAST. "F" stands for facial drooping, not faintness (Option A). "A" stands for arm weakness, not airway (Option B). "T" stands for time to call 911 immediately, not taking a temperature (Option D).
</details>

### Question 523
The nurse is assessing a client's level of consciousness using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). The client opens their eyes spontaneously, is fully oriented to person, place, and time, and obeys all motor commands. What GCS score should the nurse document for this client?

A) 15
B) 12
C) 8
D) 3

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) 15<br><br>
**Lens:** Data Interpretation<br>
**Rationale:** The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) measures three areas: eye opening (up to 4 points), verbal response (up to 5 points), and motor response (up to 6 points). Spontaneous eye opening scores a 4, being fully oriented scores a 5, and obeying motor commands scores a 6. The total score is 15 (4+5+6), which is the maximum score indicating a fully alert and neurologically intact client. The minimum possible score is 3 (Option D), indicating deep coma or brain death. Scores of 8 or less generally indicate a coma.
</details>

### Question 524
A client diagnosed with epilepsy is placed on strict seizure precautions. Which task can the registered nurse safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Instructing the client on when to push their call light if they feel an aura.
B) Assessing the client's neurological status and level of consciousness.
C) Administering a scheduled dose of oral antiepileptic medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Placing suction equipment and an oxygen flowmeter at the bedside.<br><br>
**Lens:** Delegation<br>
**Rationale:** The UAP can safely perform standard environmental and room setup tasks, such as gathering and placing suction equipment and oxygen at the bedside for seizure precautions. Teaching the client (Option A), assessing neurological status (Option B), and administering medications (Option C) are all within the scope of practice of licensed nursing staff and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

### Question 525
A nurse is observing a client who was recently admitted with a suspected acute ischemic stroke. Based on basic stroke symptom recognition, which of the following is an expected neurological finding for this condition?

A) Sudden onset of a high-grade fever and chills.
B) Unilateral weakness of the arm and leg.
C) Bilateral lower extremity pitting edema.
D) A widespread, intensely itchy macular rash.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Unilateral weakness of the arm and leg.<br><br>
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected<br>
**Rationale:** Unilateral weakness (weakness on one side of the body) is a classic, expected sign of a stroke, corresponding to the "A" (Arm weakness) in the FAST acronym. High-grade fever (Option A) is typical of an infection. Bilateral lower extremity edema (Option C) is associated with cardiovascular or renal issues, such as heart failure. An itchy rash (Option D) is an allergic or dermatological response, not an expected stroke symptom.
</details>
### Question 526
A nurse is initiating seizure precautions for a client admitted with new-onset epilepsy. Which procedural step is most appropriate to ensure client safety in the room?

A) Placing a padded tongue blade on the bedside table.
B) Keeping the bed in the highest position for easy access.
C) Padding the side rails of the client's bed.
D) Securing soft wrist restraints to the bed frame.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Procedural Step

**Rationale:** Padding the side rails is a standard procedural step in seizure precautions to prevent trauma if the client has a seizure while in bed. The bed should always be kept in the lowest position (making option B incorrect). Restraints are contraindicated because they can cause fractures or soft tissue injury during the uncontrollable muscle contractions of a seizure (making option D incorrect). Using a tongue blade is an outdated and dangerous practice that is no longer recommended due to the risk of oral trauma and airway obstruction (making option A incorrect).
</details>

### Question 527
The nurse is conducting a community health education session on stroke awareness. The nurse teaches the acronym "FAST" to help attendees recognize early signs of a stroke. Which statement by an attendee indicates a correct understanding of the "S" in FAST?

A) "It reminds me to check for slurred speech or difficulty speaking."
B) "It stands for severe stomach pain that occurs suddenly."
C) "It means I should look for a sudden drop in systolic blood pressure."
D) "It tells me to check for stiffness in the neck and spine."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Client Teaching

**Rationale:** In the FAST acronym for stroke recognition, "S" stands for Speech difficulty (such as slurred speech or the inability to speak or repeat a simple sentence). The other letters stand for Face drooping (F), Arm weakness (A), and Time to call emergency services (T). Options B, C, and D do not represent the FAST acronym. 
</details>

---

### Question 528
The nurse is assessing a client's level of consciousness using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). The client opens their eyes spontaneously, is oriented to person, place, and time, and obeys motor commands. How should the nurse interpret these findings?

A) The client has a GCS score of 10, indicating moderate impairment.
B) The client has a GCS score of 15, indicating a fully alert and oriented state.
C) The client has a GCS score of 3, indicating a deep coma.
D) The client has a GCS score of 12, indicating mild confusion.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Data Interpretation

**Rationale:** The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) evaluates three areas: eye opening (up to 4 points), verbal response (up to 5 points), and motor response (up to 6 points). Spontaneous eye opening is 4 points, being oriented is 5 points, and obeying commands is 6 points. 4 + 5 + 6 = 15. A maximum score of 15 indicates a fully alert and neurologically intact client. The minimum score is 3, which indicates a deep coma.
</details>

---

### Question 529
A client with a history of tonic-clonic seizures begins to actively seize in their bed. Which action by the nurse is strongly contraindicated during the seizure event?

A) Loosening restrictive clothing around the client's neck.
B) Turning the client onto their side to protect the airway.
C) Moving nearby hard objects away from the client.
D) Inserting a firm plastic airway into the client's mouth.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Contraindication

**Rationale:** Inserting any object (including an airway, tongue depressor, or fingers) into the mouth of a client actively having a seizure is strictly contraindicated. This action can cause severe trauma to the teeth, gums, or airway, and poses a bite risk to the nurse. Options A, B, and C are all appropriate and safe interventions during an active seizure to maintain airway patency and prevent injury.
</details>

---

### Question 530
A nurse is performing a neurological assessment on a client who was admitted 24 hours ago with a confirmed right-sided ischemic stroke. Which clinical finding is expected for this client?

A) Paralysis on the right side of the face.
B) Complete loss of hearing in both ears.
C) Weakness or paralysis on the left side of the body.
D) Symmetrical movement of both arms and legs.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected

**Rationale:** Motor nerve tracts cross over (decussate) in the medulla of the brainstem. Therefore, a stroke in the right hemisphere of the brain typically produces expected contralateral (opposite side) motor deficits, such as weakness (hemiparesis) or paralysis (hemiplegia) on the left side of the body. Right-sided paralysis would be expected in a left-sided stroke. Complete bilateral hearing loss and symmetrical movement are not expected findings of a unilateral ischemic stroke.
</details>
### Question 531
A nurse is preparing the room for a newly admitted client with a history of tonic-clonic seizures. Which procedural step is essential for the nurse to perform when setting up the room?

A) Place a padded tongue depressor on the overbed table.
B) Ensure all four bed rails are raised and locked.
C) Set up functioning suction and oxygen equipment at the bedside.
D) Position a soft restraint kit near the headboard.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Set up functioning suction and oxygen equipment at the bedside.

**Rationale:** As part of standard seizure precautions, a nurse must ensure that functioning oxygen and suction equipment are readily available at the bedside to help maintain a patent airway if a seizure occurs. Placing anything in the mouth (like a tongue depressor) during a seizure is strictly contraindicated as it can cause airway obstruction or dental trauma. Raising all four side rails is considered a physical restraint and is unsafe, and applying restraints during a seizure can cause musculoskeletal injuries. 

**Lens:** Procedural Step
</details>

### Question 532
A nurse is teaching a community health class about early recognition of a stroke using the FAST acronym. The nurse should explain to the class that the "S" in FAST stands for which of the following?

A) Sudden syncope
B) Speech difficulty
C) Sensation loss
D) Severe headache

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Speech difficulty

**Rationale:** The FAST acronym is a widely used tool for stroke recognition. It stands for **F**ace drooping, **A**rm weakness, **S**peech difficulty, and **T**ime to call emergency services. While sensation loss and severe headache can be associated with neurological events, they are not part of the FAST acronym. 

**Lens:** Client Teaching
</details>

### Question 533
The nurse is assessing a client's level of consciousness using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). The client opens their eyes spontaneously, obeys all motor commands, and is fully oriented to person, place, and time. How should the nurse interpret these data?

A) A GCS score of 15
B) A GCS score of 12
C) A GCS score of 9
D) A GCS score of 3

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) A GCS score of 15

**Rationale:** The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) measures three areas: eye-opening, verbal response, and motor response. Spontaneous eye opening scores 4 points, an oriented verbal response scores 5 points, and obeying motor commands scores 6 points. This yields a total maximum score of 15, which indicates a fully alert and oriented client. A score of 3 is the lowest possible score and indicates a deep coma.

**Lens:** Data Interpretation
</details>

### Question 534
A nurse is performing a basic neurological assessment on a client who suffered a right-sided ischemic stroke. Which physical finding is expected for this client?

A) Sudden onset of a high fever
B) Bilateral lower extremity paralysis
C) Right-sided arm and leg weakness
D) Left-sided facial drooping

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Left-sided facial drooping

**Rationale:** Due to the decussation (crossing over) of motor neural pathways in the brainstem, an injury or stroke on the right side of the brain typically causes motor deficits on the contralateral (opposite) side of the body. Therefore, a right-sided stroke is expected to cause left-sided hemiplegia, hemiparesis, or left-sided facial drooping. 

**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
</details>

### Question 535
A nurse is caring for a client who requires seizure precautions. Which task is appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client's neurological status every shift.
B) Teaching the client's family about what to do during a seizure.
C) Padding the client's bed side rails with commercial pads.
D) Administering a scheduled oral antiepileptic medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Padding the client's bed side rails with commercial pads.

**Rationale:** Padding the side rails is a basic safety measure that does not require the nursing process, specialized judgment, or critical thinking, making it safe to delegate to a UAP. Assessing neurological status, providing family education, and administering medications all fall strictly within the scope of practice of a licensed nurse and cannot be delegated to a UAP.

**Lens:** Delegation
</details>
### Question 536
A nurse is preparing a room for a newly admitted client who has a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. As part of seizure precautions, which procedural step should the nurse take when setting up the bed?

A. Placing a padded tongue blade on the bedside table
B. Padding the side rails of the bed
C. Keeping the bed in the highest position
D. Applying loose wrist restraints to the client

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Padding the side rails of the bed.<br><br>
**Lens:** Procedural Step<br>
**Rationale:** Padding the side rails is a standard procedural step for seizure precautions to protect the client from head and limb injuries if a seizure occurs. Placing a tongue blade or any object in a seizing client's mouth is strictly contraindicated due to the risk of airway obstruction or dental trauma. The bed should always be kept in the lowest position, and restraints should never be applied to a seizing client, as this can cause severe musculoskeletal injury.
</details>

### Question 537
A nurse is conducting a community health class about recognizing the early signs of a stroke using the "FAST" acronym. A participant asks what the "S" represents. What is the nurse's best response?

A. Sensory loss
B. Sudden pain
C. Speech difficulty
D. Shallow breathing

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Speech difficulty.<br><br>
**Lens:** Client Teaching<br>
**Rationale:** The "FAST" acronym is a widely taught tool for early stroke recognition. It stands for Face drooping, Arm weakness, Speech difficulty, and Time to call emergency services. Educating the public on these basic signs helps ensure rapid medical intervention, which is critical for stroke survival and recovery. 
</details>

### Question 538
The nurse is documenting the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) for a client who sustained a minor head bump. The client opens their eyes spontaneously, obeys all motor commands, and is fully oriented to person, place, and time. How should the nurse interpret these data to calculate the GCS score?

A. 3
B. 8
C. 12
D. 15

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. 15.<br><br>
**Lens:** Data Interpretation<br>
**Rationale:** The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) measures eye opening, verbal response, and motor response. A client who opens their eyes spontaneously gets 4 points (maximum), is fully oriented gets 5 points (maximum), and obeys motor commands gets 6 points (maximum). Therefore, the total score is 15, which indicates a fully alert and neurologically intact individual. A score of 3 is the lowest possible score and indicates deep coma. 
</details>

### Question 539
The registered nurse (RN) is caring for a client placed on strict seizure precautions. Which task is most appropriate for the RN to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A. Placing the suction canister, tubing, and oxygen equipment at the bedside
B. Assessing the client's neurological status immediately following a seizure
C. Administering an oral antiepileptic medication scheduled for the morning
D. Educating the client's family about how to safely position the client during a seizure

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. Placing the suction canister, tubing, and oxygen equipment at the bedside.<br><br>
**Lens:** Delegation<br>
**Rationale:** Gathering and setting up basic supplies, such as suction and oxygen equipment, is within the scope of practice for the UAP. Assessing neurological status, administering medications, and providing client or family education are all responsibilities that require the nursing judgment of an RN and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

### Question 540
A nurse is assessing a client who has just been diagnosed with an ischemic stroke affecting the right hemisphere of the brain. Based on the location of the stroke, which clinical finding is expected?

A. Right-sided hemiplegia
B. Left-sided body weakness
C. Complete absence of deep tendon reflexes in all extremities
D. Bilateral pupillary constriction

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Left-sided body weakness.<br><br>
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected<br>
**Rationale:** Because the motor pathways cross over (decussate) in the medulla, damage to the right hemisphere of the brain typically results in motor deficits on the opposite (contralateral) side of the body. Therefore, left-sided weakness or paralysis (hemiplegia) is an expected finding for a right-brain stroke. Right-sided hemiplegia would be expected in a left-brain stroke.
</details>
### Question 541
**Lens:** Procedural Step
A nurse is preparing the room for a newly admitted client who has a known history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which of the following items is most important for the nurse to ensure is set up at the bedside?

A) Four-point soft limb restraints
B) Functioning suction equipment and an oxygen setup
C) A sterile dressing change kit
D) A rigid bite block

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Standard seizure precautions require the nurse to have functioning suction equipment and oxygen readily available at the bedside to maintain a patent airway and provide oxygen support if needed after a seizure. Restraints (A) and bite blocks (D) are strictly contraindicated because they can cause severe injury to a seizing client.
</details>

### Question 542
**Lens:** Client Teaching
A nurse is educating a community group on how to quickly recognize the signs of a stroke using the "FAST" acronym. The nurse should explain to the group that the "S" in FAST stands for which of the following?

A) Sudden numbness
B) Sensory loss
C) Speech difficulty
D) Severe headache

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
The "FAST" acronym stands for: **F**ace drooping, **A**rm weakness, **S**peech difficulty (like slurring of words), and **T**ime to call emergency services. This is a common and easy tool taught to the public to quickly identify a potential stroke. 
</details>

### Question 543
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
The nurse is completing a neurological assessment on a client and calculating their Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) score. The client opens their eyes spontaneously, obeys all motor commands, and is fully oriented and conversing appropriately. Which GCS score should the nurse document?

A) 15
B) 12
C) 9
D) 3

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
A score of 15 is the maximum (best) score on the Glasgow Coma Scale, indicating a fully alert and neurologically intact individual. It consists of spontaneous eye opening (4 points), oriented verbal response (5 points), and obeying motor commands (6 points). A score of 3 is the lowest possible score, indicating deep coma.
</details>

---

### Question 544
**Lens:** Contraindication
A client begins having a generalized tonic-clonic seizure while resting in bed. Which action by the nurse is strictly contraindicated during the seizure event?

A) Moving sharp or harmful objects away from the bed
B) Turning the client onto their side to prevent aspiration
C) Loosening restrictive clothing around the client's neck
D) Placing a padded tongue depressor into the client's mouth

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Placing any object, including a padded tongue depressor or bite block, into the mouth of a seizing client is strictly contraindicated. This action can cause broken teeth, severe soft tissue damage, or an airway obstruction. Protecting the client from injury, positioning them on their side, and loosening tight clothing are all safe and appropriate nursing actions.
</details>

---

### Question 545
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
A nurse is assessing a client who was just brought into the emergency department with a suspected acute stroke. Based on the FAST assessment tool, which of the following is an expected physical finding that strongly supports this suspicion?

A) Bilateral leg tremors
B) Sudden chest pain radiating to the jaw
C) Unilateral facial drooping
D) Productive cough with frothy sputum

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Unilateral facial drooping is an expected finding during an acute stroke and correlates directly with the "F" (Face drooping) in the FAST acronym. Chest pain radiating to the jaw suggests a cardiac event (myocardial infarction), while bilateral leg tremors or a productive cough are not primary indicators of a stroke.
</details>
### Question 546
The nurse is admitting a client with a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures to the medical-surgical unit. As part of implementing seizure precautions, which action should the nurse take when setting up the client's room?

A) Place a padded tongue blade on the bedside table.
B) Secure all four of the bed's side rails in the raised position.
C) Ensure functioning oxygen and suction equipment are at the bedside.
D) Maintain the client's bed in the highest position.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Lens:** Procedural Step
**System Focus:** Seizure precautions

**Rationale:** 
Setting up functioning oxygen and suction equipment at the bedside is a critical procedural step when implementing seizure precautions. If a client has a seizure, they may have excess secretions or require supplemental oxygen to maintain a patent airway and adequate oxygenation. Placing a padded tongue blade (Option A) is contraindicated, as inserting anything into the mouth during a seizure can cause severe injury. Raising all four side rails (Option B) is considered a physical restraint and can increase the risk of injury. The bed should always be kept in the lowest, not highest, position (Option D) to minimize the risk of falls and injury if the client tries to get out of bed.

</details>

### Question 547
A nurse is teaching the family of a client with a high risk for ischemic stroke about the "FAST" acronym to help them recognize early warning signs. Which statement by a family member indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "S reminds us to listen for slurred speech or difficulty talking."
B) "T stands for taking their temperature immediately if they feel ill."
C) "F stands for frequent headaches, which is the first sign we should watch for."
D) "A means checking if they have an abnormal or irregular heart rate."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Lens:** Client Teaching
**System Focus:** Basic Stroke signs (FAST)

**Rationale:** 
The FAST acronym is a tool used to quickly identify the signs of a stroke. "S" stands for Speech difficulty, such as slurred speech or inability to speak correctly. "F" stands for Face drooping (not frequent headaches), "A" stands for Arm weakness (not abnormal heart rate), and "T" stands for Time to call 911 (not taking a temperature).

</details>

### Question 548
The nurse is assessing a client's level of consciousness using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). During the assessment, the client opens their eyes spontaneously when the nurse walks into the room, obeys all motor commands accurately, and is fully oriented to person, place, and time. Based on these findings, what GCS score should the nurse document?

A) 12
B) 15
C) 8
D) 3

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Lens:** Data Interpretation
**System Focus:** GCS basics

**Rationale:** 
The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) evaluates three categories: Eye Opening, Verbal Response, and Motor Response. Spontaneous eye opening scores 4 points. Being fully oriented scores 5 points. Obeying motor commands scores 6 points. The total score is 15 (4 + 5 + 6), which is the maximum score, indicating a fully alert and neurologically intact client. A score of 3 is the lowest possible score indicating a deep coma, and a score of 8 or less typically indicates severe brain injury ("less than 8, intubate").

</details>

### Question 549
A client with epilepsy has just experienced a generalized tonic-clonic seizure that lasted for 2 minutes. The seizure activity has now stopped. Which assessment finding does the nurse recognize as expected during this immediate postictal phase?

A) Clear memory of the seizure event
B) Sudden onset of a high fever
C) Hyperactivity and excessive talking
D) Deep sleep and confusion upon waking

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**System Focus:** Seizure precautions

**Rationale:** 
The postictal phase is the recovery period immediately following a seizure. It is expected for the client to experience profound fatigue, deep sleep (somnolence), lethargy, and confusion upon waking. Clients typically have amnesia regarding the seizure event itself (making Option A incorrect). Hyperactivity, excessive talking, and sudden high fevers are unexpected findings and would warrant further investigation.

</details>

### Question 550
The registered nurse (RN) is caring for a client who has been placed on seizure precautions. Which task is most appropriate to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assessing the client's neurological status every 4 hours.
B) Padding the bed's side rails with blankets or commercial pads.
C) Educating the client on what to do if they experience an aura.
D) Administering scheduled oral antiepileptic medications.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Lens:** Delegation
**System Focus:** Seizure precautions

**Rationale:** 
Delegation to UAP must fall within their scope of practice, which includes routine, non-invasive tasks related to environmental safety and activities of daily living. Padding the side rails is a safe and appropriate task to delegate. Assessing neurological status (Option A), providing client education (Option C), and administering medications (Option D) require the nursing judgment, knowledge, and skill of a licensed nurse and cannot be delegated to the UAP.

</details>
### Question 551
A nurse is preparing a room for a newly admitted client with a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which action is an essential procedural step when setting up seizure precautions?

A) Placing a padded tongue blade on the bedside table.
B) Ensuring the client's bed is kept in the lowest possible position.
C) Applying four-point soft restraints to the bed frame.
D) Keeping the room brightly lit at all times.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Procedural Step
**Rationale:** Keeping the bed in the lowest possible position is a fundamental procedural step in seizure precautions. If a seizure occurs while the client is in bed, this minimizes the distance to the floor and reduces the risk of serious injury. Restraints and padded tongue blades are strictly contraindicated during a seizure because they can cause severe physical harm or airway obstruction. Bright lights can sometimes trigger seizures and do not promote a restful environment.
</details>

### Question 552
A nurse is teaching a community health class about the early recognition of stroke using the "FAST" acronym. Which statement by a participant indicates successful teaching?

It stands for **F**acial drooping, **A**rm weakness, **S**peech difficulties, and **T**ime to call emergency services immediately. Option C correctly identifies "A" as checking for arm weakness. The other options are incorrect interpretations of the acronym.

### Question 553
A nurse is assessing a client's level of consciousness using the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS). The nurse notes that the client opens their eyes spontaneously, is fully oriented and conversing, and obeys all motor commands. What is the correct GCS score for this client?

B) 12

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
**Rationale:** The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) evaluates a client's neurological status based on three categories: eye opening (up to 4 points), verbal response (up to 5 points), and motor response (up to 6 points). Spontaneous eye opening (4), being oriented and conversing (5), and obeying commands (6) result in a perfect score of 15. A score of 15 indicates a fully alert and neurologically intact client. ### Question 554
A nurse is assigning morning care for a client who is on strict seizure precautions. Which task is appropriate to delegate to the Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP)?

A) Educating the client on what to expect after taking antiepileptic medications.
B) Assessing the client's pupillary response and hand strength every 4 hours.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Rationale:** Padding the side rails of a bed is a routine, non-invasive environmental safety measure that falls well within the UAP's scope of practice. ### Question 555
The nurse is observing a client who just experienced a 2-minute generalized tonic-clonic seizure. The client is now entering the postictal phase. Which clinical finding is an expected observation during this time?

A) The client speaks rapidly and clearly about the event.
B) The client is lethargic and difficult to arouse.
C) The client is highly energetic and attempts to ambulate.
D) The client has a clear and vivid memory of the seizure.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**Rationale:** Following a generalized tonic-clonic seizure, the client enters the postictal phase, which is a period of recovery for the brain. Expected findings during this phase include profound fatigue, lethargy, confusion, deep sleep, and amnesia regarding the event. Being energetic, having rapid speech, or possessing a clear memory of the seizure are unexpected findings during this phase.
</details>


# SECTION: Renal System (Count: 140 Questions)


### Question 556
A nurse is educating a female client who has a history of recurrent urinary tract infections (UTIs). Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the preventative teaching?

A) "I should wipe from back to front after using the toilet."
B) "I will take warm bubble baths instead of taking showers."
C) "I should empty my bladder completely after sexual intercourse."
D) "I will limit my fluid intake so I don't have to urinate as often."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C (Client Teaching Lens)

**Rationale:** 
- **A is incorrect:** The client should wipe from front to back to prevent the spread of bacteria from the anal area to the urethra.
- **B is incorrect:** Showers are preferred over baths. Bubble baths and harsh soaps can irritate the urethra and increase the risk of UTIs.
- **C is correct:** Emptying the bladder after sexual intercourse flushes out bacteria that may have been introduced into the urethra, effectively preventing UTIs.
- **D is incorrect:** The client should increase, not limit, fluid intake (unless contraindicated) to help flush bacteria from the urinary tract.

</details>

---

### Question 557
The nurse is reviewing the intake and output record of a client with acute kidney injury. The client has an intake of 2,800 mL and an output of 500 mL over the last 24 hours. Based on this data, which assessment finding should the nurse expect to observe?

A) Bounding peripheral pulses and pitting edema
B) Dry mucous membranes and excessive thirst
C) Decreased blood pressure and an increased heart rate
D) Flattened neck veins when lying in a supine position

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A (Data Interpretation Lens)

**Rationale:** 
- **A is correct:** The client's intake significantly exceeds their output, indicating fluid retention. Bounding pulses and pitting edema are classic, expected signs of fluid volume overload.
- **B is incorrect:** Dry mucous membranes and excessive thirst are signs of dehydration (fluid volume deficit), not fluid overload.
- **C is incorrect:** Decreased blood pressure and tachycardia are associated with hypovolemia (fluid deficit), whereas fluid overload typically causes an increase in blood pressure.
- **D is incorrect:** Flattened neck veins indicate dehydration. In fluid overload, the nurse would expect to see jugular vein distention (JVD).

</details>

---

### Question 558
A client with end-stage renal disease is placed on a strict fluid restriction and a low-potassium diet. Which task is most appropriate for the registered nurse (RN) to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Teaching the client which high-potassium foods to avoid on their lunch tray
B) Assessing the client's lung sounds for signs of fluid overload
C) Evaluating the client's daily laboratory results for hyperkalemia
D) Providing the client with a pre-measured cup of ice chips to relieve thirst

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D (Delegation Lens)

**Rationale:** 
- **A is incorrect:** Teaching is outside the scope of practice for a UAP. Education must be done by the RN.
- **B is incorrect:** Assessing the client's physical condition (like lung sounds) requires nursing judgment and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
- **C is incorrect:** Evaluating laboratory results requires nursing judgment and is the responsibility of the RN.
- **D is correct:** Providing pre-measured ice chips is a basic care task related to comfort and intake/output monitoring that safely falls within the UAP's scope of practice.

</details>

---

### Question 559
A nurse is assessing a client receiving continuous intravenous fluids. The client suddenly reports shortness of breath and a new onset of a cough. Upon auscultation, the nurse hears crackles in the lower lung bases. Which condition does the nurse suspect based on these unexpected findings?

A) Urinary tract infection
B) Fluid volume overload
C) Severe dehydration
D) Potassium deficiency

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B (Expected vs Unexpected Lens)

**Rationale:** 
- **A is incorrect:** A urinary tract infection presents with dysuria, frequency, urgency, and possibly fever, not respiratory distress and lung crackles.
- **B is correct:** Shortness of breath, a new cough, and crackles in the lung bases are classic signs of pulmonary edema, a severe consequence of fluid volume overload. This is a common risk when administering IV fluids, especially to clients with compromised renal function.
- **C is incorrect:** Severe dehydration would present with poor skin turgor, dry mucous membranes, and clear lungs, not crackles.
- **D is incorrect:** Potassium deficiency (hypokalemia) presents with muscle weakness and cardiac dysrhythmias, not respiratory crackles.

</details>

---

### Question 560
A nurse is evaluating a client's understanding of a newly prescribed low-sodium, low-potassium renal diet. Which food choice selected by the client indicates that the teaching has been effective?

A) A large baked potato with melted cheese
B) Canned chicken noodle soup with saltines
C) A fresh apple and a side of white rice
D) Half an avocado with sliced tomatoes

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C (Evaluation Lens)

**Rationale:** 
- **A is incorrect:** Potatoes are very high in potassium, and cheese contains significant amounts of sodium and phosphorus, making this a poor choice for a renal diet.
- **B is incorrect:** Canned soups and saltine crackers are highly processed and contain excessive amounts of sodium.
- **C is correct:** Apples are a low-potassium fruit, and white rice is low in both potassium and sodium. This choice demonstrates the client understands the renal diet basics.
- **D is incorrect:** Both avocados and tomatoes are extremely high in potassium and must be avoided or strictly limited on a low-potassium renal diet.

</details>
### Question 561
A nurse is teaching a female client who has a history of frequent urinary tract infections (UTIs) about prevention strategies. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should take bubble baths instead of taking showers."
B) "I will wipe from front to back after using the toilet."
C) "I should wait to empty my bladder until it feels completely full."
D) "I will wear nylon underwear to keep the area dry."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Client Teaching
**System Focus:** UTI Prevention

Wiping from front to back after using the toilet prevents the introduction of bacteria from the gastrointestinal tract (such as *E. coli*) into the urethra, which is a primary cause of UTIs. Taking bubble baths (Option A) can irritate the urethra and increase infection risk; showers are preferred. Waiting until the bladder is completely full (Option C) promotes urinary stasis, giving bacteria time to multiply. Nylon underwear (Option D) traps moisture; clients should be taught to wear breathable cotton underwear to prevent bacterial growth.
</details>

---

### Question 562
A nurse is reviewing the daily weight record for a client with chronic kidney disease. The client has gained 2 kg (4.4 lbs) in the past 24 hours. How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A) The client has experienced an increase in lean muscle mass.
B) The client is consuming too many high-calorie foods.
C) The client is severely dehydrated and needs IV fluids.
D) The client is retaining fluid and may be experiencing fluid overload.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
**System Focus:** Basic Fluid Overload

A rapid weight gain of 2 kg in a 24-hour period strongly indicates fluid retention, as 1 kg of weight gain roughly equals 1 liter of retained fluid. Clients with chronic kidney disease often struggle to excrete excess fluid, leading to fluid overload. Muscle mass (Option A) and fat from calories (Option B) do not accumulate this rapidly. Dehydration (Option C) would result in weight loss, not rapid weight gain.
</details>

---

### Question 563
A nurse is reviewing the meal tray for a client with end-stage renal disease who is prescribed a standard renal diet. Which food item on the tray would be an *unexpected* and incorrect choice for this client?

A) Half a banana
B) A slice of white bread
C) Apple juice
D) Scrambled egg whites

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**System Focus:** Renal Diet Basics

A standard renal diet typically restricts potassium, phosphorus, and sodium. Bananas are very high in potassium, making them an unexpected and inappropriate choice for a client with end-stage renal disease. White bread (Option B) is lower in phosphorus than whole wheat bread and is an expected choice. Apple juice (Option C) is a low-potassium beverage. Scrambled egg whites (Option D) provide high-quality protein with very low phosphorus levels, making them an excellent expected choice for a renal diet.
</details>

---

### Question 564
The nurse is instructing a client on how to collect a clean-catch midstream urine specimen to test for a urinary tract infection. Which procedural step should the nurse instruct the client to perform first?

A) Urinate a small amount into the toilet bowl.
B) Place the sterile specimen cup under the urine stream.
C) Cleanse the urethral meatus with the provided antiseptic wipes.
D) Secure the lid tightly on the specimen container.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Procedural Step
**System Focus:** UTI Prevention / Diagnostic Basics

To collect a clean-catch midstream urine specimen, the client must first cleanse the urethral meatus with antiseptic wipes (Option C) to remove external bacteria. After cleansing, the client should urinate a small amount into the toilet to flush the urethra (Option A), then place the cup in the stream to catch the midstream urine (Option B), and finally secure the lid (Option D) without touching the inside of the container. 
</details>

### Question 565
A nurse is evaluating a client with fluid overload related to acute kidney injury who has been receiving diuretic therapy. Which finding indicates to the nurse that the treatment has been effective?

A) The client complains of increased shortness of breath when lying flat.
B) The client's lung sounds are clear to auscultation.
C) The client's blood pressure increases from 140/90 to 160/95 mm Hg.
D) The client's ankles show new 3+ pitting edema.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Evaluation
**System Focus:** Basic Fluid Overload

Clear lung sounds indicate an absence of pulmonary edema, which shows that the diuretic therapy has effectively reduced fluid overload. Increased shortness of breath when lying flat (orthopnea) (Option A), increased blood pressure (Option C), and worsening pitting edema (Option D) are all signs that fluid overload is continuing or worsening, meaning the treatment has not yet been effective.
</details>
### Question 566
A nurse is providing discharge instructions to a female client who frequently develops urinary tract infections (UTIs). Which statement by the nurse is appropriate to include in the teaching?

A) "You should wipe from back to front after using the toilet."
B) "Take bubble baths instead of taking showers."
C) "Limit your daily fluid intake to prevent overworking your bladder."
D) "Urinate shortly after having sexual intercourse."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Client Teaching
**System Focus:** UTI Prevention

Emptying the bladder after intercourse helps flush bacteria out of the urethra, significantly reducing the risk of developing a UTI. Wiping should always be done from front to back to prevent introducing bowel flora into the urinary tract. Bubble baths can irritate the urethra and should be avoided. Fluid intake should be increased, not decreased, to continually flush out bacteria.
</details>

---

### Question 567
A nurse is reviewing the dietary choices of a client with chronic kidney disease (CKD) who is prescribed a low-potassium diet. Which of the following food choices indicates a risk for dietary noncompliance?

A) Half a cantaloupe
B) Sliced apples
C) White rice
D) Fresh green beans

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Risk Recognition
**System Focus:** Renal Diet Basics

Cantaloupe is a high-potassium fruit. Consuming it poses a risk for hyperkalemia in a client with CKD who requires a low-potassium diet. Apples, white rice, and fresh green beans are universally considered low-potassium foods and are safe choices for this renal diet.
</details>

---

### Question 568
A nurse is reviewing the morning assessment data for a client with an acute kidney injury. Which finding most clearly indicates that the client is experiencing fluid volume overload?

A) Capillary refill time of 2 seconds
B) Blood pressure of 110/70 mm Hg
C) 2+ pitting edema in the bilateral lower extremities
D) Urine specific gravity of 1.015

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
**System Focus:** Basic Fluid Overload

Pitting edema in the lower extremities is a classic, objective sign of fluid volume overload, a very common complication when the kidneys are unable to excrete fluid effectively. A capillary refill of 2 seconds, a blood pressure of 110/70 mm Hg, and a urine specific gravity of 1.015 are all within normal limits and do not suggest fluid overload.
</details>

---

### Question 569
A nurse is directing an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) to provide morning care for a client with an indwelling urinary catheter. Which instruction by the nurse represents appropriate delegation to prevent a urinary tract infection (UTI)?

A) "Irrigate the catheter with sterile saline if the urine looks cloudy."
B) "Clean the perineal area and the catheter tubing with soap and water."
C) "Disconnect the catheter from the drainage bag to drain it faster."
D) "Place the urinary drainage bag on the bed next to the client's leg."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Delegation
**System Focus:** UTI Prevention

Routine catheter and perineal care using mild soap and water is an appropriate task to delegate to a UAP and is essential for preventing catheter-associated UTIs. Irrigating a catheter requires sterile technique and is a nursing task. Disconnecting the drainage bag breaks the sterile closed system and introduces bacteria. The drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the bladder (not on the bed) to prevent the backflow of contaminated urine.
</details>

---

### Question 570
A client with end-stage renal disease and fluid overload receives a prescribed dose of a loop diuretic. Which outcome best demonstrates to the nurse that the medication has been effective?

A) The client reports feeling increased thirst.
B) The client's heart rate increases from 80 to 95 beats per minute.
C) The client's lung sounds change from clear to scattered crackles.
D) The client's daily weight decreases by 1 kg (2.2 lbs).

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Evaluation
**System Focus:** Basic Fluid Overload

A decrease in daily weight is the most accurate and objective indicator of fluid loss, demonstrating that the diuretic therapy is effectively resolving the fluid overload (1 kg of weight loss equals approximately 1 liter of fluid excreted). New crackles indicate worsening fluid overload. Thirst and an increased heart rate are potential side effects of fluid volume shifts, but they do not objectively confirm the successful resolution of fluid excess like daily weights do.
</details>
### Question 571
A nurse is providing discharge instructions to a female client who has had recurrent urinary tract infections (UTIs). Which statement should the nurse include in the teaching to promote UTI prevention?

A) "Wear snug-fitting nylon underwear to absorb moisture."
B) "Limit your daily fluid intake to reduce the need to urinate."
C) "Always wipe from front to back after using the bathroom."
D) "Delay emptying your bladder until it feels completely full."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Client Teaching
**Rationale:** Wiping from front to back prevents the introduction of bacteria from the anal area to the urethra, which is a primary cause of UTIs in females. Clients should be taught to wear breathable cotton underwear (not nylon), increase (not limit) fluid intake to flush bacteria, and void regularly rather than holding urine.
</details>

---

### Question 572
The nurse is evaluating a client with chronic kidney disease who recently received education on following a low-potassium renal diet. Which food choice selected by the client indicates that the teaching was effective?

A) A large baked sweet potato
B) Sliced raw apples
C) Fresh spinach salad
D) A sliced banana

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Evaluation
**Rationale:** Apples are an excellent low-potassium fruit choice for clients on a renal diet. Sweet potatoes, spinach, and bananas are all high in potassium and should be limited or avoided by clients with chronic kidney disease to prevent hyperkalemia.
</details>

### Question 573
The nurse is reviewing the morning vital signs and daily measurements of a client with kidney failure. Which assessment data most clearly indicates that the client is developing fluid overload?

A) A sudden weight gain of 1 kg (2.2 lbs) since yesterday
B) A documented urine output of 1,400 mL over the previous 24 hours
C) A resting blood pressure reading of 118/74 mmHg
D) Oxygen saturation of 98% on room air

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
**Rationale:** Daily weights are the most accurate indicator of fluid volume status. A rapid weight gain of 1 kg (2.2 lbs) in 24 hours equals exactly 1 liter of retained fluid, which is a hallmark sign of fluid overload in clients with compromised renal function. The other data points are within normal, healthy limits.
</details>
