### Question 574
A nurse is working with an Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP) to care for an older adult client on strict bed rest who is at high risk for urinary tract infections (UTIs). Which task is most appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the UAP?

A) Evaluating the effectiveness of the client's current antibiotic therapy.
B) Instructing the client on the importance of drinking adequate fluids.
C) Assessing the client's urine characteristics for cloudiness or foul odor.
D) Assisting the client with routine perineal hygiene after incontinent episodes.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Delegation
**Rationale:** Assisting with hygiene, including perineal care, is a standard, routine procedure that safely falls within the scope of practice for a UAP and is vital for UTI prevention. Evaluating therapy, providing clinical education, and performing nursing assessments are outside the UAP's scope and must be done by the nurse.
</details>

---

### Question 575
A client with a history of acute kidney injury is admitted to the medical-surgical unit. The nurse observes bilateral 2+ pitting edema in the client's lower extremities. How should the nurse interpret this assessment finding?

A) An expected manifestation of a localized skin infection.
B) An unexpected sign indicating severe systemic dehydration.
C) An expected finding associated with renal fluid retention.
D) An unexpected reaction to the client's low-sodium diet.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**Rationale:** When the kidneys fail to adequately excrete water and sodium, fluid accumulates in the body tissues. Dependent pitting edema is an expected finding of fluid overload associated with acute or chronic kidney injury. It is not an indicator of dehydration, a localized infection, or a reaction to a low-sodium diet.
</details>

---
### Question 576
A nurse is educating a female client who has a history of frequent urinary tract infections (UTIs). Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should take bubble baths instead of taking showers."
B) "I will wipe from back to front after using the toilet."
C) "I should empty my bladder as soon as I feel the urge to void."
D) "I will limit my fluid intake to avoid going to the bathroom too often."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** Emptying the bladder promptly prevents urinary stasis, which is a major risk factor for bacterial growth and UTIs. Wiping front to back (not back to front), taking showers instead of baths, and increasing (not limiting) fluid intake are correct preventative measures that the client should adopt. This aligns with the **Client Teaching** lens.
</details>

---

### Question 577
The nurse is reviewing the dietary choices of a client with chronic kidney disease (CKD). Which food choice presents the greatest risk for a complication and indicates a need for further dietary education?

A) Half a cup of sliced bananas
B) One medium-sized peeled apple
C) A small serving of white rice
D) A slice of toasted white bread

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** Bananas are high in potassium. Clients with CKD often require a low-potassium diet because impaired kidneys cannot excrete excess potassium effectively, placing them at risk for life-threatening hyperkalemia. Apples, white rice, and white bread are lower in potassium and phosphorus, making them safer, basic choices for a standard renal diet. This aligns with the **Risk Recognition** lens.
</details>

---

### Question 578
A client with a history of acute kidney injury is being monitored on the medical-surgical unit. Which assessment finding is the most direct indicator of fluid volume overload?

A) Poor skin turgor over the sternum
B) A drop in blood pressure when standing
C) Clear breath sounds auscultated bilaterally
D) 2+ pitting edema in the lower extremities

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** Pitting edema in the lower extremities is a classic, objective sign of fluid volume overload, which commonly occurs when the kidneys fail to properly excrete fluids. Poor skin turgor and postural hypotension are signs of fluid volume deficit (dehydration). Clear breath sounds are a normal, expected finding. This aligns with the **Data Interpretation** lens.
</details>

---

### Question 579
The registered nurse (RN) is directing an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) regarding the care of a client with an indwelling urinary catheter. Which task is appropriate for the RN to delegate to the UAP to help prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI)?

A) Assessing the client's urine for cloudiness and foul odor.
B) Securing the catheter tubing to the client's upper thigh.
C) Recommending a smaller catheter size to the healthcare provider.
D) Irrigating the catheter tubing with a sterile saline solution.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Securing the catheter tubing prevents friction, excessive movement, and trauma to the urethra, which are key steps in preventing a CAUTI. This is a routine, non-invasive task that is safe and appropriate to delegate to a UAP. Assessing urine, recommending changes to the provider, and performing sterile irrigations require nursing judgment and advanced skills, which cannot be delegated to a UAP. This aligns with the **Delegation** lens.
</details>

---

### Question 580
A client admitted with fluid overload secondary to renal impairment has been receiving a daily loop diuretic. Which finding provides the best evidence that the diuretic therapy has been effective?

A) The client's weight decreased by 1 kg (2.2 lbs) since yesterday.
B) The client's resting heart rate increased from 75 to 95 beats per minute.
C) The client reports feeling significantly less thirsty today.
D) The client's urine output was measured at 20 mL over the last hour.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** Daily weights are the most accurate and reliable indicator of fluid volume status. A weight loss of 1 kg correlates to a fluid loss of approximately 1 liter, indicating that the diuretic is effectively pulling off excess fluid. An increased heart rate or feeling less thirsty does not directly evaluate fluid removal. A urine output of 20 mL/hr is actually too low (oliguria) and does not indicate effective diuresis. This aligns with the **Evaluation** lens.
</details>
### Question 581
A nurse is educating a female client about preventing recurrent urinary tract infections (UTIs). Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should wipe from back to front after using the toilet."
B) "I will take bubble baths instead of showers to stay clean."
C) "I should empty my bladder completely after sexual intercourse."
D) "I will limit my daily water intake to avoid using public restrooms."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Voiding after sexual intercourse flushes out bacteria that may have entered the urethra, which helps prevent UTIs. Wiping should always be from front to back to avoid introducing fecal bacteria to the urethra. Bubble baths can irritate the urethra and increase UTI risk. Fluid intake should be increased, not limited, to help flush bacteria from the urinary tract. 
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Client Teaching
</details>

---

### Question 582
A nurse is assessing a client with chronic kidney disease who missed their last scheduled dialysis treatment. Which finding is an expected manifestation of fluid volume overload in this client?

A) Flattened neck veins
B) 2+ pitting edema in the lower extremities
C) Decreased blood pressure
D) Poor skin turgor and dry mucous membranes

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B
<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> When the kidneys fail to excrete adequate amounts of fluid, the client develops fluid volume overload. Expected manifestations include peripheral edema (such as 2+ pitting edema in the legs), hypertension, bounding pulses, and jugular vein distention. Flattened neck veins, decreased blood pressure, and poor skin turgor are signs of fluid volume deficit, not overload.
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Expected vs Unexpected
</details>

---

### Question 583
A nurse is reviewing the dietary log of a client on a strict low-potassium renal diet. Which food item documented by the client indicates a need for further dietary instruction?

A) Half a cup of sliced apples
B) A bowl of plain white rice
C) A cup of steamed green beans
D) A medium baked potato

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D
<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Potatoes (especially baked with the skin on) are very high in potassium and should be strictly limited or avoided by clients on a low-potassium renal diet. Apples, white rice, and green beans are all considered lower-potassium foods and are generally safe choices for renal clients.
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Data Interpretation
</details>

---

### Question 584
The registered nurse (RN) is caring for a client with acute kidney injury who is on a strict fluid restriction. Which task is appropriate for the RN to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Recording the client's daily morning weight using the same scale.
B) Assessing the client's lung sounds for new crackles.
C) Evaluating the client's adherence to the prescribed fluid restriction.
D) Educating the client on the purpose of measuring hourly urine output.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A
<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Recording daily weights is a standard, routine task that falls within the scope of practice for UAPs. Assessing lung sounds, evaluating client adherence to a treatment plan, and providing client education are all nursing responsibilities that require clinical judgment and cannot be delegated to a UAP.
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Delegation
</details>

### Question 585
A nurse is evaluating a client who has been receiving oral antibiotics for a urinary tract infection (UTI) for the past three days. Which finding best indicates that the treatment is effective?

A) The client reports mild lower back ache.
B) The client's urine is clear and pale yellow.
C) The client continues to have a strong urge to void every hour.
D) The client experiences a burning sensation during urination.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale & Lens</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B
<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Clear, pale yellow urine indicates that the infection and associated inflammation are resolving. Cloudy or foul-smelling urine would indicate ongoing infection. Urgency, burning during urination (dysuria), and backache are symptoms of an active UTI and would indicate that the treatment may not yet be effective or that the infection is persisting.
<br><br>
<strong>Lens:</strong> Evaluation
</details>
### Question 586
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a female client who has had recurrent urinary tract infections (UTIs). Which instruction should the nurse include to help prevent future infections?

A) Wear nylon underwear during the day.
B) Wipe from back to front after using the toilet.
C) Empty the bladder soon after sexual intercourse.
D) Take frequent bubble baths instead of showers.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** CLIENT TEACHING

**Rationale:** 
Emptying the bladder after sexual intercourse flushes bacteria out of the urethra, significantly reducing the risk of a UTI. The client should be taught to wipe from front to back (not back to front) to prevent introducing fecal bacteria to the urethra. Cotton underwear is recommended over nylon because it is breathable and prevents moisture buildup, which encourages bacterial growth. Showers are preferred over bubble baths, as bubble baths can irritate the urethra and increase the risk of infection.
</details>

---

### Question 587
A nurse is reviewing the assessment findings for a client with chronic kidney disease (CKD). Which finding most clearly indicates that the client is experiencing fluid overload?

A) A bounding radial pulse and 2+ pitting edema in the ankles.
B) A sudden decrease in blood pressure upon standing.
C) Poor skin turgor over the sternum.
D) Increased urine output over the last 12 hours.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** DATA INTERPRETATION

**Rationale:** 
A bounding pulse and pitting edema are classic, basic signs of fluid volume excess (fluid overload), which is a common complication in clients with failing kidneys who cannot excrete excess fluid. A decrease in blood pressure upon standing (orthostatic hypotension) and poor skin turgor are signs of fluid volume deficit (dehydration). Increased urine output is not typically associated with advanced CKD and fluid overload; rather, clients with CKD often have oliguria (decreased urine output).
</details>

---

### Question 588
A nurse is evaluating a client's understanding of a basic renal diet. The client has chronic kidney disease and has been instructed to limit sodium, potassium, and phosphorus. Which meal choice indicates that the teaching has been successful?

A) A sliced banana with a large glass of orange juice.
B) A lunch of canned chicken noodle soup with saltine crackers.
C) A snack of processed deli turkey and cheese on whole wheat bread.
D) A meal of white rice with grilled chicken and an apple.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** EVALUATION

**Rationale:** 
White rice, grilled chicken, and apples are all relatively low in potassium, sodium, and phosphorus, making them appropriate choices for a basic renal diet. Bananas and orange juice are very high in potassium. Canned soup and saltine crackers are extremely high in sodium. Processed deli meats and cheese are high in both sodium and phosphorus, and whole wheat bread contains more potassium and phosphorus than white bread.
</details>

---

### Question 589
A nurse is caring for a client with renal failure who is on strict monitoring for fluid overload. Which task can the nurse safely delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Assess the client's lung sounds for newly developing crackles.
B) Obtain and record the client's daily morning weight.
C) Teach the client about the daily 1,500 mL fluid restriction.
D) Evaluate the 24-hour intake and output trend to determine fluid balance.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** DELEGATION

**Rationale:** 
Obtaining and recording a client's daily weight is a routine, standardized procedure that falls safely within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing lung sounds requires nursing judgment and physical assessment skills. Teaching the client about fluid restrictions is a primary nursing responsibility. Evaluating clinical trends (like 24-hour I&O balance) to make clinical decisions also requires the licensed nurse's specialized knowledge and cannot be delegated.
</details>

---

### Question 590
A nurse is performing routine indwelling urinary catheter care for a client to help prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI). Which step should the nurse include in this procedure?

A) Keep the urinary drainage bag resting securely on the floor.
B) Cleanse the catheter starting at the meatus and moving downwards.
C) Disconnect the drainage tubing from the catheter to empty the urine.
D) Position the drainage bag at the exact level of the client's bladder.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** PROCEDURAL STEP

**Rationale:** 
Proper catheter care requires cleaning away from the body to prevent introducing bacteria into the urinary tract; therefore, the nurse should clean starting at the urethral meatus and move downwards along the catheter tubing. The drainage bag should never rest on the floor due to the high risk of contamination. The drainage system should remain closed; disconnecting the tubing to empty the bag breaks the sterile seal and introduces bacteria. The drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the bladder to prevent the backflow of urine.
</details>
### Question 591
**Lens: Client Teaching**
A nurse is educating a female client about preventing recurrent urinary tract infections (UTIs). Which statement by the client indicates that the teaching was effective?

A) "I will take bubble baths instead of showers to keep clean."
B) "I will make sure to drink a glass of water and urinate after sexual intercourse."
C) "I should wipe from back to front after using the toilet."
D) "I will wait to urinate until my bladder feels completely full."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Drinking water and voiding after sexual intercourse helps flush bacteria from the urethra, reducing the risk of a UTI. Wiping should always be done from front to back to prevent introducing fecal bacteria into the urinary tract. Showers are preferred over bubble baths, as bubble baths can irritate the urethra. The client should void regularly (every 3 to 4 hours) rather than waiting until the bladder is completely full to prevent urinary stasis.
</details>

---

### Question 592
**Lens: Expected vs Unexpected**
A client with mild kidney impairment is receiving intravenous normal saline. Which assessment finding should the nurse recognize as an *unexpected* sign indicating the development of fluid volume overload?

A) Bounding peripheral pulses
B) Blood pressure of 118/76 mmHg
C) Clear breath sounds on auscultation
D) Urine output of 1,500 mL over 24 hours

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Bounding peripheral pulses are a sign of increased intravascular volume and can indicate fluid volume overload. Other signs include hypertension, crackles in the lungs, and edema. A blood pressure of 118/76 mmHg, clear breath sounds, and a urine output of 1,500 mL in 24 hours are all expected, normal findings that indicate proper fluid balance.
</details>

---

### Question 593
**Lens: Data Interpretation**
A client with early-stage chronic kidney disease is placed on a basic renal diet restricting sodium and potassium. The nurse reviews the client's lunch tray. Which food item indicates a need for further dietary education?

A) White rice
B) Half a cup of fresh blueberries
C) A large baked potato
D) Steamed green beans

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** A renal diet typically restricts potassium, phosphorus, and sodium to reduce the workload on the kidneys. A baked potato is very high in potassium and should be avoided or severely limited. White rice, blueberries, and green beans are all lower in potassium and are generally acceptable choices on a standard renal diet.
</details>

---

### Question 594
**Lens: Delegation**
A nurse is caring for a client who is at risk for fluid overload due to poor kidney function. Which task is most appropriate for the nurse to delegate to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP)?

A) Evaluating the client's lung sounds for the presence of crackles.
B) Teaching the client about the importance of restricting daily fluids.
C) Adjusting the rate of the intravenous fluids to match the care plan.
D) Weighing the client on the same scale at the same time every morning.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Daily weights are a crucial intervention for monitoring fluid overload and are within the UAP's scope of practice. Assessing lung sounds (evaluating), providing dietary teaching, and adjusting intravenous fluid rates require clinical judgment and a nursing license; therefore, these tasks cannot be delegated to a UAP.
</details>

---

### Question 595
**Lens: Therapeutic Communication**
A client on a strict renal diet says to the nurse, "I am so tired of this bland food. I just want to eat a huge slice of pizza with extra cheese and sausage." Which response by the nurse is the most therapeutic?

A) "You know that eating pizza will cause your sodium and phosphorus levels to rise."
B) "It sounds like you are feeling really frustrated with your dietary restrictions."
C) "Why do you want to eat foods that will only make your kidney condition worse?"
D) "I will ask the dietician if they can add a slice of pizza to your lunch tray tomorrow."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Acknowledging and validating the client's feelings of frustration uses the therapeutic communication technique of reflection, which encourages the client to express their emotions openly. Option A is lecturing and non-therapeutic. Option C uses "why," which often makes clients feel defensive. Option D violates the provider's dietary prescription for a renal diet, as pizza is very high in sodium and phosphorus.
</details>

---
### Question 596
A nurse is educating a female client who has a history of frequent urinary tract infections (UTIs) on prevention strategies. Which statement by the nurse is appropriate to include in the teaching?

A) "Wear tight-fitting synthetic underwear to prevent moisture buildup."
B) "Always wipe from back to front after having a bowel movement."
C) "Empty your bladder immediately after sexual intercourse."
D) "Take bubble baths frequently to keep the perineal area thoroughly clean."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Client Teaching
**System Focus:** UTI Prevention

Emptying the bladder after sexual intercourse helps flush any bacteria that may have entered the urethra during intercourse, reducing the risk of a UTI. The other options increase the risk of UTIs: synthetic, tight underwear retains moisture; wiping back to front introduces fecal bacteria to the urethra; and bubble baths can irritate the urethra and alter normal flora.
</details>

---

### Question 597
The nurse is assessing a client with acute kidney injury who is currently experiencing fluid overload due to decreased urine output. Which of the following physical assessment findings should the nurse *expect* to observe?

A) Bounding peripheral pulses
B) Poor skin turgor
C) Flat neck veins when lying flat
D) Decreased blood pressure

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**System Focus:** Basic Fluid Overload

In fluid volume excess (fluid overload), the increased intravascular volume leads to expected findings such as bounding peripheral pulses, elevated blood pressure, and distended (not flat) neck veins. Poor skin turgor and flat neck veins are unexpected findings for fluid overload, as they indicate dehydration (hypovolemia). 
</details>

---

### Question 598
A nurse is reviewing a lunch menu selected by a client on a low-potassium, low-phosphorus renal diet. Which food choice indicates to the nurse that the client accurately understands the dietary restrictions?

A) Baked potato with melted cheddar cheese
B) White rice with a side of steamed green beans
C) Spinach salad topped with sliced bananas
D) Whole grain toast with chunky peanut butter

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Evaluation
**System Focus:** Renal Diet Basics

White rice and green beans are low in both potassium and phosphorus, making them excellent choices for a renal diet. Potatoes, bananas, and spinach are high in potassium. Dairy (cheese), whole grains, and peanut butter are high in phosphorus and should be limited or avoided.
</details>

### Question 599
The nurse is instructing a female client on how to collect a midstream "clean-catch" urine specimen to test for a urinary tract infection. Which instruction represents the correct sequence of procedural steps?

A) Urinate directly into the cup, then cleanse the perineum with an antiseptic wipe.
B) Cleanse the perineum, urinate completely into the cup, and pour off the excess into the toilet.
C) Void a small amount into the toilet, cleanse the perineum, and collect the rest of the urine in the cup.
D) Cleanse the perineum front to back, void a small amount into the toilet, and then collect the urine in the cup.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Procedural Step
**System Focus:** UTI Prevention / Diagnostic Basics

To obtain an uncontaminated midstream clean-catch urine specimen, the client must first cleanse the perineum (front to back) with an antiseptic wipe. Next, the client should initiate the urine stream into the toilet to flush any lingering bacteria from the distal urethra. Finally, the client should place the sterile cup into the stream to "catch" the middle portion of the urine.
</details>

---

### Question 600
A client with chronic kidney disease (CKD) has a prescribed daily fluid restriction. The nurse reviews the client's morning assessment data: a weight gain of 1.5 kg since yesterday, blood pressure 158/92 mmHg, and crackles auscultated in the lung bases. How should the nurse interpret these findings?

A) The client is adhering well to the prescribed daily fluid restriction.
B) The client is showing signs of moderate hypovolemia.
C) The client is experiencing fluid volume excess.
D) The client has a systemic infection causing fluid retention.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
**System Focus:** Basic Fluid Overload

A sudden weight gain (1 kg equals approximately 1 liter of retained fluid), elevated blood pressure, and crackles in the lungs are classic assessment data indicating fluid volume excess (fluid overload). The client is retaining fluid, which suggests they are either not adhering to the fluid restriction or their kidney function has worsened, preventing proper fluid excretion.
</details>
### Question 601
A nurse is reinforcing dietary teaching for a client with chronic kidney disease who needs to restrict potassium intake. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) "I will eat a fresh banana with my breakfast every morning."
B) "I can snack on apples instead of oranges."
C) "I will season my food with a salt substitute to avoid sodium."
D) "Baked potatoes are a great side dish for my dinners."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Client Teaching</strong><br>
Apples are a low-potassium fruit, whereas oranges are high in potassium. Clients with chronic kidney disease are at high risk for hyperkalemia and need to follow a low-potassium diet. Bananas (Option A) and baked potatoes (Option D) are very high in potassium and should be avoided or limited. Salt substitutes (Option C) often replace sodium with potassium chloride, which can cause dangerously high potassium levels in a renal client.
</details>

---

### Question 602
The nurse is assisting a female client in collecting a clean-catch midstream urine specimen to rule out a urinary tract infection (UTI). What is the correct procedural step the nurse should instruct the client to take?

A) Collect the urine in the cup immediately as the urine stream begins.
B) Cleanse the perineal area thoroughly by wiping from back to front.
C) Begin voiding into the toilet, pause, and then collect the specimen in the cup.
D) Rest the inner rim of the specimen cup against the perineal skin during collection.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Procedural Step</strong><br>
To obtain a clean-catch midstream specimen, the client should begin voiding into the toilet to flush contaminants from the urethra, pause, and then collect the remaining urine in the sterile cup. Collecting immediately (Option A) captures bacteria from the distal urethra. Cleansing should always be done from front to back (Option B) to prevent introducing fecal bacteria into the urinary tract. The sterile cup should not touch the perineal skin (Option D) to prevent contamination of the specimen. 
</details>

---

### Question 603
A client with acute kidney injury is being monitored on the medical-surgical unit. The nurse is reviewing the client's vital signs and physical assessment. Which finding should the nurse interpret as a classic sign of fluid volume overload?

A) Poor skin turgor over the sternum
B) Flattened neck veins when lying supine
C) Decreased blood pressure upon standing
D) Bounding peripheral pulses

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Data Interpretation</strong><br>
Bounding peripheral pulses are a classic indicator of fluid volume excess (fluid overload), which is a common complication in clients with kidney injury. Poor skin turgor (Option A), flattened neck veins (Option B), and decreased blood pressure (Option C) are all signs of fluid volume deficit (dehydration), not fluid overload. 
</details>

---

### Question 604
A nurse is assigning tasks for the shift. Which task related to a client with an indwelling urinary catheter is most appropriate to delegate to Unlicensed Assistive Personnel (UAP) to assist in preventing a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI)?

A) Emptying the urinary drainage bag at the end of the shift
B) Assessing the urine for cloudiness and foul odor
C) Irrigating the catheter tubing with sterile normal saline
D) Educating the client on the importance of increasing oral fluid intake

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Delegation</strong><br>
Emptying the urinary drainage bag and recording the output is a standard, non-invasive task that falls within the scope of practice for UAP. Keeping the bag emptied prevents the reflux of urine, which helps prevent CAUTI. Assessing urine (Option B), performing invasive/sterile procedures like irrigation (Option C), and providing client education (Option D) are exclusively within the scope of practice for the licensed nurse.
</details>

---

### Question 605
A client admitted with acute renal failure and fluid overload has been placed on a fluid restriction and is receiving prescribed loop diuretics. Which finding indicates to the nurse that the treatment plan is effective?

A) The client's urine output remains at 20 mL/hr.
B) The client reports shortness of breath when lying flat.
C) The client's body weight has decreased by 3 pounds since admission.
D) The client reports a constant increase in thirst.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Lens: Evaluation</strong><br>
Daily weight is the most reliable indicator of fluid volume status. A decrease in weight indicates that the diuretic therapy and fluid restriction are effectively reducing the client's fluid overload (1 kg of weight loss equals roughly 1 liter of fluid loss). A urine output of 20 mL/hr (Option A) is oliguria and indicates worsening or unresolved renal impairment. Shortness of breath when lying flat (Option B) indicates pulmonary edema, a sign of ongoing fluid overload. Increased thirst (Option D) is a side effect of fluid restriction, not a direct clinical indicator that the fluid overload is resolving.
</details>
### Question 606
A nurse is providing discharge teaching to a female client regarding the prevention of recurrent urinary tract infections (UTIs). Which instruction should the nurse include in the teaching?

A. "Wipe the perineal area from back to front after using the toilet."
B. "Take warm bubble baths instead of taking showers."
C. "Empty your bladder completely every 3 to 4 hours while awake."
D. "Wear tight-fitting synthetic underwear to keep the area dry."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Lens:** Client Teaching
**Rationale:** Emptying the bladder regularly (every 3 to 4 hours) prevents urinary stasis, which is a major risk factor for the bacterial growth that causes UTIs. Wiping should always be from front to back to prevent introducing bowel flora into the urinary tract. Showers are preferred over bubble baths, as bath oils and soaps can irritate the urethra. Clients should wear loose-fitting, cotton underwear rather than synthetic materials to allow for ventilation and reduce moisture.
</details>

---

### Question 607
A nurse is reviewing the daily weight log of a client diagnosed with chronic kidney disease. The client has gained 2 kg (4.4 lbs) in the past 24 hours. How should the nurse interpret this finding?

A. The client is experiencing acute fluid retention.
B. The client has consumed excessive dietary calories.
C. The client is maintaining adequate internal hydration.
D. The client is experiencing a shift of fluids leading to dehydration.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Data Interpretation
**Rationale:** In clients with kidney issues, sudden weight gain is the most reliable indicator of fluid retention (fluid overload). One kilogram (2.2 lbs) of acute weight gain is roughly equivalent to 1 liter of retained fluid. A gain of 2 kg in 24 hours is far too rapid to be attributed to dietary fat or muscle gain and clearly indicates excess fluid volume, not hydration maintenance or dehydration.
</details>

---

### Question 608
The nurse is evaluating the meal choices of a client with chronic kidney disease who is prescribed a low-potassium diet. Which food choice selected by the client indicates a correct understanding of the diet?

A. A baked potato with the skin on
B. Half a cup of applesauce
C. A freshly sliced avocado
D. Half of a small cantaloupe

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Lens:** Evaluation
**Rationale:** Apples and applesauce are considered low-potassium foods and are safe choices for a client on a renal diet. Potatoes (especially with the skin), avocados, and cantaloupe are all very high in potassium and must be avoided or severely limited by clients with compromised renal function to prevent hyperkalemia. 
</details>

### Question 609
A nurse is performing routine indwelling urinary catheter care for a client to help prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI). Which procedural step is correct?

A. Rest the urinary drainage bag on the client's bed during a bed bath.
B. Disconnect the catheter from the drainage tubing daily to flush the line.
C. Clean the perineal area strictly using a strong alcohol-based solution.
D. Clean the catheter tubing starting from the meatus and moving outward.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Lens:** Procedural Step
**Rationale:** When cleaning an indwelling urinary catheter, the nurse should clean starting at the urinary meatus and wipe downward (outward) along the catheter tubing to prevent pulling bacteria toward the urethra. The drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the bladder (never on the bed) to prevent backflow of urine. The system should remain closed; disconnecting it increases the risk of introducing bacteria. Gentle soap and water are recommended for perineal care, not harsh alcohol-based solutions, which can cause skin breakdown and irritation.
</details>

---

### Question 610
A nurse is assessing a client with end-stage renal disease who missed their last dialysis appointment. Which finding is an expected manifestation of fluid overload in this client?

A. Crackles in the lung bases
B. Flat neck veins when lying supine
C. Decreased resting blood pressure
D. Poor skin turgor and dry mucous membranes

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Lens:** Expected vs Unexpected
**Rationale:** Missing dialysis causes the body to retain excess fluid (hypervolemia). An expected manifestation of this fluid overload is pulmonary edema, which presents as crackles in the lung bases upon auscultation. Flat neck veins, decreased blood pressure, and poor skin turgor are expected findings of fluid volume deficit (dehydration), not fluid overload. In fluid overload, the nurse would expect distended neck veins (JVD) and hypertension.
</details>
